**ROUND 11 WAS MISSING FROM THAT TABLE AND IS NOW IN IT**, which is worth
saying rather than quietly closing: for four rounds it read "ROUND 11 IS
MISSING FROM THAT TABLE ON PURPOSE AND NOT BY ARITHMETIC", because the tank
case study sat unmerged on its own branch while rounds 12 to 16 landed.

**NUMBERING THAT NEXT ROUND 12 RATHER THAN TAKING THE NEXT FREE INTEGER IS
WHAT MADE THIS RESOLVABLE AT ALL.** Two branches both calling themselves 11 is
the clean-merge arithmetic failure CLAUDE.md documents -- git merges two
same-numbered rows without a conflict -- and it has already cost this project a
profile format version. Leaving the gap meant the row could simply be dropped
into it, four days and five rounds later, with nothing to renumber.

**AND THE NOTE THAT HELD THE PLACE IS DELETED WITH THE THING IT DESCRIBED**,
which is this file's own rule about a caveat outliving its doubt: a reader who
found "round 11 is missing on purpose" next year could not tell a live gap from
a filled one.

**ROUND ELEVEN HAS NO MEAN, AND THAT IS THE POINT.** It is the first round whose
figures the owner measured rather than this repository -- see **The baseline is
the owner's now** below, which also says which rows of the table further down
are stale.

## Round 77: a Hunter's cast shots are sped by ranged attack speed too

The owner: "Hunter's cast time are affected by Haste AND ranged attack speed
increases. This includes: Rapid Fire, Berserking, Haste from gear/enchants,
Haste from Racials, and Deadly Aspects proc 'Quick Shots', and the Quiver attack
speed increase. This affects: Multi-shot, Sniper Shot, and Aimed Shot cast
times."

| | what changed |
| --- | --- |
| the three shots | declare `Ability.castSpedByRangedAttackSpeed`, so their cast time divides by **haste x swing speed x the quiver**. Each is already a product of its own sources, so they multiply. Nothing else in the Hunter book carries it, since a ruling covers what it names |
| the quiver | still shortens the bow's swing timer as before, and now also records its 1.15 as `WeaponProfile.speedMultiplier`, which the cast reads. It is not applied to the swing a second time |
| the global cooldown | still global haste only, round 75's rule. Rapid Fire speeds up the cast, not the GCD after it |

**SO ROUND 75 REMOVED HALF OF THIS AND ROUND 77 PUTS IT BACK DELIBERATELY.**
Before round 75, Rapid Fire and Deadly Aspects shortened the casts because they
shared `hasteRating` with everything else. Round 75 split swing speed out, which
cost LW Ranged 27.9. Now the owner has said those casts do read ranged attack
speed, and the quiver is new on top.

Measured over 300 fights a profile against round 75's figures:

| | before | after | |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| LW Ranged | 1042.0 | 1087.3 | **+45.3 REAL** |
| BM Hunter | 1017.4 | 1015.3 | -2.1, noise |

The other 35 rows are identical to the decimal; both melee Hunters cast none of
the three shots.

## Round 76: an ability's hover card in the APL panel

The owner asked for a tooltip card on an ability when the mouse is over it in
the APL sections. Every place the panel names an ability now shows one: the
list entry's ability, a `sequence` step, a pre-combat action and a Melee Weave
option. Keyboard focus shows it too, as the gear and talent cards do.

**IT IS THE CLIENT'S OWN TOOLTIP**, from the spellbook captures that were
already checked in and that nothing in `src/` had read. It shows rank and
school, cost and range, cast and cooldown, any requirement line, and the
description, all at max rank. `game/abilities/abilityTooltips.ts` matches an
ability to its capture entry **by name**, because the capture is keyed by the
client's spell id. Every class spell every preset can cast matches;
`abilityCard.test.ts` pins that, so a renamed ability fails a test rather than
silently losing its card.

**WHAT HAS NO CAPTURE SAYS SO** rather than borrowing text: racials (the
client's `/racials.js` is not captured), consumables, Start Auto-attack, Melee
Weave, Mana Ruby and Eureka!. Their card shows the simulator's own cast time,
cooldown and global cooldown, under "No client tooltip on file". Capturing
`/racials.js` would fill the racial half.

No simulation code changed; all combat logs are unchanged.

## Round 75: haste shortens the global cooldown, and swing speed is not haste

The owner: "Haste effects reduce the global cooldown triggered by spells. Haste
effects are not Flurry, Rapid Fire, etc that specifically apply to weapon swing
speed ... Haste can reduce the global cooldown from 1.5 sec to a floor of 1.0
seconds."

| | what changed |
| --- | --- |
| the global cooldown | divided by GLOBAL haste, floored at **1.0s** (`HASTED_GCD_FLOOR_MS`). 20% haste makes 1.5s into 1.25s; 50% or more makes it 1.0s. A Rogue's or a Cat's 1.0s does not move |
| a new stat, `attackSpeedRating` | swing speed only, on haste's scale, compounding source by source the way haste does. It speeds up a swing timer and nothing else: no cast time, no GCD. Swing timers and parry haste divide by `swingHasteMultiplierFrom`, global haste times swing speed |
| moved to swing speed | Flurry (Warrior and Shaman), Slice and Dice, Blade Flurry, Rapid Fire, Deadly Aspects, the pet's Frenzy, Seal of the Crusader, and Thunder Clap's slow on the boss |
| still global haste | gear and enchant haste, Berserking, the Skyborne 3%, the Spellstone, Rage of the Farseer ("melee attack speed **and** spell casting speed") and Nature's Grace |
| Nature's Grace | its separate 10% GCD cut (`gcdFraction`) is removed. Its haste now shortens the GCD by itself, and keeping both would have counted the cut twice |

**SO THREE SWING BUFFS STOPPED SHORTENING CASTS**, which they had done since
they were written, as a side effect of sharing `hasteRating`. Rapid Fire was
speeding up Aimed Shot and Sniper Shot; Seal of the Crusader was speeding up
the Retribution Paladin's casts by 40%.

Measured over 300 fights a profile against `main`:

| | before | after | |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Ele Shaman | 750.8 | 771.2 | **+20.3 REAL** |
| Fire Demo | 1084.0 | 1098.0 | **+14.1 REAL** |
| Frostfire | 1038.6 | 1053.2 | +14.6 |
| Fire | 1127.4 | 1141.2 | +13.8 |
| Frost Mage | 970.4 | 981.5 | +11.2 |
| Arcane | 1052.9 | 1062.9 | +10.0 |
| Moonkin | 848.4 | 856.7 | +8.2 |
| Firelock | 942.1 | 949.6 | +7.5 |
| BM Hunter | 1011.0 | 1017.4 | +6.4 |
| Seal Twist Ret | 983.7 | 959.3 | **-24.4 REAL**, Seal of the Crusader no longer hastes its casts |
| LW Ranged | 1069.9 | 1042.0 | **-27.9 REAL**, Rapid Fire no longer hastes its cast shots |

The rest are within noise. **Every Warrior and Rogue profile, the Cat, the Bear,
the Prot Pally and both melee Hunters are bit-identical**: a Rogue or Cat GCD is
already at the floor, and on the others the buff moved to swing speed paces
the swings exactly as before.

The five healers move only slightly. At 2 to 4% haste the GCD only goes from 1.5s
to about 1.45s, and their heals are cast-time bound. Over 200 fights each, every
tank and raid HPS is within its interval of `main`'s.

**WHAT THE RESULTS PAGE'S "HASTE" TILE MEANS HAS NARROWED.** The average haste
on the results page is GLOBAL haste, so a Venom Rogue's Slice and Dice no longer
shows there. Swing speed is not reported anywhere; adding it would change the
stat-sample event and with it every combat log, so it was left for the owner to
ask for.

**TWO FIGURES ARE FOR THE OWNER:**

- **Shadow Word: Death's held cast now usually misses its window.** The Shadow
  Priest's faster GCD brings its third cast back just before the final 20%
  instead of inside it. The test now pins "at most one in the window"; whether
  to retune the hold is a list question.
- **Rage of the Farseer stayed global haste** on its own wording, "melee attack
  speed and spell casting speed". If the owner means it as two effects, its
  melee half would become swing speed.

Ranks from the capture; Flash Heal and Binding Heal are foreverchanges.pro's 774 to 910, which wins a tie with the capture's 773 to 909 |

| Power Word: Shield | puts on **Weakened Soul** for 15s and is refused while it is up. Soul Warding takes its cooldown off |
| Prayer of Mending | heals its carrier the first time they are hit, then **jumps to another raid member at random**, six heals in all, one per caster. Needed a new aura hook, `onCarrierDamaged`, because the raid fight's blows roll on no table and a `taken` reaction never sees them |
| Penance | three heals: one at once, then one each second of a two-second channel |
| Divine Aegis | 15% of each non-periodic crit as a shield that **adds up** to 1,200 and restarts its 12s, the owner's |

Two engine changes rode along, and both leave every existing profile
byte-identical:

- **A refreshed shield is sized by the definition being applied**, not the one
  already on the carrier. Divine Aegis topping itself up exposed it, because
  its amount lives in each cast's definition. Seal of Fury and Templar's Bulwark
  size theirs from the carrier, so they come out the same.
- **A `sequence` remembers its entry's target.** Steps after the first used to
  aim at the default enemy, so an Inner Focus then Penance on the tank would
  have cast the Penance at the boss.

Measured over 200 fights per fight type:

| --- | --- | --- | --- |

| **Disc Priest** | 578.8 ± 69.7 | 1,110.6 ± 53.7 | 2.10 / 20.07 allied |

Holy's tank fight is 69% Flash Heal. Its raid fight is 68% Prayer of Healing
and 18% Prayer of Mending, about 4.8 heals per Mending. Disc's tank fight is

time.

**ALL 35 EXISTING COMBAT LOGS ARE IDENTICAL TO `main`**, the Shadow Priest and

**THESE CHOICES ARE OURS, NOT THE OWNER'S:**

- both are Troll, Piss Beam's reason (Berserking is haste);
- "spam" Flash Heal and Prayer of Healing means "while the target, or its
  party, is below full";
- Prayer of Mending goes on the tank whenever none of yours is out anywhere;
- the gear is the healer template in Cloth with a caster staff, and the
  enchants are Resto Drood's.

**AND FOUR READINGS ARE QUESTIONS FOR THE OWNER:**

- **Prayer of Mending moves on damage only.** The tooltip's "or receive
  non-periodic healing" is not modelled, since the owner's mechanic names
  damage.
- **Divine Aegis takes 15% of the RAW heal**, overhealing included, so a crit on
  a full target still makes a shield.
- **Twin Disciplines and Spiritual Healing do not reach the shield**, which
  absorbs rather than heals. Improved Power Word: Shield does.
- **Inner Focus's crit reaches every Penance heal**, as it does every Mind Flay

## Round 72: Swiftmend's coin flip, and Omen of Clarity on heals

The owner ruled on two of round 71's four questions:

| | round 71 | round 72 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| which effect Swiftmend uses | Rejuvenation when yours is up, else Regrowth | **a 50/50** when both of yours are up; the one that is up, with no roll drawn, when only one is |
| Omen of Clarity on a healer | nothing: the proc read damage dealt, and a heal could not spend the charge | a **healing spell cast** can proc it, and a heal **spends** it |

**THE PROC IS A CAST REACTION**, `omenOfClarityHealReaction`, on every Druid
beside the damage half. It fires once per cast, on its last tick, for an
ability marked `heals`, at the same chance (4%, doubled in Moonkin Form). **The
two halves share one ten-second internal cooldown**, which now lives on the
character rather than in each reaction's closure, so a heal and a damage hit
cannot both proc inside the window.

**SPENDING IT NEEDED ONE ENGINE FIELD.** Clearcasting is spent only by an
ability with an attack table, the owner's definition of "offensive", and a
heal rolls on none. The owner asked only for the proc, but a proc a pure healer
could never spend would sit unused, and the tooltip says "your next damage **or
healing** spell". `CastModifier.healsCount` lets an ability marked `heals` past
`requiresAttackTable`. Innervate costs mana and heals nothing, so it still
cannot spend the charge.

Resto Drood, 200 fights per fight type:

| | HPS | deaths | Clearcasting |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| tank healer | 644.8 -> **653.7** | 1.83 -> 1.66 | 0.59 a fight, 382 mana saved, mostly on Healing Touch |
| raid healer | 1,843.8 -> **1,907.1** | 8.06 -> 6.57 allied | 1.19 a fight, 645 mana saved, mostly on Rejuvenation |

**THE MOONKIN, CAT AND BEAR ARE UNCHANGED**: all 35 combat logs match `main`.
None of them casts a heal, and moving the internal cooldown onto the character
changes nothing else.

Two of round 71's questions remain open: whether Swiftmend should consume the
heal over time, and whether Improved Regrowth's crit reaches the ticks.

## Round 68: the Melee Weave, revised

The owner revised round 65's weave:

| | round 65 | round 68 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| the gate | the bow fired in the last 0.5s and EVERY action is castable | the bow fired in the last 0.5s and ANY ONE option is castable |
| in melee | every action, in order | the FIRST castable option, plus Raptor Strike if ready |
| the default options | Raptor Strike, Mongoose Bite, Strider Kick | Immolation Trap, Strider Kick, Mongoose Bite |
| the ranged restart | a full hasted swing plus 0.5s after the run back | 0.5s after the run back, or when the bow's own timer runs out if later |
| the run back | at the next decision, a global cooldown later | the instant the option and any Raptor Strike are done |

**BOTH SWING TIMERS COOL DOWN WHILE THE OTHER KIND RUNS.** Each kind's next
swing is due a full swing after its OWN last one. `startAutoAttackMode` already
works that way for melee, so the run in needed nothing. Only the ranged restart
changed: it now asks the same rule, with half a second as the floor.

**THE RUN BACK NO LONGER WAITS OUT A GLOBAL COOLDOWN.** A subroutine's next
decision used to go through `nextDecisionTime`, which sleeps a free actor until
its GCD ends. So after Wing Clip the Hunter stood in melee 1.5s before running
back. `Simulation.subroutineDecisionTime` now picks the next step at the end of
a cast in progress, at once after a cast that went out, and otherwise on a
100ms poll. **One residue**: a Raptor Strike waiting on a melee swing is
noticed on the next poll, so the run back can trail its delivery by up to
100ms.

**IT IS NOW A GAIN, WHICH ROUND 65'S WAS NOT.** LW Ranged, 300 paired fights
at 60s, with the owner's three options (this build knows only Immolation Trap):

| | DPS | against the stock list |
| --- | --- | --- |
| stock list | 1,075.2 | |
| weave at the bottom | 1,123.7 | **+48.5 ± 8.4** |
| weave at the top | 1,141.5 | **+66.3 ± 9.6** |

Round 65's version cost about 54 on the same build. Not added to any stock
list; whether it goes in, and where, is the owner's call. Not measured at a
longer fight.

**NO PUBLISHED FIGURE MOVED**: all 34 combat-log hashes match `main`, because
no stock list holds a Melee Weave.

`AplEntry.weave` keeps its shape, and its meaning is now "the options".
Raptor Strike is never an option, since it comes if ready anyway; one in a
saved list is ignored.

## Round 67: Multi-DoT only on an ability with a DoT

The owner's: "if an ability doesn't have a DoT effect then it should not have
the multi-dot GUI element". The APL panel now offers Multi-DoT only on the 27
abilities in `game/abilities/dots.ts`. A count already stored on any other
entry is still shown, so it can be cleared.

**WRITTEN OUT, AND CHECKED BY CASTING.** Whether an ability applies a DoT is
decided inside its `onCast`, so nothing on its declaration can say.
`tests/game/dotAbilities.test.ts` casts every ability in every class's
catalogue (`classAbilityCatalog`, base and talent-granted) on a preset of that
class, and requires the abilities whose own ticks land to be exactly the list.

**"ITS OWN TICKS" IS THE HALF THAT MATTERS.** The probe forces every roll to
land, so the first version read Heroic Strike, Mutilate and Mortal Strike as
DoTs: they had procced Deep Wounds and the poisons. A tick counts only if it
carries the ability's id or name. Bane of Doom deals its one blow a minute
after the cast, so the probe watches for 65 seconds.

The list includes a direct spell with a burn (Fireball, Pyroblast, Frostfire
Bolt), ground effects (Consecration, Flamestrike), the totems and traps that
tick, and Summon Hawk, which is modelled as a debuff that ticks. Twelve
abilities need a fight state the probe cannot build, and none is a DoT. They
are listed by name, so a new one fails until somebody checks it.

No engine or profile code changed, so no figure can move.

## Round 65: Start Auto-attack, and the Hunter's Melee Weave

Two features from the owner, in one round because the second is built on the
first.

**START AUTO-ATTACK** is an APL action for every character, in three kinds:
**Main Hand Melee Weapon, Dual Wield and Ranged**. The panel shows it as one
action, with a second dropdown for the kind, and fills in the build's own kind
by default -- a caster gets Main Hand.

| | |
| --- | --- |
| **nothing swings until one runs** | the owner's choice of "only via the action". Every profile carries one in its pre-combat list at 0s, so the weapons swing at T-0 as before |
| **a kind that is already running is refused** | so a list that asks for it on every decision never restarts the timer and starves the swing it was waiting for |
| **one kind at a time** | starting melee stops ranged, and the reverse |
| **no global cooldown, and not blocked by one** | `Ability.ignoresGcd`, a new flag: the GCD check is skipped, and so is starting one |
| **a mid-fight start** | swings at the later of now and the last swing plus a full timer |

It fires no cast reaction and breaks no stealth: the swing it starts does both,
like any swing. Without the stealth exception a Rogue's T-0 start would end its
opening stealth before the Ambush.

**A CASTER AND A RANGED HUNTER NOW HAVE A MELEE WEAPON TO SWING.**
`weaponsForEquipment` used to give no weapon for a held stat stick, so a caster
had nothing for a start to swing. It gives one now for the `caster` and `ranged`
styles, and swings it only if a start runs. Druid forms still don't, because a
form's paw is built from the held weapon and a weapon profile there would
replace it.

**PROFILE FORMAT 19.** Version 18 to 19 appends the style's start to each
section's pre-combat list when neither list names one. `withStartAutoAttack`
does the same for a hand-built profile when the fight is built, and
`syncDefaultRotation` does it on in-app edits.

**THE MELEE WEAVE** is one APL entry for Hunters that runs a whole sequence.
It is built on a new engine `Subroutine` hook that owns an actor's decisions
until it finishes:

1. **The gate**: auto-attack is on Ranged, a ranged shot landed in the last
   0.5s, and every action the weave would run can be cast (ignoring the GCD).
   The owner tied the conditions to the actions: "a melee weave occurs when
   Mongoose Bite is usable, Strider kick is usable, Raptor Strike is usable".
2. **Run in**: `melee_weave` is a cast that lasts the run. Ranged stops and
   melee starts as it begins, and a melee swing due during the run waits for it
   to end. So the first swing lands no sooner than the run and no later than a
   melee swing interval.
3. **In melee**: the routine casts the actions in order. It waits on a GCD or a
   resource, and skips an action that is unknown or stops being castable. An
   on-next-swing action (Raptor Strike) is queued the moment the run ends, so
   it rides the swing that lands on arrival, and the routine waits until that
   swing delivers it.
4. **Run out**: `return_to_range` lasts the run too. Melee stops as it begins.
   When it ends, ranged restarts with its first shot a full hasted ranged swing
   plus 0.5s later.

The entry's actions are editable in the panel (`AplEntry.weave`). Leaving them
blank gives the owner's three: Raptor Strike, Mongoose Bite, Strider Kick.

**THE RUN IS 1,000ms, OR 926 WITH MINOR SPEED ON THE BOOTS**: 7 yards at 7
yards a second, times 1.08 for the enchant, the owner's figures. Neither run is
hasted.

**THE OWNER'S THREE NEVER WEAVE ON A MARKSMANSHIP BUILD**, and that is the gate
working. Strider Kick is a Survival talent and Mongoose Bite needs Expose
Prey's window, also Survival. LW Ranged weaves with Raptor Strike alone, or
with Raptor Strike and Wing Clip, which the tests use.

**IT COSTS LW RANGED ABOUT 54 DPS, ON ONE 200-FIGHT RUN IN THE APP**: 1,074.0
without the entry, 1,019.7 with Raptor Strike alone at the bottom of the list,
about 4.9 weaves a fight. Not tuned and not a published figure; the owner's
call whether a gate or a placement should change it.

**NO FIGURE MOVED.** All 34 profiles reproduce `main` to the decimal under
`measure_profiles.ts`, including every caster and both ranged Hunters, whose
new weapon is never swung.

Still open:

- **`ignoresGcd` does not wake an actor that is asleep through a GCD.** The
  start is castable during one, but a list only reads it at the next decision.
  Nothing reaches this today: a start is in pre-combat, and the weave is gated
  on a ranged shot.
- **`MELEE_WEAVE_STALL_MS`, 10s, is a safety limit, not ruleset data.** If the
  actions are still not done 10s after arriving (a melee swing that never
  comes), the Hunter goes back to range instead of staying in melee all fight.

## Round 64: APL Single Target and APL Multi Target

The owner split the Priority List into **two sections**, each collapsible on
its own and each with its own **pre-combat list and combat list**:

| | runs in | shows |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **APL: Single Target** | DPS and tank fights at one target, and the **tank healer** fight | no target controller, no Multi-DoT |
| **APL: Multi Target** | DPS and tank fights at **2+ targets**, and the **raid healer** fight, always | the target controller and Multi-DoT |

The tank healer fight runs Single Target because it heals one target; the
owner chose that when asked, since the request placed healer fights under both.

**SINGLE TARGET IS THE LIST THAT ALREADY EXISTED** -- `rotation` and
`precombat` on the profile root, unchanged -- so every reader that existed
goes on reading the list it always did. Multi Target is the new
`multiTarget` field. `profiles/aplSections.ts` is the one place a fight
chooses (`fightSection`) and the one place a section is swapped into the root
(`withSection`), for the panel that edits it and the fight that runs it.

**NO FIGURE MOVED, AND THAT WAS CHECKED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** A saved
profile's Multi Target list is a copy of its one list (format version 18),
and a preset's starts the same way. So all 34 combat-log hashes are identical
to `main`, and both healers' tank and raid figures match to the decimal.

**A PRESET BUILT FROM ANOTHER ONE WOULD HAVE INHERITED ITS MULTI TARGET
LIST.** Fury Prot is built from Prot Warr, so it carried Prot Warr's pre-combat
in its second list. The tests caught it, and a preset's second list now always
starts from its own.

## Round 63: every haste source multiplies

The owner's ruling: "All Haste effects are treated as individual sources and
are multiplied together." Their example, a Windshaper Skyborne Fury Warrior
with "+1% Haste" on head, gloves and legs, in Flurry:

    1.03 x 1.01 x 1.01 x 1.01 x 1.25 = 1.3265     (it read 1.31 before)

**HASTE WAS ADDED FOR THE WHOLE PROJECT.** Every source was a `hasteRating`
and the stat block summed them, so a 25% and a 3% made 28% rather than 28.75%.

**THE STAT BLOCK MULTIPLIES NOW, FOR HASTE ONLY.** Each flat haste modifier is
one source and so is the base, and the product is written back as the
equivalent rating. Every reader that converts the rating into a multiplier
gets the compounded figure unchanged: the swing timers, cast times, parry
haste, the pre-cast lead and the character sheet.

**SO EACH SOURCE HAS TO ARRIVE APART.** `createPlayer` used to fold the items,
enchants, weapon stone and race into the starting stats as one number. It now
takes their haste back out and adds each one as a modifier of its own --
one per item, one per enchant, one each for the stone and the race. An aura
(Flurry, Slice and Dice, Berserking, Seal of the Crusader, Thunder Clap's
slow) was already its own modifier. A slow multiplies too: 25% haste under a
10% slow is 1.25 x 0.9.

Pinned by `tests/engine/hasteCompounds.test.ts`:
- the owner's example, read off the built character, to ten decimal places;
- a swing gap and a cast time, each measured against the compounded figure.
5 of its 7 tests fail with the engine change reverted.

Measured, 300 fights a profile against `main`: **12 rows up, 20 identical,
none down**, mean 878.6 -> 879.9 over 32. Combining positive haste sources by
multiplying can only add haste, so a profile that moved down would be the bug.
The largest moves are LW Melee +11.6, Combat Mut +6.6, Enh Shaman +5.1 and LW
Ranged +5.1, all inside their own intervals. DW Fury reads +0.0, because its
only haste source is Flurry.

## Round 62: raid frames, a diagnostic for the raid healer

The owner's ask: "a snapshot of all 20 players' hit points as the fight
progresses", to troubleshoot the Raid Healer simulation. It is a **Raid frames**
section under Healing done, shown in the raid healer fight only:

- **One frame every 2 or 3 seconds** through the representative fight. Each
  frame is one column per party with the tank first, red for health left and
  grey for health missing -- the owner's own picture.
- **A slider with Prev and Next** steps through the frames, and **"every frame"**
  lays all of them out in order to compare side by side.
- **"(Died)" marks a death since the previous frame.** The healer fights revive
  a member the instant it dies, so a death is never a state a snapshot could
  catch.
- **The healer's own auras are named on each member** -- a Light's Vigil, a
  Riptide -- because who a heal was aimed at is most of what a raid healer list
  decides.

**READ OFF THE EVENT STREAM**, the way the tank health chart is: every damage
and heal carries its target's health afterwards. To lay out the parties, each
actor in a fight's result now carries its `party` and `raidRole`. Both are
absent outside the healer fights, so nothing else changed. The replay is
`ui/charts/raidFrameSnapshots.ts`.

| 5 | DoTs, Bane of Havoc included, need **"Multi-DoT: X targets"** in the APL; X above the number of targets is the number of targets | `AplEntry.multiDot` and the engine's `PriorityEntry.multiDot`: the entry is tried against the first X enemies, boss first, and cast at the first whose CONDITION holds and that the ability can be cast on -- so "Corruption, if the target's Corruption is not up, Multi-DoT 3" walks it across three targets. Offered in the APL panel on every enemy entry once the fight has adds; dropped when cleared or set to 1; validated on load. **Bane of Havoc is limited to one target** -- casting it takes it off every other enemy |
| 6 | Hellfire's self-damage is recorded and automatically restored, like Life Tap's | each tick spends 210 health and the group heals it back a second later as **"Hellfire (healed by the group)"** -- `payHealthAndHealBack`. Life Tap keeps its own copy of the same shape, untouched, so its fights stay byte-identical |
| 7 | Consecration keeps its coefficient past the fourth target and loses base damage | what shipped |
| 8 | parry haste works for adds and tanks alike | what shipped |

The slow and the heal are noted as out of scope on the two abilities |

## Round 56: a cast holds the swing timer at zero until it finishes

The owner asked to verify a Shaman weaving Lightning Bolt between swings, with
the expected order "Auto-attack .. start casting (auto-attack swing timer reset
back to 0) finish casting .. auto-attack swing timer starts again". **The
engine did not do that.** A resetting cast restarted the timer when the cast
STARTED, and a swing that came due inside the cast was held to its end -- so
the next swing landed at `max(cast start + swing, cast end)`.

Probed on the Enhancement Shaman, a two-second bolt 200ms after each swing, a
2.88-second hasted swing:

| | next swing after the bolt lands |
| --- | --- |
| before | **880ms** -- a full swing from the cast's START |
| after | **2,880ms** -- a full swing from its END |

So a cast shorter than the swing timer cost only the swing it interrupted, and
weaving was nearly free. `resetSwingTimers` now takes the cast time and starts
the full swing from an event at the cast's end, hasted by whatever haste the
character has THEN; a cast that ends early -- a channel interrupted, a pre-cast
cancelled -- starts it at the moment it actually ended. `hold` and `continue`
are unchanged.

**TWO PROFILES MOVE AND THIRTY ARE IDENTICAL TO THE DECIMAL**, 300 fights each:

| | before | after | |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Shockadin | 837.3 | 822.7 | **-14.6 REAL** |
| Shockadin 2H | 946.3 | 928.0 | **-18.3 REAL** |

Both cast a one-second Hammer of Wrath, which now costs a whole swing on top of
the cast. Seal Twist Ret's Hammer is instant through Instrument of Law, and the
Enhancement Shaman casts Lightning Bolt only at five Maelstrom stacks, where it
is instant, so neither moves -- **a melee profile is moved by this only if it
casts something with a cast bar**, which in the stock lists is two.

**AND HAMMER OF WRATH IS NOW WOVEN** (round 56b, the owner's call): both
Shockadins cast it only within 300ms of a main-hand swing, since a cast started
just after a swing throws away almost none of the next one. Paired over 500
fights against no Hammer of Wrath at all:

| | 60s ungated | 60s woven | 180s ungated | 180s woven |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Shockadin | +13.3 | **+16.0** | +14.7 | **+17.5** |
| Shockadin 2H | +8.1 | **+14.6** | -8.2 | **-3.6** |

Better at both lengths on both builds. The 2H build is still a loss at 180s,
where it is mana-bound; removing the entry would gain that 3.6 and cost 14.6 at
60s, so it stays.

## Round 55: healer and Mage bug fixes

Seven items from the owner, four healer and three Mage:

| | what changed |
| --- | --- |
| pre-cast mana | a pre-cast PAYS ONLY IF IT LANDS: `CastOptions.payCostOnCompletion` takes the cost when the cast completes, so a cancelled one spends nothing and starts no five second rule. Round 53's refund is gone, and with it the "Holy Light (cancelled)" gains in the mana panel |
| raid tank | 10% of its health every 2 seconds, no ramp, where it was 30% |
| APL values | a health clause now asks WHOSE -- you, the target, the tank, or the mean of your party or the raid -- so every Holy Pal condition is editable where it was a fixed sentence; heal entries get a target dropdown |
| pre-cast toggle | offered only on a heal with a cast bar (`Ability.heals`, and a cast time that is not a channel): Holy Light and Flash of Light, not Holy Shock |
| Clearcasting | Arcane Concentration rolls ONCE per Arcane Missiles, on the first missile that hits, keyed by `castSequence - channelTicksDelivered` |
| cost rule | cost INCREASES first, and a reduction is a share of the increased cost. Four Arcane Blast stacks (+700%) and Clearcasting (-100%) used to sum to seven times the base; now it is free. The cast-charge saving is measured against the cost without the charge, so it reads the eight times it saved |
| Ice Barrier | a castable trainer spell for any Mage on the owner's ruling (it is also a tier-30 talent Frostfire cannot reach): 480 mana, 30s cooldown, **+6% Frost damage for 60s**. Frostfire casts it at T-10 and recasts it when it falls off with at least 60s of fight left, first of its global-cooldown entries. A Frost buff reaches Frostfire Bolt through `countsAsSchools`, taking the larger per aura |

Measured, 300 fights a profile against `main`:

| | before | after | |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Frostfire | 1010.1 | 1014.1 | +4.0 noise |
| Arcane | 1058.1 | 1052.9 | -5.2 noise |
| Fire | 1123.8 | 1127.4 | +3.6 noise |

**ICE BARRIER IS WORTH +30.6 AT 60s, ONCE THE RECAST IS GATED** (round 55b,
the owner's catch: "+4 dps seems low ... for a 6% increase"). The T-10 cast
takes no global cooldown, so a 60 second fight spends ONE, on the recast at
~50s -- and that recast bought ten seconds of buff for a Frostfire Bolt. It now
recasts only while at least 60 seconds remain, the length of a new buff.
Paired over 500 fights against no Ice Barrier at all:

| | 60s | 180s |
| --- | --- | --- |
| recast whenever it falls off (first push) | +5.0 +/- 5.9 | -39.4 +/- 6.4 |
| T-10 only | +30.6 +/- 5.3 | -10.0 +/- 6.0 |
| **recast only with >= 60s left (shipped)** | **+30.6 +/- 5.3** | -37.8 +/- 6.4 |

+30.6 is what the arithmetic predicts: 6% of the ~44% of damage that counts as
Frost on a ~1,018 DPS build. Frostfire Bolt's non-crit hit is 1,392.6 with the
buff against 1,317.6 without, x1.057.

**AND THE RECAST IS GATED ON 90% MANA** (round 55c, the owner's: "only cast
Ice Barrier when mana is above a threshold"). The build is mana-bound past a
minute, so 480 mana is worth more as Frostfire Bolts. Swept paired against no
Ice Barrier, recast gated on:

| | none | 40% | 50-70% | 80% | 90%+ |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 60s | +30.6 | +30.6 | +30.6 | +30.6 | +30.6 |
| 180s | -37.8 | -25.9 | -25.6 | -21.0 | **-10.0** |
| 300s | -32.7 | -16.2 | -16.1 | -13.8 | **-7.7** |

Monotone, and 90% is where the recast stops firing: it ties the T-10 cast alone
at every length, and no threshold that lets a recast through beats it. 60s is
unchanged because the time gate already holds the recast there.

**THE T-10 CAST ITSELF STILL COSTS -10.0 AT 180s AND -7.7 AT 300s**, which no
gate reaches -- a pre-combat line is not conditional. The owner's call.

Healer, 1000 iterations: tank fight **536 HPS, 2.24 tank deaths**, mana spent
10,956 where it was 15,917 with the cancelled pre-casts charged and refunded;
raid fight **570 HPS, 38.95 deaths** where it was 43.19.

Open: whether Holy Shock's instant heal should be offered a pre-cast is
answered no by the owner's wording ("cast-bar-healing-spells only"), and a
cancelled pre-cast still spends a cast-start charge such as Infusion of Light.

the replaced Seal's effects" meant the hit -- and Seal of Command's effect is a

way. A 3.5-second weapon is 40.8% where it was 100%.

| | what changed |
| --- | --- |

| Seal of Righteousness | `base(speed) x speed x hand + 10%/12% SP`, base 23 at 1.3 to 77 at 4.0 |
| Seal of Command | rolls `melee-special`, so it can miss or be dodged on its own |
| all three seals | do not ride on Holy Strike -- nor the Echo, nor Seal of Fury's shield |
| Judgement | `1.5 / 3.5` with every seal, on `melee-special`: 2x crits, no Revelation |
| Shaman | Flametongue and Rockbiter Weapon; one main-hand imbue at a time; Elemental Weapons reaches both |
| raid buffs | Flametongue Totem, exclusive with Windfury Totem, selected by no preset |
| consumables | Frost Oil, a third weapon effect per hand: 10% for 30 Frost + 27% SP |
| new spells | Cone of Cold, Bane of Doom and Soul Fire, each wired to the talents that name it |
| DoT application | Corruption, Bane of Agony, Siphon Life, Shadow Word: Pain and Devouring Plague now ROLL TO HIT when applied, through a new `rollApplication` that folds in school-scoped hit |

**THE HAND-KEPT LISTS ARE WHERE A NEW SPELL GOES MISSING, AND A TEST CAUGHT ONE.**
Cone of Cold had to join `CHILL_ABILITY_IDS`, `FROST_SPELL_IDS`,
`MAGE_DAMAGE_SPELL_IDS` (and through it `MAGE_COSTED_SPELL_IDS`) and Frost
Channeling's list. The Arcane Power cost test derives its list from the book and
failed until it was added -- the other three would have been silent.

**THE ENHANCEMENT LIST'S IMBUE ENTRY HAD TO CHANGE FOR THE OPTION TO WORK AT
ALL.** It read `withoutAura('windfury_weapon')`, so a Flametongue put in the
pre-combat list would have been overwritten by a Windfury at the pull. All
three imbue entries are gated on NO imbue being up; Windfury is the enabled one.

**FIVE PURE DoTs NEVER ROLLED TO HIT, AND THE WHOLE SUITE PASSED WITH IT.** The
owner's rule is that a DoT application rolls; an ability that deals damage gets
the roll from `dealDamage`, and these five deal nothing on the cast, so nothing
asked. Found while declaring Bane of Doom. **`simulation.rollAttack`, which
Insect Swarm and Serpent Sting use, was the near miss**: it reads the table
alone, so Shadow Focus -- the school-scoped hit the Shadow Priest reaches its cap
through -- would never have reached Shadow Word: Pain. `rollApplication` in
`engine/combat/damage.ts` is `rollTable` itself with no damage in it. The new
tests fail 6 of 26 with the fix reverted. SM/DS's Corruption goes 4.0 to 4.2
casts a fight, which is the misses being recast.

**MERGED AS `69d8e1d` ([#294](https://github.com/Donz-Dev/SimForever/pull/294)),
FOUR COMMITS SQUASHED, CI GREEN ON NODE 20 AND 22, 3,808 TESTS.** The DoT fix was
the fourth commit, added after the PR was opened, so the round grew while it was
in review -- which is this file's "a feature arrives in waves" pointed at a
single PR. Nothing in it was re-baselined.

**WHAT THE ROUND ITSELF GOT WRONG, BOTH CAUGHT BEFORE MERGE:**

| | what happened | what caught it |
| --- | --- | --- |
| a `git stash` | run by accident in the round's worktree, taking the uncommitted spell work; popped back intact. The stash list held another session's entry beneath it | reading `git stash list` before popping |
| the first DoT tests | FAILED against the CORRECT code -- every DoT landed 100% with no hit -- because `buildSimulation` rolls the engine's default table, which has no 17% spell miss. It read exactly like the fix not working | asking which table the harness rolls. Given the Forever table they passed, and failed 6 of 26 with the fix reverted |

**AND ONE THING IT FOUND THAT IS NOT ITS OWN:** `measure_profiles.ts` prints the
Rupture Rogue's pre-combat `stealth` line as `<-- NEVER HAPPENED`. It does on
`main` too: stealth is a `PRECOMBAT_STATES` entry with no ability, and the tool
does not read that table the way the APL panel and the opener guard do. A
reporting gap, not a rotation one.

## Round 50: the tier sweep as one command

**THE OWNER RUNS EVERY PROFILE AT ALL FOUR TEMPLATE GEAR LEVELS EACH WEEK**, and
this week asked for TPS and damage taken per second on the tanks where last
week's run had DPS. So it is a tool now, merged as `dc50ebb`
([#315](https://github.com/Donz-Dev/SimForever/pull/315)):

    HTML=sweep.html npx vite-node tools/tier_sweep.ts                  # the page, 300 fights a point
    OUT=sweep.json HTML=sweep.html npx vite-node tools/tier_sweep.ts   # and the raw figures
    IN=sweep.json OUT=sweep.html npx vite-node tools/tier_sweep_chart.ts   # redraw without re-running

Three charts: DPS for the 27 non-tank profiles, and threat per second and damage
taken per second for the four tanks, each across Pre-BiS, Tier 1, Tier 2 and
Tier 3 (B55, E65, E75, E85). One line per profile in its class colour, a dash
pattern to tell a class's profiles apart, labels at the right end pushed apart
so none overlap. **Hover lights ONE line at a time** and shows its four values;
the owner's second ask, because a lit line that stayed lit when the pointer
moved to the next one made the chart harder to read than no highlight at all.
The DPS chart is 1,100px tall with its axis fitted to the data, on the owner's
"compressed as much as possible to allow for line separation".

check that the committed tool is the one that made the chart.

## Round 49: GUI declutter -- notes, the threat table, and forced auras

and `459b5a1` ([#314](https://github.com/Donz-Dev/SimForever/pull/314)).

  the target attacks back, forced by the ENCOUNTER rather than the raid panel,
  which is why the first PR left it in and said so.

over a list the batch carries; the analyzers still compute every row.

## Round 47c: the client-data sweep for hidden threat passives

**ASKED AFTER THE ROGUE'S 0.8 CHALLENGE**: round 47b's lesson was that a value
two simulators agree on is still an unasked question, and the cheapest way to
ask the data is to read every threat row in it. A subagent cloned wowsims/forever
(`spells_auto_gen.go`) and mythicsim-forever-engine (`spells.jsonl`, client build
1.60.1.70425); six of its rows were re-checked by hand against the clones.
**Nothing in the repository changed.**

**NO HIDDEN CLASS, FORM OR STANCE PASSIVE IS MISSING.** The only such rows in the
data are the ones carried. Confirmed by both files: Rogue and Cat x0.71, the
three stances, Righteous Fury +90% Holy, Salvation -30%, both threat enchants,
every threat talent curve, and Sunder 206, Demoralizing Shout 47.8 and
Demoralizing Roar 48.4.

**NOT IN EITHER FILE, SO A MEASUREMENT, AN OVERRIDE OR A GUESS:**

| | where it comes from |
| --- | --- |
| Bear Form x1.5 | wowsims hard-codes it from a client row neither file carries |
| Heroic Strike 152.4, Cleave 91.8, Sunder's 5% of attack power | wowsims' in-game threat capture |
| Revenge x2.25, Holy Shield x1.2, the Bear's four multipliers, the three Classic x2 spells | **no source at all** |
| 5 threat a point of rage, 0.5 of mana | an engine rule in both simulators, not client data |

**INSTRUMENT OF LAW'S ROW SAYS -20% AND ITS CURVE SAYS +10/+20.** We follow the
row, which matches the tooltip and the owner's ruling; wowsims follows the curve.
And Righteous Fury carries a -100% modifier on the talent in the data, which is
the owner's "Fury switches it off" rule written down by the client.

**IN THE DATA AND NOT MODELLED, NONE ON A PRESET**, because every preset wears
the E65 benchmark set: Rockbiter Weapon's flat threat a hit, Plagueheart 6pc
(-25% on spell crits and DoTs), Bonescythe 6pc (-8% on four strikes), Frostfire
8pc (a proc: the next spell makes no threat), Eye of Diminution (-35% for 20s),
Fetish of the Sand Reaver (-70%), Black Amnesty (a proc: -540 threat), Treacherous
Treads (+1%), Omokk's Girth Restrainer and Ebon Hilt of Marduk (-1%), and Clever
Hat (-4%). Two rows in the questions table below.

## Round 46b: the piercing experiment

The owner's ask: the Arcane Mage, Firelock and Moonkin at E65 with +10, +25 and
+50 Spell Piercing. A throwaway probe in a throwaway worktree, 1,500 fights a
variant, paired on the baseline's seeds; **nothing in the repository changed.**

| | baseline | +10 | +25 | +50 | DPS a point |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Arcane Mage** (carries 15) | 1042.8 | **+17.8** ±0.3 | **+43.0** ±0.4 | **+82.0** ±0.6 | 1.78 -> 1.64 |
| **Firelock** | 983.2 | **+15.6** ±3.5 | **+36.4** ±3.6 | **+68.5** ±3.7 | 1.56 -> 1.37 |
| **Moonkin** | 845.0 | **+13.8** ±4.1 | **+33.9** ±4.1 | **+64.3** ±4.2 | 1.38 -> 1.29 |

**THE CEILING IS ARITHMETIC**: the boss resists nothing, so a direct hit gains
`p / (p + 540)` on average -- 1.8%, 4.4% and 8.5%. The Arcane Mage collects 98-99%
of it (direct hits, and a channel's ticks take the full share); the Firelock
82-87% and the Moonkin 87-91%, because a DoT tick sees a TENTH of the piercing
and only Insect Swarm is exempt. Returns fall about 8% from +10 to +50 as the
curve flattens, so piercing never caps.

**THE INTERVALS ARE THE FINDING ABOUT PAIRING**, which is CLAUDE.md's stat-weight
rule seen from a new side: the Firelock and Moonkin draw ZERO resist rolls at
baseline, so their first point of piercing adds a random draw to every hit, the
fights stop matching and the interval is ±4. The Arcane Mage already rolls with
its 15, so adding more changes no draw count and its interval is ±0.5. **A
variant that introduces a random draw the baseline never made unpairs itself.**

| | the ruling | what changed |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Rogue | first "Rogues have a passive 0.8x multiplier", then, the same day, "revert the rogue ruling it should be 0.71x" | **nothing**: it stays **x0.71**, the client row both simulators read -- now confirmed by the owner rather than taken on their agreement |
| Instrument of Law | the Retribution Paladin's reduction: with Righteous Fury OFF it reduces threat; ON, there is no reduction AND the Fury raises Holy threat | **already built that way.** The Retribution list never casts the Fury, so it reads 0.8 x 0.7 at the pull, now pinned by a test |
| Bear | "fine for now" | nothing. Bear and Dire Bear are ONE combat style here, so the x1.5 passive applies to the Bear profile, which is in Dire Bear Form |
| Searing Pain, Mind Blast, Earth Shock | "use classic values for now. There are talents that reduce some of these abilities" | nothing: Demonic Brand already halves Searing Pain's, Silent Resolve cuts Mind Blast's, Natural Grace cuts Earth Shock's |
| healing and mana | not for now, other than 0.5 threat per point of mana gained | nothing: that is what is built |

**CAT FORM IS STILL x0.71**, which the ruling did not name -- a ruling covers what
it says. Its passive is the client's -29%, read the same by both simulators.

**AND THE ROGUE QUESTION EXPOSED A TRIAGE GAP, WHICH IS THE PART WORTH KEEPING.**
Round 47 put to the owner only what the two Forever simulators DISAGREED on, and
built everything they agreed on -- the stances, the forms, the Rogue, Righteous
Fury -- without asking. Two simulators agreeing means they read the same client
file; it says nothing about what the owner knows from playing. The Rogue survived
the challenge, but **a consensus value is still an unasked question**, and the
hidden class and form passives are where one would hide.

## Round 47: Threat, TPS, Salvation, and the threat ruling repealed

**THE OWNER'S FEATURE, AND THE FIRST SCOPE RULING EVER REPEALED.** Threat was a
member of `OutOfScope`; it is a feature now. One point of damage is one point of
threat, every factor multiplies, Salvation is -30% and on exactly when the target
does not attack back, and the two threat enchants are factors of 0.98 and 1.02.

| | what | where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| the rule | `(damage x ability mult + ability flat) x every modifier`, landed hits only, post-mitigation | `engine/combat/threat.ts` |
| derived | `ThreatTracker` reads damage and resource events off the stream and emits `threat` events; no random number | engine |
| class and form | Rogue x0.71, Cat x0.71, Bear and Dire Bear **x1.5** (Forever's hotfix of 1.3) | `game/combat/threat.ts` |
| stances | Battle 0.8, Defensive 1.3, Berserker 0.8; Defiance +5/10/15% in Defensive **with a shield** | auras, talents |
| Righteous Fury | **+90% on Holy**, where the code said 60; Instrument of Law -20% while it is down | auras, talents |
| talents | Arcane Subtlety, Burning Soul, Frost Channeling, Subtlety, Silent Resolve (Holy and Shadow), Natural Grace, Suppression, Spirit Weapons, Iron Creed, Demonic Brand | the `threat` effect kind |
| Warrior | Heroic Strike +152.4, Cleave +91.8, Revenge x2.25, Shield Slam x1, Sunder 206 + 5% AP, Demoralizing Shout +47.8 -- wowsims/forever's MEASURED set | |
| Bear | Maul x1.75, Swipe x2, Lacerate x3.33, Primal Bite x1.5 -- mythicsim's, `PLACEHOLDER_`; Demoralizing Roar +48.4 | |
| borrowed | Searing Pain, Mind Blast, Earth Shock x2, Classic's, `PLACEHOLDER_` | |
| resources | rage 5 a point with modifiers; mana 0.5 a point without; energy nothing | |
| GUI | a TPS tile beside DPS, and a Threat breakdown above Damage done | Results panel |

**MEASURED: ALL 31 PROFILES' DPS REPRODUCE TO THE DECIMAL** against `main`,
which is the containment check -- threat changes no decision. TPS at 200 fights:

| | TPS/DPS | | TPS/DPS |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Bear | **2.53** | Moonkin, Ele, Frostfire, Fire | ~0.70 |

| Fury Prot | **1.75** | Warlocks | 0.56 - 0.59 |

## Round 46: spell resistance, Spell Piercing, and Curse of the Elements at 10% and -75

**THE OWNER ADOPTED THE PARTIAL-RESIST RULE FROM wowsims/forever#76**, and four
rulings came with it: a raid boss resists **nothing in any school**; Curse of
the Elements' -75 is **floored at zero**; `K = 9 x caster level`, **540 at
60**; and "Magic" means **all six schools, Holy included**.

| | what | where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| the roll | 10% steps around `a = abs(R) / (abs(R) + K)`, the issue's two-regime distribution; never 25/50/75 | `engine/combat/spellResistance.ts` |
| K | `9 x level`, injected the way `attackChances` is; absent means resistance is OFF | `game/combat/spellResistance.ts` |
| the target | `Combatant.resistanceFor`: its own plus every aura's `resistanceBySchool`, floored at zero | engine |
| Spell Piercing | a new stat, subtracted AFTER the floor, so past zero it is bonus damage on the same roll | `spellPiercing` |
| ticks | a tenth of R, except Insect Swarm, whose ticks take the full share (25.6% against 25.0%) | `fullResistanceTicks` |
| damage shields | Thorns and Retribution Aura are never partially resisted | `noPartialResist` |

**CURSE OF THE ELEMENTS WAS WRONG ON BOTH HALVES.** It was 8% with no
resistance clause; Forever's own page (spell 1311680) reads "reducing Magic
resistances by 75 and increasing Magic damage taken by 10%". The -75 does
nothing against a boss with none, which is the floor working.

**SPELL PIERCING COMES FROM TWO PLACES AND BOTH WERE UNREAD.** "+N Spell
Penetration" sits on **25 items** and matched no item rule; and **Arcane
Subtlety's** resistance clause was filed under the `threat` scope with one
reason covering both halves, so the census counted it as answered -- the
compound-reason failure CLAUDE.md records. The Arcane Mage takes 2/2, **15
piercing**.

**NO RANDOM NUMBER IS DRAWN AT ZERO RESISTANCE**, so a build dealing no magic
damage runs the identical fight: **2H Arms, DW Fury and the Cat are 0.0 to the
decimal**, which is the containment check.

**MEASURED, 300 FIGHTS, 31 PROFILES: mean 865.0 -> 873.5, 28 up, 3 at 0.0, none
down.** The casters move by the 1.08 -> 1.10 alone -- Moonkin +15.3, Ele
Shaman +13.6, Fire +21.0, Frostfire +22.4, Shadow +16.8, the four Warlocks
+14.1 to +20.4. **The Arcane Mage is +34.0**, the largest, because its 15
piercing adds about 2.7% on every Arcane hit on top. The melee rows move a few
DPS through poisons, magic procs and seals.

Three questions, in the table below.

**THE OWNER'S PROFILE, AS SPECIFIED**: `warlock_sbolt`, "Sbolt Spam", Orc, the
Imp sacrificed for +15% Shadow, and the Firelock's gear, enchants, raid buffs
and consumables. 22/11/18, decoded from their link and legal as written:

Pandemic 3, Malevolence 4, Nightfall 2 / Demonic Embrace 5, Demonic Aegis 2,
Improved Voidwalker 3, Demonic Sacrifice / Improved Shadow Bolt 5, Bane 5,
Ruin 5, Agonizing Flames 3.

**THE LIST IS THEIRS ENTRY FOR ENTRY**, `WARLOCK_SBOLT`, chosen by Ruin with no
31-point capstone. Entry 1 -- "Major Spellblasting Potion + Blood Fury + Amplify
Curse + Bane of Agony (if those cooldowns are available)" -- is ONE SEQUENCE,
which checks every step's cooldown before it starts and lets nothing in between,
so its Bane of Agony is the amplified one. The other three free racials sit
under it so the list serves any race; an Orc's stored list drops them. Opener:
Shadow Bolt at 2.5s, which is its cast with Bane.

**913.0 DPS at 60s** (`USES=1`, 30 fights). Every entry fires: the stack once,
Shadow Bolt 20.6 casts, Corruption 3.9, Bane of Agony 3.0, the Rune once and
Life Tap 2.6. Not optimised -- it is the owner's list as given.

**AND ROUND 40 HAD BROKEN THREE PROFILES' SAVED GEAR, FOUND WHILE ADDING THIS
ONE.** A benchmark item's id is `BENCHMARK_ID_BASE + templateIndex * 400 + ...`,
so a template inserted mid-table renumbers every set after it -- which two
comments in that file already said, beside rows that were appended for exactly
that reason. Fire Demo's row was inserted beside the Firelock's, and Shadow
Priest, Shockadin 2H and Fury Prot all moved 400 ids: a profile saved before
round 40 pointed at items it never wore, and nothing failed. **Both Warlock
rows are at the end now**, which restores those three; Fire Demo's own ids
change once, and its saved files are a day old at most.
**AND THE CAUSE IS GONE, NOT ONLY GUARDED**: every row now declares an
`idBlock` and its ids are built from that, never from its position, so the
table's order is free. The 31 rows took the blocks their positions already
gave them -- all 2,004 generated items are id-for-id identical across the
change -- and `checkIdBlocks` throws at load on a duplicate.
`tests/game/benchmarkIdStability.test.ts` pins each row's block by hand.

**AND B55 COULD NOT HOLD 150 HIT UNDER THE HALF-SLOT CAP**, the first set ever
to overflow. The overflow code put the excess on the first slots in its list --
the wrists and the NECK, the smallest -- though its own header said "the
biggest slots"; it does what it says now, round-robin from the largest. The
shape test that pinned the cap allows an overflow only once every hit slot in
the set is full, and names the sets where it happens: today, **Fire Demo B55
alone**. No item exceeds its budget.

**1120.8 DPS at 60s** (`USES=1`, 30 fights), where it was 1021.0 before the

## Round 43: Life Tap is (430 + Spirit), replacing the ruled 840

**THE OWNER'S INSTRUCTION**, after Wowhead's Forever page for rank 6 (spell
11689) was found to state the Spirit clause's rate: "Converts [(430 + Spirit) *
(1)] Health into [(430 + Spirit) * (1)] Mana for you." Life Tap had treated
that clause as `unmodelled` -- "states no rate" -- and converted a flat 840.

**BUILT AS STATED**: 430 plus the character's effective Spirit at the moment of
the cast, the same figure for the health paid and the base mana, not rounded.
Improved Life Tap's +20% lands on the mana only, and the group heal a second
later returns exactly what was paid. `lifeTapHealth` and `lifeTapMana` are the
two readers; the flat `LIFE_TAP_AMOUNT` is gone, and the ability no longer
carries an `unmodelled` caveat.

**ON THE THREE WARLOCK PRESETS SPIRIT IS 243-244**, so a tap is about 674 where
it was 840 -- 809 mana with Improved Life Tap on the Firelock, where it was
1,008. **Smaller taps mean more of them, and each is a global cooldown**
(`probe_clock.ts`, 40 fights a length, before -> after):

| | 60s | 120s | 180s | 300s | taps at 300s |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SM/DS | 767.1 -> 767.1 | 744.8 -> 745.1 | 731.4 -> 727.5 | 725.9 -> 722.4 | 11.1 -> 13.3 |
| Firelock | 979.0 -> **971.4** | 905.8 -> 881.2 | 891.3 -> 867.5 | 872.3 -> 846.9 | 28.8 -> 34.2 |
| Demonology | 1021.0 -> 1021.0 | 957.7 -> 943.2 | 947.3 -> 934.5 | 934.9 -> 919.6 | 25.8 -> 31.0 |

**THE FIRELOCK IS THE ONLY PRESET THAT MOVES AT 60s**, -7.6 on one probe run,
because it is the only one that taps inside a minute. The 5% Life Tap gates
were priced on the 840 tap and are not re-swept here.

## Round 41: Life Tap is healed back by the group, and what that did to Demonology

**THE OWNER'S RULING**: "Life tap needs to no longer deal damage to the warlock.
For the purposes of this simulation, it can be assumed to be healed off by a
group player ... after damage is taken from a life tap, the same amount is
restored 1 second later." The 840 health is still paid at the cast -- so
`canCast` still reads it and a second tap inside the second pays from health
not yet returned -- and an external heal named "Life Tap (healed by the group)"
gives it back 1,000ms later. `tests/game/lifeTapHeal.test.ts`.

**NOTHING MOVES AT 60 SECONDS AND THE LONG FIGHTS ARE TRANSFORMED.** No Warlock
runs out of health inside a minute, so all three read the same to the decimal
at 60s; past it, Life Tap was the binding limit and is now not one
(`probe_clock.ts`, 40 fights a length, before -> after):

| | 60s | 120s | 180s | 300s | taps at 300s |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SM/DS | 767.1 -> 767.1 | 744.8 -> 744.8 | 729.4 -> 731.4 | 655.3 -> **725.9** | 5.4 -> 11.1 |
| Firelock | 921.2 -> 921.2 | 655.7 -> **846.1** | 509.9 -> **828.9** | 386.2 -> **809.5** | 5.2 -> 33.2 |
| Demonology | 969.8 -> 969.8 | 904.1 -> 915.0 | 714.2 -> **895.4** | 561.5 -> **886.1** | 5.3 -> 21.7 |

**SO THE "MANA-BOUND PAST A MINUTE" FINDINGS WERE ABOUT LIFE TAP'S HEALTH.**
The Firelock's falling curve and two of round 40's held decisions rested on it.

**AND TWO OF ROUND 40'S HELD DECISIONS SHIP NOW**, re-measured paired at 400
iterations on the healed Life Tap:

| | 60s | 180s | 300s |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Aftermath 5/5 over Cataclysm 3/3 | +38.61 | +33.52 | +31.68 |
| + Corruption above Life Tap | +17.35 | +21.33 | +19.55 |
| **both, shipped** | **+55.07 +/- 5.02** | **+51.31 +/- 2.82** | **+50.72 +/- 2.16** |

later, all inside the combined interval). **Soul Fire all fight is still a

the owner's list and a separate round.

## Round 39: Warlock demons -- the Imp and the Succubus fight

**A WARLOCK CAN HAVE A DEMON OUT NOW**, chosen under the talent trees and above
the sacrifice chooser ("Demon summoned": None, Imp, Succubus). It is a new
profile field, `character.summonedDemon`, SEPARATE from `petFamily` -- which
for a Warlock is the SACRIFICED demon -- because with Demonic Pact one build
holds both. Absent means none out, which is what every saved Warlock was, so no
migration; `validateProfile` names the field, which is the trap it has sprung
twice.

| | the Imp | the Succubus |
| --- | --- | --- |
| acts | Firebolt, back to back: 2s cast, 45-50 Fire (the owner) | melee 95-131 at 2.0s, Lash of Pain on its 12s cooldown (the owner) |
| coefficient | 0.571 | Lash 50 base, 0.429 |
| own crit | 4.44% spell (264 Int at the Mage rate) | 6.2% spell, 4.25% melee (85 Agi / 20) |

**WHAT A DEMON INHERITS** is mythicsim-forever-engine's, the repository the owner
pointed at (`src/classes/warlock/prepare/pets.rs`): 100% of the Warlock's spell
hit and spell crit to both its tables, 10% of its spell power as spell power,
17% as attack power. No stamina, armor or "happiness" multiplier -- that is the
Hunter's. `createDemon`, apart from `createPet`, because almost nothing is shared.

**EVERY TALENT THE OWNER NAMED, AND WHAT EACH ONE MEASURES** (`warlockDemons.test.ts`,
each against the same legal allocation without it):

| talent | built as | measured |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Unholy Power 5/5 | `petStat` damage | x1.1 on every demon hit |
| Improved Imp 3/3 | new `petAbilityDamage` | x1.3 on Firebolt, x1 on anything else |
| Improved Sayaad 3/3 | `petAbilityDamage` | x1.3 on Lash of Pain |
| Demonic Brand | a Warlock reaction and a demon reaction | Searing Pain brands with 2/4/6 charges; each demon attack spends one for 65-68 Fire (Imp) or Shadow (Succubus) |
| Soul Link | `conditionalDamage` + new `petDamageFactor`, the 3 hand-filled | x1.03 on Warlock and demon, only with a demon out; MULTIPLIES with Unholy Power (x1.133) |
| Demonic Knowledge 3/3 | `statFromLevel` + new `petSpellPowerFromLevel` | +60 spell power on both, only with a demon out |
| Master Demonologist 5/5 | `schoolDamage` and new `petSchoolDamage`, gated on a new `BuildRequirement.summonedDemon` | +10% Fire on both with the Imp, +10% Shadow with the Succubus |
| Demonic Sacrifice + Pact | `sacrificedDemon` in the encounter | no demon: stands; a demon without Pact: cancelled; Pact and a DIFFERENT demon: stands; Pact and the same one: cancelled |
| Decimation 2/2 | percentage cooldown, two cast modifiers, `abilityDamageInFinalFraction` | Soul Fire 6s cooldown, 2.4s cast with Bane 5/5 (3.6s without), no shard; Shadow Bolt and Searing Pain x1.06 in the last 35% |

**THE TEST'S FIRST DRAFT READ SOUL LINK AS x1.4729**, which is 1.03 x 1.1 x 1.3:
`legalise` pads a deep talent's tiers with filler, and the filler lands in
Improved Imp and Unholy Power. So every ratio is taken against the same legal
allocation WITHOUT the talent rather than against an empty tree -- the CLAUDE.md
rule about a rank-scaling test reading a stripped talent, met from the other
side: padding can ADD a multiplier as easily as stripping can remove one.

**THREE SMALL ENGINE-SIDE ADDITIONS, NONE A NEW MECHANISM.** `abilityCooldown`
takes `unit: 'percent'`; `grantCastModifier` takes a fixed `fraction` (no shards
is the whole cost, and the row carries no 100); and a talent granting TWO cast
modifiers suffixes the second aura's id, because two auras with one id refresh
rather than coexist.

**CONTAINMENT: ALL 29 PRESETS' COMBAT-LOG HASHES ARE IDENTICAL TO `main`.** No
preset summons a demon -- both Warlock presets sacrifice theirs -- so nothing
published moves. `tools/probe_demons.ts` prints a demon's built stats and damage
rows; Firelock with the Imp out reads 861.0 against 906.7, which is the
sacrifice being cancelled with no Pact, and is correct.

**AND THE IMP NOW PAUSES 0.4 SECONDS BETWEEN FIREBOLTS**, one of this round's
five questions answered by the owner as "likely correct". It is mythicsim's
figure from beta logs (281 Firebolts 2.435 seconds apart), built as a 2.4 second
cooldown running from the start of the cast, which is the same thing while a
demon has no haste. Firebolts go **30.9 to 25.8 a minute**, and the probe's
Firelock-with-Imp reads 848.5 against 861.0 -- one probe run, not a baseline.

**AND THE HUNTER PET CAVEAT NO LONGER FOLLOWS A DEMON**: it was attached to any
`kind: 'pet'`, and is now attached only to a pet that runs on focus.

## Round 38: Fury Prot, the tank Execute clause, and its optimisation pass

**A 29TH PROFILE AND A SECOND WARRIOR TANK.** Fury Prot is the owner's 5/31/15
(decoded from their URL, matches it), and copies Prot Warr in everything else
by their word -- so it DERIVES from that preset and its list derives from the
Prot list. Bloodthirst replaces Shield Slam in place, Death Wish sits above
Heroic Strike, and the opener is theirs: Bloodrage and Battle Shout at 10s,
Defensive Stance at 3s, Death Wish at 1.5s, Mighty Rage Potion, a queued Heroic
Strike and Bloodthirst at 0s. No Charge: Charge reaches Defensive only through
Vanguard, and this tree does not take it. `warriorRotation` now takes talents --
a Defensive shield warrior with Bloodthirst runs `WARRIOR_FURY_PROT`.

**THE EXECUTE CLAUSE, ON BOTH SHIELD TANKS.** In the last 20% of the fight the
list casts Battle Stance to reach Execute -- the engine's own stance-reaching
rule, no stance entry needed. **STAYING THERE IS THE HALF THAT NEEDED WORK**:
Shield Block and Revenge require Defensive, so left alone the list would reach
for one the moment Execute was unaffordable and dance back every global
cooldown. Every rage spender other than Execute is switched off inside the
window (`outsideExecute`). Survival, Bloodrage, consumables and racials stay
live; Shield Wall is deliberately not gated, so a dying tank does go back to
Defensive for it. Execute fires 5.9 times a fight on Prot Warr and 7.1 on Fury
Prot at 60s.

**THREE STANCE TESTS SAID "NEVER LEAVES DEFENSIVE" AND NOW SAY "NEVER BEFORE THE
WINDOW"**, read off the event stream by `tests/helpers/stanceCasts.ts`. A count
of `stance_change` rows was the old check and cannot see WHEN a swap happened.

**THE TEMPLATE ROW IS APPENDED, NOT PLACED BESIDE PROT WARR**, and the first
draft placed it there. Benchmark item ids are allocated by template POSITION,
so the insert renumbered every later set's items -- which a saved profile
stores. A test comparing the two presets' equipment is what showed the ids
moving; the file already said this in its own words beside the Shockadin 2H.

### The optimisation pass -- deaths first, then DPS

`probe_build.ts` now prints a paired DEATHS delta beside the DPS one whenever
the baseline dies, because the procedure says a tank is read on deaths and the
tool could not show them. 400 paired iterations at 60s unless stated.

| dimension | what was tried | result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| theme | Warrior, Fury majority, shield, Defensive, target swings back | fixed, nothing moved |
| gear | E65 template, untouched | verified only |
| talents | Imp. Berserker Rage (dead) and Booming Voice into Unbridled Wrath; Defiance (threat) into Anticipation + Imp. Bloodrage or Imp. Thunder Clap | **every variant noise** at 60s and 180s, on DPS and deaths -- the owner's tree stands |
| pre-combat | the owner's opener | nothing to add: Sunder and Battle Shout are raid-supplied, Demoralizing Shout reduces nothing on this target |
| APL | Bloodthirst above Heroic Strike / Shield Block / Revenge; Heroic Strike's floor swept 26 to 90 | **SHIPPED: Heroic Strike at 65.** At 26 it took the pool every time it passed 26 and Bloodthirst (30) was cast 2.2 times a fight, one of them the opener. A plateau: every floor from 45 to 90 is +41 to +50 at 60s, 35 to 70 is +18 to +20 at 180s; 65 is inside both with the flattest death deltas (+0.05, -0.03). **660.6 -> 708.0 DPS, deaths 5.21 -> 5.25 (noise)**. Second pass: every list change noise |
| enchants | the two dead slots Prot Warr's table gives it | **priced, not shipped -- see the questions**. Gloves +2% Threat -> +15 Strength is **+7.33 +/- 0.30** at 60s and +7.22 +/- 0.07 at 180s, deaths 0.00 |
| consumables | strength food, sharpening stone | stone loses (-8.4, -9.5). **Strength food priced, not shipped**: +17.0 +/- 8.4 at 60s, +12.8 +/- 8.8 at 180s, deaths +0.12 and +0.21, both noise |

**TWO PASSES**: the first shipped the Heroic Strike floor, the second shipped
nothing. **WHAT WAS NOT DONE**: no profile re-baselined, on the standing
instruction; Prot Warr's figure moves with the Execute clause and was not
measured.

## Round 37: the owner's list across 25 profiles

**WHAT THE ROUND WAS**: one message from the owner with an item for nearly every
profile, plus four general asks. All of it is in one PR. The general half:

| | what it was | what it took |
| --- | --- | --- |
| unviable racials | an entry for a racial no legal race of the class learns is REMOVED, not switched off | **40 entries across 19 presets** -- Druids 3/3/4, Paladins 4 each, Shamans 2, Mages, Hunters, Warlocks and the Shadow Priest 1 each; Warriors and Rogues none. `racialsForRace` takes the class; `tools/audit_racial_entries.ts` re-counts |
| sequences | "Life Tap 4x only works once" | the engine repeats a sequence correctly (Searing Pain x4 over three windows). Life Tap's own `canCast` refuses -- it needs 840 mana of room AND more than 840 health, and nothing heals a Warlock on a dummy -- so the later taps are refused for the RESOURCE and the sequence abandons |
| Vanish-led sequences | Premeditation, Ambush and Garrote need Stealth, which only Vanish grants, so checking the whole sequence first refused all of them | `Ability.enabledBy: ['vanish']` -- the sequence check forgives a `condition_failed` on a step only when an EARLIER step is named there. Forgiving every condition broke the Cold Blood + Eviscerate test at 0 points |
| channels | Revelation and Inner Focus were spent on the FIRST tick of Wrack, Mind Flay and Arcane Missiles | both spenders wait for `final`, so the whole channel rolls at the charge's crit |
| template gear | B55 / E65 / E75 / E85 in the gear listing | listed first in every slot picker; a hand-built character gets the templates of the template items it WEARS (`benchmarkTemplatesWorn`) -- found by checking the picker in the browser, where a fresh character showed none |
| off-hand weapon effect | "OH weapon effect should be empty" on eleven profiles | ONE rule, `withoutUnusedOffHandEffect`: dropped wherever the style's off hand is not a weapon -- which is exactly the eleven |

Per profile, the owner's items as given: new trees for Venom, Combat Mut,
Combat, Pure Hemo and Arcane; Ele 1 point Totemic Focus -> Shamanistic Focus;
Seal Twist 2 Unyielding Faith -> Reverence; Shockadin 2H 1 point Two-Handed
Weapon Specialization -> Consecrated Ground. Slam ungated; Spearing Strike out
of DW Fury. Frenzy Potion conditions on the three Combat-tree Rogues, Cold Blood
+ Eviscerate as one sequence on five, Vanish + Premeditation + Ambush on two,
Vanish + Premeditation + GARROTE on Pure Hemo. Garrote declared (7% AP a tick,
18s, a bleed, stealth only; Opportunity and Initiative reach it). Murder now
grants nothing -- a target cause. The Cat pools to 74 energy for Berserk with
Shifting Power spent and Rake and Rip up. Imbues out of the Enh list, Flametongue
in its opener, the Ret's weapon and a spell-crit stone on its main hand. Fire
Blast at <=1 Heating Up on both Fire lists. Arcane: Power + PoM + Wrath stacked
on the Spellblasting potion at four stacks. Holy Shock outside One-Handed Weapon
Specialization. Hunter traps 13% of max(melee, ranged) AP; Rapid Fire and
Bestial Wrath off the GCD; every Hunter's cooldowns in one Frenzy Potion stack;
MM's weave gates and Summon Hawk gone. Shadow Word: Death ungated, Inner Focus
paired with Mind Flay. Openers as written, except one ordering below.

**ONE CONSEQUENCE NOBODY ASKED FOR**: with Flametongue on the main hand instead
of Windfury, the ticked Windfury Totem now pays the Enhancement Shaman -- its
own-imbue clause no longer applies. `windfuryImbue.test.ts` pins both halves.

The stealth rule ends Stealth on any completed action but Premeditation, and the owner's line put the potion first | the stealth opener first, the potion second, in the same instant. **A question**: the first Ambush goes without the potion's AP |

ENTRY CAN SILENTLY REMOVE ANOTHER"** -- removing a standalone Premeditation

broken; the uses column is what showed it.

**WHAT WAS NOT DONE**: no profile re-baselined, on the standing instruction. The

## Round 36: racials, Paladins, three raid buffs and the Arcane Mage

| | what changed |
| --- | --- |
| crit damage racials | a FACTOR on the table's base: 1.5 -> 1.53, 2 -> 2.04; a talent bonus scales the bonus half that leaves. `critMultiplierWith` |
| Holy Strike | `(weapon + 93 + 42.9% SP) x 50%` -- the flat Holy damage used to land whole |
| Consecration | 4.125% spell power a tick |
| One-Handed Weapon Specialization | +10% to everything except Consecration, Retribution Aura and Thorns (`exceptAbilityIds`) |
| Devotion Aura | +735 armor, not item armor, on every preset |
| Thorns / Retribution Aura | 18 Nature / 20 Holy + 6% of the SUPPLIER'S spell power to every attacker; the three tanks only; a value box, default 700, stored in `raidBuffValues` |
| Arcane Blast | already behaved as ruled -- nothing clears the stacks but the clock. A dead `castReaction` that implied otherwise is gone |
| Missile Barrage | no longer procs off Arcane Missiles, ahead of the client |
| race swap | a Race dropdown on the confirmed character, legal races of both factions, nothing else touched |
| racial entries | KEPT in every list and switched off by race (the eye), where they used to be dropped -- custom lists included |
| Arcane Blast stacks | a step chart in Results, the combo-point chart's shape |

**THE CHECK THAT MATTERED WAS ONE FIGHT'S EVENTS, NOT THE CODE.** The ask was
that Arcane Missiles stop clearing Arcane Blast; no code clears it, and printing
the aura's events for one Arcane fight showed it applied at 0 and removed at
14,971ms with two Missiles casts in between. What DID exist was a talent still
declaring the deleted spender's `castReaction`, silently dropped by the build --
the kind of dead line that makes a reader believe something is happening.

**AND THE OWNER'S FOURTH WORKED CASE CONTRADICTED THE OTHER THREE, AND WAS
RULED.** Case D was written `(2 x 1.02 - 1) x 2.2 = 2.288`. Taken as a rule it
gives an UNTALENTED attack 2.08 where case C says 2.04, and a talented spell
1.06 -- so no single formula produces all four. Shown that, the owner ruled B's
rule for everything: `1 + (base x racial - 1) x (1 + talent%)`, which makes D
**2.248**. Asked once, with the arithmetic that separates the readings, and
answered in one message; `racials.test.ts` pins 2.248 and asserts 2.288 is not
what the engine gives.

**THE PREVIEW SERVED THE WRONG CHECKOUT FIRST.** `preview_start` reads the
SHARED checkout's `.claude/launch.json`, so it ran `main` without any of this
round -- caught by fetching a source file and looking for `devotion_aura`. A
temporary entry with `npm run dev --prefix <worktree>` fixed it and was reverted
after. Vite then ignored the assigned port and took 5173, so the tab had to be
pointed there by hand.
