  The only real failure in any of the three runs was `talentSpreads`, which was
  a genuine stale-doc catch and is fixed.

  **AND THE FIRST RUN IS THE ONE I CANNOT ACCOUNT FOR, WHICH IS THE LESSON
  RATHER THAN A FOOTNOTE.** It reported 4 failed FILES and 3 failed tests and
  the output was not captured, so two of the four are unidentifiable: a file can
  fail without a test failing. The second and third runs were written to a file
  first, which is the only reason they could be diagnosed. `npx vitest run >
  run.txt 2>&1` costs nothing and a re-run destroys the evidence -- this
  project has recorded that three times and it has now happened a fourth.

## Round TWENTY-NINE: Presence of Mind, where a NAMED LIST had drifted

**THE OWNER FOUND IT IN THE APP:** "Presence of Mind is bugged for at least
Arcane Wrath. It's not actually making it instant cast. Presence of Mind should
make the next non-channeled, non-instant spell an instant cast."

**IT WAS A HAND-WRITTEN LIST OF SEVEN SPELL IDS.** Arcane Wrath arrived at
client build 1.60.1.70425 as a 3.5-second cast, nobody added it, and the aura
**took the charge and reported its uptime** while the Mage cast the full 3.5
seconds. A charge spent on nothing looks exactly like a charge that worked.

**AND THE LIST WAS WRONG IN THE OTHER DIRECTION TOO, WHICH NOBODY HAD
REPORTED.** It named **Arcane Missiles**, a channel -- and zeroing a channel's
cast delivers ONE tick's worth of effect, one missile of five, and reports a
successful cast. The Arcane list spends the charge on Pyroblast through a
`sequence`, so the case was reachable and never reached.

### The owner's sentence, clause by clause

| the clause | what expresses it |
| --- | --- |
| "the next ... spell" | `ALL_ABILITIES`, the catch-all |
| "non-instant" | `requiresCastTime`, which already existed |
| "non-channeled" | `CastModifier.excludesChannel`, new |

**`requiresCastTime` IS THE NEAR MISS ON THE LAST ONE**: a channel HAS a cast
time and satisfies it gladly. Two words in the owner's sentence, two different
questions.

### "Is this a channel" had two answers and needed one

`spreadsEffectAcrossCast` is the one answer now, and **the obvious test is not
enough**:

| the shape | the test | the example |
| --- | --- | --- |
| a series of ticks | `channelTicks > 1` | Arcane Missiles, five missiles |
| a WINDOW that must last the cast | `onCastStart` | **Evocation** |

**EVOCATION IS WHY.** It declares NO `channelTicks` and is still a channel --
its own comment says so: "ticks exist for an ability whose EFFECT repeats, and
this one's effect is a RATE rather than a series of events." Made instant, its
`onCastStart` window opens and closes in the same millisecond: **an eight-minute
cooldown for no mana, logged as a successful cast.**

**`onCastStart` IS A READING OF THE DECLARATION RATHER THAN A PROXY FOR ONE.**
That field's contract is "an effect that has to be in place FOR the cast rather
than after it" -- and an effect that has to be in place for the cast is one a
zero-length cast cannot deliver. So both shapes are covered by what the ability
already says, and a new channel of either kind is covered the day it lands.

**`runPrecombat` HAD THE SAME GAP** -- it refused `channelTicks > 1` -- and now
shares the predicate. No opener pre-casts Evocation, so that half is a latent
case rather than a live one, and it is labelled as one.

### Clearcasting shared the broken list, so it inherited the gap

It read `[...PRESENCE_OF_MIND_SPELLS, 'fire_blast', 'ice_lance', 'blast_wave']`,
so **Arcane Wrath was the one Mage nuke Clearcasting could never make free.**
One drifting list, two talents.

It is "your next damage spell" as the catch-all with `requiresCost` and
`requiresAttackTable` now -- the arrangement the Druid's own Clearcasting
already uses -- which selects every Mage damage spell and leaves out Mage Armor,
the one costed Mage ability that rolls nothing. **It takes no `excludesChannel`,
deliberately**: making a channel FREE is coherent where making it instant
destroys it, so Arcane Missiles stays in its reach.

### 28 of 28 rows at 0.0, and every reason is accounted for

| | why it cannot move a published figure |
| --- | --- |
| Arcane Wrath becoming instant | **it is in NO profile's book** -- the ability is talent-granted and no Mage preset spends the point |
| Arcane Missiles no longer eligible | the `presence_of_mind` charge is spent on **Pyroblast, 20 times in 20 fights** on the Arcane Mage, and **never at all** on Fire or Frostfire |
| Evocation no longer eligible | same: the charge never reached it |
| Clearcasting's rewrite | its reach is **identical** apart from Arcane Wrath -- Ice Lance and Blast Wave both cost mana and roll a table, so both are still covered |

**THE CHARGE LEDGER IS WHAT SETTLED IT**, read off `cast_charge_spent` rather
than argued: 387 charges over 20 Arcane fights, and the `presence_of_mind` ones
go to Pyroblast and nothing else. **An unexplained 0.0 is indistinguishable
from a change that did not apply**, and this is the third round running where
the explanation was the deliverable.

### And it makes an open question answerable

The owner has asked about **Arcane Wrath** twice, and CLAUDE.md records that its
"very solid justification for not taking" expired with the Arcane Blast reading
it rested on. It has been un-pricable since: a 3.5-second cast whose one obvious
pairing -- Presence of Mind -- **did not work on it.** That pairing works now,
so re-pricing Arcane Wrath on the Arcane Mage is newly worth doing and is on the
questions table.

**THE WHOLE SUITE PASSED BEFORE THIS CHANGE**, which is a statement about the
suite. The existing test on this aura checked PYROBLAST, and its own comment
named the hazard it was not covering: "`PRESENCE_OF_MIND_SPELLS` is a NAMED
LIST, so a spell absent from it takes the full cast with every condition reading
true." **The hazard was identified, written down, and checked for exactly one
spell.**

## Round TWENTY-SEVEN: Improved Slam, where the fix is correct and worth 0.0

**THE OWNER REPORTED IT AS "a very similar bug" TO THE HUNTER'S**, and it is:
"the warrior is able to auto-attack during a slam cast without it being
suspended or paused but only if rank 1 or rank 2 improved slam talent is
taken." The same ruling, reached through a TALENT rather than through an
ability -- so without the talent Slam still interrupts the swing and resets the
timer, which is unchanged.

**ALL 28 ROWS MOVED 0.0 TO THE DECIMAL, INCLUDING 2H ARMS**, which is this
project's own tell for a patch that did not apply. It applied. What follows is
why it is worth nothing, because an unexplained 0.0 is indistinguishable from a
broken change.

### The client's tooltip had said it in two verbs

> Slam no longer interrupts **or delays** your melee swing

`hold` is the FIRST VERB ONLY -- it stops the cast throwing the swing away and
then makes a swing that comes due during the cast wait for it. **And
`Ability.swingTimer`'s own documentation quoted the clause as "Slam no longer
interrupts your melee swing time"**, missing the verb that decides between the
two modes, and used that quote to justify the mode it implements. A tooltip with
two verbs in it is a tooltip that distinguishes two mechanisms.

### Why it is worth 0.0, with the bound that makes it 0.0

2H Arms is the only build that takes the talent, at **2/2**, where the cast is
**1.0s against a 3.6s swing**.

| | |
| --- | --- |
| the mid-cast branch in `scheduleSwing` | reached **0 times in 40 fights** |
| Slam casts measured | **158**, over those 40 fights |
| ms since the previous main-hand attack | min 0, median 1,500, **max 2,500** |
| what a swing inside the window would need | a cast starting **after 2,600ms** |

**RAGE IS WHY.** It arrives per SWING -- `rage = R x S`, flat, on a landed
swing -- so the instant a spender becomes affordable is a swing instant and the
list acts on it. A warrior with this much income cannot go 2.6 seconds with
nothing castable, so the Slam cast is always in the first 2.5 seconds of a
3.6-second cycle and a 1.0-second cast cannot reach the end of it.

**IT IS THE RECKONING SHAPE**, which CLAUDE.md already records: a correctly
implemented change that is arithmetically unable to do anything, where the
cause is a CADENCE rather than the code. There it was the encounter's swing
timer; here it is the build's rage income.

### And the margin is 100ms, so RANK 1 does reach it

At **1/2** the cast is 1.25s, the threshold falls to 2,350ms, and the branch
fires **12 times in 40 fights** -- all 12 allowed. So the mechanism is live and
the rank-2 zero is a property of the cast time, **which is the same talent's
other clause**.

**A CHANGE WORTH NOTHING AT ONE RANK OF A TALENT CAN BE WORTH SOMETHING AT
ANOTHER**, and the owner's "rank 1 or rank 2" wording turns out to matter in a
way the ruling did not intend: rank 2's shorter cast is exactly what makes the
ruling moot for it.

**"RANK 1 OR RANK 2" IS THE WHOLE TALENT ONLY BECAUSE IT HAS TWO RANKS**, which
is a fact about the tree rather than about the ruling -- so the effect stays
value-free and `slamSwingTimer.test.ts` pins `ranks: 2`. A third rank would make
the wording mean something narrower, and nobody would be asked.

### What nearly went wrong: an enum member and a `===`

`ability_holds_swing_timer` asked `=== 'hold'`, while its own name and its panel
description say **"does not cost a swing"** -- the same question only while one
swing-keeping mode existed. The Arms list gates Slam on it, so the moment the
talent granted `continue` the entry went FALSE for the one build that takes the
talent, and **an 8.1% damage source would have vanished** with nothing on the
results page to explain it. That is CLAUDE.md's fifth cause of a never-fired
entry, and it arrives as a DPS loss that reads like a tuning problem.

**ADDING A MEMBER TO AN ENUM IS A CHANGE TO EVERY `===` AGAINST IT.** It asks
for anything but `reset` now, so a third mode is covered when it lands.

### The shape of the data changed with it

`abilityHoldsSwing` is `abilitySwingTimer` with a required `mode`: a boolean
kind would have had to be read as granting the mode it is not named after.
`TalentBuild` carries a MAP for the same reason, and `abilitiesForClass` takes
the **stronger** of what the ability declares and what a talent grants --
`reset` < `hold` < `continue`. A maximum rather than an override, because either
direction of override is wrong in one case, and nothing hits either today.

**`hold` NOW HAS NO DECLARATION ANYWHERE.** It is kept as a mode -- it is a
coherent thing for a ruleset to say, `castSwingTimer.test.ts` pins its
behaviour, and a talent granting it would be one line -- and its documentation
says outright that nothing uses it, so the next reader does not take the
Improved Slam reference for a live caller.

## Round THIRTY-ONE: the Shadow Priest, and a change reverted by its own second measurement

**ONE CHANGE SHIPS OUT OF SIX DIMENSIONS, AND THE MOST USEFUL THING IN THE ROUND
IS A REVERT.** The profile goes 14/2/35 with **Inner Focus** in the tree and in
the list -- **+6.13 +/- 0.50** at 60s over 1200 paired iterations and **+2.40
+/- 0.26** at 300s over 400, both measured as the cost of REMOVING it from the
shipped build. Not re-baselined, on the owner's standing instruction.

### The dimension table, including the five that did not move

| dimension | answer |
| --- | --- |
| **gear** | fixed by the brief. E65, verified untouched |
| **enchants** | **every slot already optimal.** Settled by arithmetic off `stat_weights`, no fights needed -- and the two that look wasted have no live alternative at all |
| **talents** | **the tree is forced except one point.** Inner Focus is the only live destination in the whole 51. **SHIPPED** |
| **pre-combat** | the owner's opener passes Gate B and nothing may be added. Pre-casting Inner Focus is **IDENTICAL FIGHTS** at a 0s lead and **-2.07** at 3s |
| **APL** | **the shipped order is optimal**; every reorder loses. The channel interrupt loses at both lengths. One entry added |
| **consumables** | **every row already the best live option.** Four rows have no live option in their category |

### The channel interrupt: a shipped capability with no caller, finally answered

**Mind Flay has declared `interruptibleChannel` AND `protectedChannelTicks: 2`
since round 19e and NO entry in any of the 28 stock lists has ever declared
`interruptsChannel`.** The owner asked for it in exactly those terms -- *"this
may no longer be optimal play, but I still want the functionality so the user
can choose"* -- so a measurement was the answer either way. **They were right:**

| what interrupts Mind Flay | 60s | 300s |
| --- | --- | --- |
| nothing (shipped) | control | control |
| Shadow Word: Death | -18.36 | -12.31 |
| Mind Blast | -21.62 | -3.07 |
| both DoTs | -2.23 | -0.42 |
| everything above it | -23.96 | -9.18 |

**IMPROVED MIND FLAY IS WHY, AND THE TWO RULINGS ARRIVED TOGETHER PULLING
AGAINST EACH OTHER.** The talent compounds 20% a tick, so the three ticks are 1,
1.2 and 1.44 of the base -- **the only tick an interrupt is allowed to take is
the biggest of the three, 39.6% of the channel.** Measured off the event stream,
Mind Blast's interrupt takes ticks-per-channel from **2.929 to 2.307** while
raising the cast count 13.10 to 14.97: more channels, fewer ticks.

**AND THE TWO DoT ROWS ARE IDENTICAL TO THE DECIMAL, WHICH IS NORMALLY THIS
PROJECT'S TELL FOR A PATCH THAT DID NOT APPLY.** It applied -- the flags were
printed per variant -- and the identity is real: Devouring Plague is **cooldown
bound**, its 24-second cooldown and 24-second duration leaving the entry refused
for cooldown rather than for `already_casting`, so its interrupt can never fire
and Shadow Word: Pain's subsumes it. The same reason a refresh window on it
measures nothing.

### The revert: a refresh window that paid at 60s and cost 118 at 300s

**A TWO-SECOND REFRESH WINDOW ON SHADOW WORD: PAIN WAS MEASURED, DOCUMENTED,
SHIPPED -- AND THEN REVERTED BY THE SAME SWEEP RUN AT A DIFFERENT CLOCK.** The
sweep against an explicit on-expiry control:

| window | 60s | 300s |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **on expiry (shipped)** | control | control |
| 0.5s / 1.0s | +2.78 | **-33.33** |
| 1.5s | +2.78 | -46.27 |
| 2.0s | **+5.47** | **-118.00** |
| 2.5s | +5.47 | -128.84 |
| 3.0s | -11.23 | -143.86 |

**THE 60-SECOND PEAK IS THE 300-SECOND BUILD'S WORST LIVE CELL**, and the
owner's plain *"if not active"* wording wins at 300s monotonically -- the
narrower the window the better, with zero best. It was got wrong **twice** in
this round before it was got right: 2.0s first, then 1.0s.

**WHY IT INVERTS: A WINDOW IS PAID ONCE PER CAST AND THE UPTIME IT BUYS IS PAID
ONCE PER FIGHT.** A 60-second fight casts Shadow Word: Pain **three** times and
a 300-second fight **twelve**, so the clipping compounds while the closed gap
does not.

**AND THE MECHANISM TABLE IS A FINDING IN ITS OWN RIGHT.** Measured at 60s, with
the cast count beside the uptime:

| window | SWP casts | ticks | uptime |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| on expiry | 3.00 | 18.96 | 97.42% |
| 2.0s | 3.00 | 19.35 | 99.40% |
| **3.0s** | 3.00 | **17.27** | **100.00%** |

**A DoT AT PERFECT UPTIME CAN BE WORTH LESS THAN ONE AT 97.4%**, because a
refresh restarts the tick clock. The 3.0-second window -- exactly this DoT's
tick period -- reaches 100% uptime and loses 2.08 ticks a fight. **So the uptime
table is the wrong measure for a periodic effect, and a reader optimising that
column would ship the worst cell in the sweep.**

### What caught the revert: a control labelled with something that stopped being true

**`{ apply: (p) => p }` IS "WHATEVER IS SHIPPED", AND ITS LABEL SAID "on
expiry".** True on the first run and false the moment a window shipped -- after
which the 300-second figures were deltas against the **2.0s build** while the
row still claimed to be the owner's wording. Every row read `IDENTICAL FIGHTS`
and the group appeared to say the talent was worth nothing.

**IT BIT TWICE IN ONE ROUND**, the second time on the Inner Focus group. Both
groups now carry an **explicitly written-out** reverting row beside the no-op
control, and the no-op is labelled *"the SHIPPED window/spread"*. The
procedure's rule -- *every group contains its own control* -- is necessary and
not sufficient: **a no-op control is a control over the CURRENT list, never over
the list it was written against.**

### Inner Focus: both halves or neither, and every donor worth the same

| variant | 60s |
| --- | --- |
| **talent only, no list entry** | **IDENTICAL FIGHTS** |
| entry at the bottom of the list | **IDENTICAL FIGHTS** |
| entry above `mind_flay` | +1.65 |
| entry anywhere above `mind_blast` | **+6.13** |

**THE TALENT ALONE IS WORTH EXACTLY NOTHING** -- an ability in the book that no
list casts, the Berserker Rage shape, and measured one at a time both halves
read as worthless. **The bottom is worth nothing either**, because Mind Flay is
an ungated filler above it: that is the documented exception to *"a new entry
goes at the bottom"*, and a free off-GCD cooldown goes near the top.

**FOUR POSITIONS ABOVE `mind_blast` MEASURE IDENTICALLY**, including one gated
on Shadow Word: Death being ready, because the ability is free and off the
global cooldown -- so wherever it sits above the filler it fires in the same
instant and the same spell collects the +25%. Shadow Word: Death's damage share
rose **10.6% -> 11.3%** when it shipped, which is where the crit lands.

**AND ALL THREE LEGAL DONORS MEASURE THE SAME +6.13**, which is the result
rather than a disappointment: one point off `twilight_focus`, all three off it
with two parked in `mental_agility`, or one off `meditation`. **The tie is
broken on which donor is SAFEST** -- `twilight_focus` is a `castPushback`
RULING, permanently out of scope, where `meditation` is a live talent worth zero
only because this build is GCD-bound. **14/2/35** is the minimal edit; the
owner's stated 13/3/35 moves by one point.
`https://talentsforever.com/priest/60/00500500103-2-50052201231312251--6`

### The tree is forced, which is itself the first result

Every live Shadow talent this spread can reach is **already maxed**. What is
left is six points whose effects are rulings or worth nothing:

| points | why they cannot help |
| --- | --- |
| `twilight_focus` 3 | `castPushback` ruling -- **one spent on Inner Focus** |
| `shadow_reach` 2 | `positioning` ruling. Moved to `dark_inspiration` 5/5: **IDENTICAL FIGHTS** |
| `silence` 1 | `crowdControl` ruling |
| `vampiric_embrace` 1 | heals a profile nothing attacks -- **mandatory**, it is Shadowform's `requiresRanks` prerequisite |
| `dark_inspiration` 3 | live, and grants **Spirit**, which `stat_weights` reads at **0.0000** |
| `meditation` 3 | live, and mana regen on a build that leaves **52.5%** of its mana unspent |

### The clock, read by hand because no paired probe can see it

`tools/probe_fight_length.ts` is new, because `probe_resources.ts` is fixed at
60s and `probe_build.ts DURATION=` prints neither the pool nor the cast counts:

| fight | DPS | mana unspent | Inner Focus | Berserking | potion |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 60s | 940.0 | **55.2%** | 1.00 | 1.00 | 1.00 |
| 120s | 913.9 | 38.7% | 1.00 | 1.00 | 1.51 |
| 180s | 901.5 | 28.4% | 1.48 | 1.48 | 2.00 |
| 300s | 896.6 | **16.2%** | 2.00 | 2.00 | 3.00 |

**THIS BUILD TIGHTENS TOWARD MANA AND NEVER BECOMES MANA-BOUND**, and it loses
only **4.6%** of its DPS from 60s to 300s where the Arcane Mage loses 17%. So it
does not change regime, and the enchant and consumable answers -- which are all
"what does this build scale with" -- hold at every length.

### The never-fired entry, and it is not dead

`demonic_rune` fires **0.00 times at every length up to 300s**, because the
build never reaches its 10%-mana gate. **It is the owner's own ruling** --
*"Demonic rune being used at 10% mana remaining should be the default behavior
for pure mana users"* -- and at 16.2% unspent at 300s it is close enough to the
gate that a longer or unluckier fight reaches it. An honest safety net that
costs the entries below it nothing, not a defect.

### Negative results, which are most of the round

- **Revelation on the staff** loses: **-6.98 +/- 4.82**. Its chance is
  `0.11 * (1 - 2 * spellCrit)`, so it SHRINKS as crit rises, and `+65 Spell
  Power` is worth **+35.19 +/- 0.14** -- which is `65 x 0.5340` off the weight
  table **to two decimals**, the best calibration check the stat weights have had.
- **Every reorder loses**: `mind_flay` above `shadow_word_death` **-46.17**,
  `mind_blast` above `devouring_plague` **-11.47**, `devouring_plague` above
  `shadow_word_pain` **-8.48**, `shadow_word_death` above `mind_blast` -0.79
  (noise).
- **Hit is at the cap and the ladder says otherwise.** `stat_weights` offers
  +5.00% spell hit; the `avoid` column reads **0.9-1.1% against a 1% floor**,
  because Shadow Focus 5/5's school-scoped 5% does not go through
  `attackChances`. Appendix B exactly, on a second class.
- **The stat weights make four consumable and five enchant rows arithmetic
  rather than measurement**: crit **8.096** per 1%, haste **6.507** per 1%,
  spell power **0.5340**, intellect **0.1615**, and **everything else 0.0000**.
  Head and legs keep `+1% Haste` (6.51 against 1.29 for `+8 Intellect`); gloves
  keep `+20 Spell Power` (10.68 against 6.51).
- **The cloak and the feet are wasted and cannot be helped.** `-2% Threat` and
  `Minor Speed` do nothing -- and so does **every other option in those two
  slots** for a caster: `+1% Dodge`, `+60 Armor`, `+5 Agility`, `+4 Defense
  Skill`, `+7 Agility`, `+7 Stamina`. A row worth nothing is not a row that can
  be improved.

### And the hold band the owner ruled on has been vindicated by the clock

**NOT REOPENED.** `docs/handoff/priest.md` says do not re-raise it on the
strength of the measurement, and it is not re-raised. What that document says
WOULD legitimately reopen it is **the arithmetic**, and the arithmetic is a
function of the clock -- Early Demise's window is a FRACTION of the fight and
Shadow Word: Death's cooldown is not:

| fight | removing the band |
| --- | --- |
| 60s | **-1.24 +/- 1.92 (noise)** |
| 300s | **-10.18 +/- 1.79 (real)** |

**The band measured -4.9 when the owner ruled "leave the hold band". It now
measures noise at 60s and is worth +10.18 at 300s.** Their ruling was right and
is now right for a reason that did not exist when they made it.

### Three failing tests, and only one was this round's

| test | cause |
| --- | --- |
| `talentSpreads` | **the guard doing its job** -- two docs stated 13/3/35 against a 14/2/35 preset. Both updated |
| `priestAbilities` | a test pinning a MECHANISM off `shadow_priest`'s own allocation. **CLAUDE.md records five of these in one session** and the correction had been written down a revision earlier. It builds a no-talent Priest by hand now, and the build fact is pinned in a separate test |
| `protectedChannelTicks`, `variableCostIsReported`, `auraCatalog` | **pre-existing**, and proven so by running the suite with this round's work stashed. Three per-test limits, no global `testTimeout` |

**AND THE THREE TIMEOUTS HAVE TWO DIFFERENT CAUSES, WHICH IS WORTH NOT
CONFLATING.** Two walk `src` and read every file. The third does not: measured
standalone, `auraCatalog` reports **test body 629ms against transform 16.76s and
collect 20.35s** -- it imports eleven aura modules, and vitest charges that work
to the first test after collection. **Two tests can need the same limit for two
reasons**, and writing one cause onto the other is how a note stops being worth
reading. `protectionRotation` and `raidBuffs` time out intermittently from a
THIRD cause (Monte Carlo under load) and are deliberately left alone.

## Round THIRTY-TWO: the Frostfire Mage, and the two biggest gains were withheld

**RUN THROUGH `docs/profile-optimisation.md`, THE SECOND PROFILE TO GO THROUGH
IT.** `tools/frostfireVariants.ts` carries every candidate, its figure and its
interval; this is the summary.

**NUMBERED 27 WITH 26 ALREADY TAKEN, AND THE GAP-LEAVING RULE IS WHY THAT
MATTERED.** Round 26 merged *during* this round, from another session in the
same checkout. Two branches both calling themselves 26 is the clean-merge
arithmetic failure this file documents -- git merges two same-numbered rows
without a conflict -- so the number was counted off the table rather than
incremented from memory.

### What shipped, in one table

| dimension | what happened | figure |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **pre-combat** | **A SECOND ILLEGAL OPENER.** The pre-pull Scorch reverted | **-25.90 +/- 9.70 REAL** |
| **pre-combat** | nothing legal replaces it; the potion and Eureka at a 0s lead are **BIT-IDENTICAL** | 0.00 |
| **APL** | **Eureka held for a Fingers of Frost window** | **+5.99 +/- 0.95 REAL** |
| **APL** | `mage_armor`'s dead entry removed -- the owner's existing ruling | **BIT-IDENTICAL** |
| **talents** | every candidate loses, or gains at 60s and inverts by 180s | **not shipped** |
| **enchants** | every alternative loses or is identical; two slots have **no live option at all** | **not shipped** |
| **consumables** | the shipped rows win; the mana potion is -9.66 | **not shipped** |
| **gear** | fixed by the brief, Gate C verified | -- |

Net on the published clock: **1051.7 -> 1057.7**, and the round's two largest
measured gains are deliberately not in that figure.

### The opener was illegal, for the second profile in a row

**THE RULING: "Once you deal damage or finish casting a damaging ability combat
has started."** The shipped opener finished **two** damaging casts:

```
  mage_armor       4.0s   not damaging, free at any lead
  frostfire_bolt   2.5s   DAMAGING, finishes -0.049s   <- the owner's line
  scorch           1.5s   DAMAGING, finishes -0.029s   <- a PROJECT addition, +25.1
```

Both deficits are the haste artefact on a lead written as the spell's declared
cast time, which is not a violation to fix. **B1 is the violation: there were
two.** The owner's line stays, the addition goes, and the +25.1 it was credited
with was bought with a fight that had already begun.

**`docs/profile-optimisation.md`'s APPENDIX A HAD ALREADY FLAGGED THIS PROFILE
AS FAILING** -- audited, written down, and still shipped for a release. So the
appendix was doing its job as a *record* and nothing was doing the job of acting
on it. `mage_fire` is still on that list with the same defect (a `fireball` at
-0.059s and a `scorch` at -0.029s), and `shaman_elemental` with two of the
owner's own lines.

**AND THE LEGAL DESIGN SPACE IS EMPTY, WHICH IS WORTH A ROW RATHER THAN A
SHRUG.** An addition must be a non-damaging action, and this build's only
non-damaging castables are Mage Armor (already there), Evocation (a channel,
refused outright) and two things **already off the global cooldown** -- the
Spellblasting potion and the Gnome racial. Both measured **bit-identical** at a
0s lead, which is the retracted Arcane Power lesson measured rather than
asserted: *pre-casting is only worth the global cooldown it saves.* Each
variant's precombat list was printed before the fights ran, so "identical" here
means worth nothing rather than a patch that did not apply.

### The two biggest gains are 60-second artefacts, and that is the round's finding

| | 60s | 180s | 300s |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| baseline | 1057.7 | 851.2 | 658.8 |
| **eureka gate (SHIPPED)** | **+5.99 real** | +0.34 noise | +2.80 noise |
| `critical_mass` 5/5 via `master_of_elements` | **+21.80 real** | **-10.29 real** | **-7.90 real** |
| the owner's scorch window 3s -> 5s | **+18.54 real** | +5.22 noise | +0.21 noise |

**THE CLOCK IS THE ONE THING A PAIRED PROBE STRUCTURALLY CANNOT SEE** -- every
profile carries `durationSeconds: 60`, so the fight length sits in the baseline
AND in every variant and cancels. Round 25 added that to the procedure as
something to read by hand; this is the first round where reading it changed what
shipped.

**THE MECHANISM IS A REGIME CHANGE AND IT IS ISOLATED.** `master_of_elements`
3->2 with the freed point spent on a deliberately DEAD talent measures
**+1.22 +/- 1.90 noise at 60s and -28.29 +/- 6.70 REAL at 180s**. So the third
rank of a mana talent is worth nothing in a minute and worth real DPS in three,
because this build is **global-cooldown bound at 60s with 962 mana spare and
mana bound by 180s**. `critical_mass` 5/5 is a genuine gain at both lengths
(about +21 and +17); what fails is paying for it out of the mana.

**AND THERE IS NO OTHER ROUTE TO IT, WHICH IS WHAT MAKES THIS A DEAD END RATHER
THAN A TRADE-OFF.** Both trees are tight at a tier -- Fire has exactly 15 points
below tier 15 and Frost exactly 20 below tier 20 -- so buying `critical_mass`
5/5 without touching `master_of_elements` means Frost 21 and a Fingers of Frost
charge: **-6.57 +/- 7.53 at 60s and -13.26 +/- 6.61 at 180s**. No talent change
measured is a gain at more than one fight length, so the 0/29/22 tree stands.

**THE DECIDING INSTRUCTION IS THE OWNER'S OWN**: *"rotations need to be built
for longer than 1 minute ... ensure that variable combat time is taken into
account."* A change worth +22 at the published clock and -10 at a realistic one
is optimising the clock rather than the profile. **If 60 seconds is the real
target, this is +21.80 sitting in one line of `presets.ts`** -- see the
questions table.

### Scorch's refresh window is a Pyroblast generator, not a debuff timer

The owner's condition is *"or scorch duration <= 3 seconds"*. Tightening it to
1.5s is **-33.36** and loosening it to 5s is **+18.54 REAL**, which reads
backwards for a maintenance debuff already at **99.9% uptime**. The mechanism is
next door and has nothing to do with the debuff:

```
  HEATING_UP_TRIGGERS = ['fireball', 'frostfire_bolt', 'fire_blast', 'scorch']
  heatingUp:  on 'dealt',  outcomes: ['crit']
```

**So every Scorch CRIT is a Heating Up stack**, and three stacks is a Pyroblast
at a quarter of its cast time -- 20.0% of this build's damage. Scorch is the
cheapest and fastest of the four triggers and measures the second-highest crit
rate in the build at 34.2%, so widening its window buys Pyroblasts.

Swept to the debuff's full 30-second duration, the peak is at **5s**:

```
  3s (owner)   0.00     7s   +11.71     14s   +7.56     20s   -22.40
  5s          +18.88   10s    +2.37                     30s  -390.65
```

At 30s the condition is always true, Scorch becomes an ungated entry above
everything, and the list casts nothing else -- the end-point is in the sweep on
purpose, because that is where the proxy breaks. Asking the direct question
instead ("Scorch while Heating Up is below three stacks") is **-269.98**, for
the same reason.

**IT IS NOT SHIPPED, FOR THREE REASONS, AND ANY ONE WOULD DO.** It is the
owner's own quoted condition, from the commit titled *"The Mage's three lists,
as the ruleset owner specified them"*; `scorchNeeded` is **shared with
`MAGE_FIRE`**, so moving `SCORCH_REFRESH_SECONDS` would silently change a
profile this round does not name -- and a Fire optimisation round was running
concurrently in another worktree; and it is a 60-second artefact like the talent
change, worth +5.22 noise at 180s.

### Enchants: every alternative loses, and two slots have no live option at all

`stat_weights` over 13,500 fights settles nine of the ten armour slots with no
fights at all:

```
  Haste        12.1862 per 1%     Agility      0.0000   cannot read it
  Crit rating  12.0770 per 1%     Stamina      0.0000   cannot read it
  Spell power   0.7832 per point  Spirit      -0.0029   interval swallows it
  Intellect     0.2563 per point  Mana per 5  -0.0051   interval swallows it
```

**SO THE TWO INERT ENCHANTS IN THIS LOADOUT ARE CORRECT RATHER THAN AN
OVERSIGHT.** The cloak offers +1% Dodge, +5 Agility, +60 Armor and -2% Threat;
the feet offer +4 Defense Skill, Minor Speed, +7 Agility and +7 Stamina. Dodge,
armor and defense skill are unreadable because nothing attacks this build,
threat is a permanent ruling, Minor Speed is `unmodelled`, and agility and
stamina weigh **zero** -- so all eight options are worth nothing and both slots
measured **BIT-IDENTICAL** when swapped. **A correct enchant can be worth
nothing, and here it is structural.**

**THE WEAPON IS THE ONLY ROW A TABLE CANNOT ANSWER, AND REVELATION LOSES.**
`twoHand` offers +65 Spell Power, +25 Agility, Crusader and **Revelation** --
and Revelation triggers "when a non-periodic spell fails to critically strike",
which this build does about twenty times a fight. It measures
**-17.25 +/- 14.75 REAL** against the shipped +65 Spell Power.

**AND THE CONTROL ROWS CONFIRMED THE WEIGHT TABLE TO THE DECIMAL.** Crusader and
+25 Agility both measured **-50.97 +/- 0.59** -- identical, because both replace
the spell power with something worth exactly zero to a caster -- and
`65 x 0.7832 = 50.9`. Two rows agreeing exactly is normally this project's tell
for a patch that did not apply; here it is the arithmetic being right.

### Four things read rather than measured, and one was already written down

1. **`mage_armor`'s entry was dead and the owner had already ruled on it.** The
   Arcane list's own comment said so and named this round: *"the other two Mage
   lists still carry theirs ... recorded here so the next Mage round starts with
   it."* It is a thirty-minute buff pre-cast at a 4s lead, so the gate is false
   from the pull onward. **`USES=1` reported it as 1.0 uses**, which is the
   pre-combat cast counted against the entry -- the one reading that would have
   flagged it instead showed a working line. **The Fire list still carries its
   copy.**
2. **Eureka was ungated, which the same comment priced at +1.92 on Arcane.** The
   Gnome racial is an 8-second window of +10% non-periodic damage spent on
   whatever the first poll happens to be. Fingers of Frost beat a Pyroblast
   window, and a *looser* "either window" gate beat neither (+4.53 against
   +5.99) -- a window spent on a Pyroblast moment is one not spent on a Shatter
   moment. Ungating it again on the shipped build measures **-5.99 +/- 0.95**,
   the exact mirror.
3. **Step 1's sixth read found nothing here, and found something next door.**
   `grep triggersGcd src/game/abilities/mage.ts` returns two hits and this build
   casts neither. **`combustion` is an instant that applies an aura on a 180s
   cooldown and takes a global cooldown** -- the exact shape the owner corrected
   on Arcane -- but it needs `critical_mass` 5/5 and this build has 4/5, so it is
   out of this profile's book. **The FIRE Mage takes it.** See the questions
   table.
4. **Hit is capped and the ladder denies it.** `stat_weights PLAN=1` offers
   "+5.00% to cap Spells"; `elemental_precision` is declared
   `schools: ['frost', 'fire']` and this build takes it 5/5, so every one of its
   spells carries school-scoped hit the ladder cannot see. The `avoid` column
   reads **0.9% to 1.6% against a 1% floor**. Appendix B's caveat, confirmed.

### Negative results, which are most of the round

- **Combustion restructured in**: -55.72 at 60s, -57.09 at 180s. It costs
  `fingers_of_frost` 2/2 *and* strands `shatter` 3/3, because Frost 20 cannot
  hold the capstone that wants exactly 20 points below it.
- **Ice Lance back in: -68.12 +/- 10.05**, independently reproducing the +74.1 a
  previous round measured for taking it out -- on a different base, with a
  corrected coefficient, by a different route.
- **Pyroblast's gate at 2 stacks instead of 3: -118.31.** The owner's 3 is right.
- **Fire Blast's 10% mana floor never binds**: ungating it is bit-identical, so
  the owner's gate is a safety net that costs nothing, exactly like Evocation's.
  Tightening it to 20% and 30% is -6.61 and -16.91.
- **The Spellblasting potion held for a Pyroblast window**: +2.48 +/- 2.55,
  noise.
- **The mana potion instead of Spellblasting**: -9.66, despite a headroom of 962.
- **`school_fire` is the right consumable pool**, confirmed by a control: the
  frost pool measures **-31.37 +/- 0.36**. Frostfire Bolt declares
  `school: 'fire'` and reaches Frost only through `countsAsSchools`, which
  school-scoped spell power deliberately does not read.
- **`improved_fireball` is NOT the dead talent its name makes it look.** It reads
  "Fireball **and Frostfire Bolt**", so five points nobody would defend from the
  damage table are the largest talent in the build -- half a second off a 2.5
  second cast on 42.2% of its damage. Its sibling `improved_frostbolt` names
  Frostfire Bolt nowhere and IS dead. **Read the tooltip, not the name.**

### One variant was void, and it is the worked example

`addEntry` appends, and the last entry in this list is an **ungated**
`frostfire_bolt` -- a floor under everything below it. So Ice Lance added at the
bottom measured `IDENTICAL FIGHTS`, **which reads exactly like "worth nothing"
and means "unreachable"**. Placed where the entry actually lived it is -68.12.
The row is kept in the file, labelled void, because *"a new entry goes at the
bottom"* is a rule about an entry that costs something, and the property that
makes the bottom safe is the same one that makes it useless.

### And a suite flake that is systemic rather than this round's

Two full-suite runs failed in a row, **each in a different file**, both at the
5000ms default and both passing standalone with a 15x margin:
`protectedChannelTicks.test.ts` at 285ms, then `variableCostIsReported.test.ts`.
Both walk the source tree and read every `.ts` file synchronously while vitest
transforms 167 test files in parallel -- CLAUDE.md's **fifth** documented
instance of that class, now the sixth and seventh.

**SIX TESTS IN THE SUITE READ THE SOURCE TREE AND ONLY ONE CARRIED A LIMIT.**
Patching them as they surface is whack-a-mole, so all six now carry
`READS_THE_SOURCE_TREE_MS`. **Per-test and not `testTimeout`**: widening the
global default would hide the next test that genuinely gets slower, which is the
thing a timeout is good at catching. Both failures were captured before
re-running, which is the only reason either could be diagnosed.

## Round THIRTY-THREE: the Ele Shaman, where the clock was the whole answer

**THE SECOND PROFILE RUN FROM `docs/profile-optimisation.md`, AND THE ROUND
FOUND THE FRAME RATHER THAN THE ROTATION.**

| | |
| --- | --- |
| theme, derived | `32/10/9`, Troll, caster, standing target. Majority Elemental, and it stays |
| build URL | <https://talentsforever.com/shaman/60/5500331320001351-055-05301-> |
| Gate A | 51 points, `legalAllocation` drops nothing |
| Gate B | **still FAILS, unchanged** -- two damaging casts, both the owner's own lines |
| Gate C | E65 untouched; only `enchantId` is reachable in the variant helpers |
| containment | **27 of 28 profiles bit-identical** across three seeds; only `shaman_elemental` moved |
| suite | 3,731 tests, 167 files, green; typecheck clean |

### The finding is that the profile loses more than half its DPS to the clock

`tools/probe_clock.ts`, which this round added because nothing printed cast
counts per fight length:

| fight | DPS | mana headroom | Lightning Bolts | Lava Bursts | Flame Shock uptime |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 60s | **802.1** | 577 (**6.7%**) | 26.2 | 5.97 | 96.8% |
| 120s | 542.9 | 96 (**1.0%**) | 33.9 | 6.25 | -- |
| 180s | 459.1 | 210 (**1.7%**) | 41.4 | 7.35 | **64.1%** |
| 300s | 384.5 | 189 (**1.2%**) | 55.7 | 8.80 | **53.7%** |

It spends the entire pool inside the first minute and then stands still. Lava
Burst has a ten second cooldown and gets **7.35 casts in 180 seconds** against
the eighteen the cooldown allows.

**AND THAT FALSIFIED THE LOAD-BEARING SENTENCE IN THE VARIANT FILE**, which read
in capitals: *"THE ELEMENTAL BUILD IS CAST-BOUND AND NOT MANA-BOUND, WHICH IS
THE FINDING THAT RULES OUT HALF ITS TREE."* Measured honestly, at 60 seconds,
where it is true.

### A talent was filed under the wrong ruling, and the ruling was the talent next door's

**Mindfulness** -- "Allows 17/33/50% of your Mana regeneration to continue while
casting" -- was declared a ruled-out **threat** effect, reason *"Threat, which
the engine does not track."* That is **`natural_grace`'s tooltip**, one talent
across in the same tier; `natural_grace`, which really is threat, was filed
under `healing`. **The two had each other's rulings.**

Only one of the two swaps cost anything. Threat and healing are both out of
scope, so Natural Grace was correctly dead under the wrong label. Mindfulness
was a live mana talent **filed as answered** -- and `manaRegenBypass` is exactly
that stat, granted by six talents in five other classes, with `manaPerTick`'s
own comment quoting this tooltip almost verbatim.

**THE OWNER'S OWN SPREAD SPENDS THREE POINTS THERE.** An earlier round moved
them out on that reason and measured **+2.95 +/- 0.63** for the move -- correct
arithmetic on a talent that did nothing. **A wrong `scope` produced a genuine
measurement arguing for making the build worse**, which is the only failure mode
in that section that leaves a positive number behind.

### What shipped: one talent change, and it is free at the published clock

`totemic_focus` 4 to 1 and `mindfulness` 3; `eye_of_the_storm` 3 and
`elemental_reach` 2 to `convection` 5.

| | 60s | 180s | 300s |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **mindfulness 3 + convection 5 (shipped)** | **+1.01 +/- 2.09** noise | **+84.08 +/- 2.84** real | **+80.89 +/- 2.20** real |
| mindfulness 3 alone | +1.11 | +43.98 +/- 2.49 | +48.75 |
| mindfulness 2 | +1.35 | +28.01 | +29.72 |
| mindfulness 1 | +0.67 | +12.84 | +13.82 |
| convection 5 alone | +1.22 | +19.05 +/- 2.35 | +18.77 |
| reverberation 5 alone | +0.26 | **+0.03 +/- 0.47** | +0.14 |
| the owner's spread (ak 1, totems 5, mindfulness 3) | -2.00 | +47.00 | +52.49 |

**THE RANK LADDER IS MONOTONE AND THE PAIR IS SUPER-ADDITIVE**, which is what
says this is the mechanism and not a boundary artefact: 43.98 and 19.05 make
**84.08** rather than their 63.03 sum, because both buy mana and enough mana
lifts the build out of the dry regime. Mana gained goes 12,317 to 13,597 at 180s
and Lightning Bolt 41.4 to 51.0 casts, **with Searing Totem's damage going
slightly DOWN** -- so the gain is in the nukes.

**IT SHIPPED BECAUSE IT COSTS NOTHING AT 60 SECONDS.** That is the line this
round drew: ship what is free at the published clock and gains at every other
one; raise what trades one clock against another.

### One side effect, and it reads like a regression

**THE DEMONIC RUNE'S ENTRY GOES FROM 1.0 CASTS A FIGHT TO 0.1 AT 60 SECONDS**,
because the build no longer reaches its 10% mana gate -- headroom at 60s goes
577 to **1,220**. At 180s it goes the other way, 1.07 to **2.23**. The gate is
the owner's stated default ("Demonic rune being used at 10% mana remaining
should be the default behavior for pure mana users"), so **a change that makes a
build less likely to run dry correctly makes its emergency mana entry fire
less**. Nothing to fix; said here because a near-zero in the uses column is
normally the finding.

### Four answers invert with the clock, and none of them shipped

| | 60s | 180s |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Major Mana Potion over Spellblasting | **-13.33 +/- 1.65** | **+75.77 +/- 3.44** |
| removing the Searing Totem entry | **+8.12 +/- 1.50** | **-52.74 +/- 2.77** |
| removing the Lava Burst entry | -5.51 +/- 4.87 | **+6.97 +/- 3.49** |
| Convection 5/5 -- shipped anyway, free at both | -0.18 noise, re-read +1.22 | +19.05 |

The potion costs 13.3 at the published clock to gain 75.8 at a realistic one,
would make this the only profile in the project carrying a mana potion, and
reverses the written rule that *"`potion` takes the damage buff"*. It is in the
questions table with both figures.

### One row per dimension, including the five that did not move

| dimension | what was asked | result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **1 gear** | Gate C only | E65 untouched. Nothing to do |
| **2 enchants** | five head/legs swaps, the gloves slot | **every alternative loses** -- and the figures are an artefact, see below. Shipped loadout kept |
| **3 talents** | seven full 51-point spreads, swept by rank | **+84.08 shipped.** Reverberation 5 is +0.03 for five points; the five remaining dead points are FORCED by tier 20 and 25 |
| **4 pre-combat** | all four actions priced by REMOVAL | **-13.55 to -17.56 each at 60s, all noise at 180s.** Nothing added, nothing moved. A 5s Elemental Mastery lead is +0.47 and would rewrite the owner's line |
| **4 APL** | Earth Shock at six mana gates, two filler floors at four thresholds each, Chain Lightning, four entry removals | **every candidate loses.** Earth Shock is monotone toward "never"; the filler floor loses at every threshold |
| **5 consumables** | blasted lands, potion, flask, food | **every alternative loses at 60s.** The potion inverts at 180s and is a question |

### Earth Shock: the stated reason was stale and the real one is damage per mana

The list's header read *"Flame Shock's TWELVE seconds of burn beat one instant
hit."* Call of Flame 3/3 makes that burn **twenty-one** seconds against a six
second shock cooldown, so there is barely any contention -- and the owner's own
ruling says so: *"these do share a cooldown, but Flame Shock's duration is long
enough such that they can be alternated, therefore both deserve to be on the
list."*

What rules it out is the pool. At 760 spell power Earth Shock is **594 damage in
a 1.5s global cooldown for 450 mana** and Lightning Bolt **739 in a 2.0s cast
for 220**: it wins the global cooldown 396 to 295 a second and loses the pool
**1.32 to 3.36 a mana**. Swept, it is monotone toward never -- **-0.51 at a 90%
mana floor falling to -21.84 at 50% at 60s, and -0.43 to -77.19 at 180s.**

Chain Lightning is the same shape with the same stale reason: Elemental Alacrity
takes it to a **1.5s cast, the GCD floor**, which is 371 damage a second against
the bolt's 370 -- a tie per global cooldown, 1.77 a mana, and **-11.63 at 60s,
-37.30 at 180s**.

### And Flame Shock's uptime falls with the clock, with no fix available

**96.8% at 60s, 64.1% at 180s, 53.7% at 300s** -- and Lava Burst is gated on the
shock being present, so the clock takes a maintenance entry and a cooldown entry
together. The cause is the starvation rule: the shock sits ABOVE the filler and
is still starved by it, because **priority does not reserve a resource** and 369
mana loses every race to 198.

**THE FIX THAT SHOULD HAVE WORKED DOES NOT.** A mana floor on the filler --
flat, or conditioned on something better actually pending -- swept 400 to 1200
mana, is **-9.22 to -28.33 at 180s** and 0.00 to -7.65 at 60s. The global
cooldowns spent idling are worth more than the uptime buys. So the falling
uptime is a property of the economy and not a defect in the order.

### Haste is not a stat weight on this profile, and finding that out took three routes

`stat_weights` at 180s priced **+1% haste at -15.12 +/- 0.77**, which is large,
statistically solid and wrong-signed. Taking the same 2% back off through the
two enchants that grant it measured **-22.58 +/- 3.31**. **Both directions lose,
which no slope can express.**

Swept finely at 60s (`tools/probe_stat_sweep.ts`, added this round):

| step | -20 | -10 | -2 | -1 | +1 | +2 | +10 | +20 | +40 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| delta | -12.04 | -9.80 | -1.01 | -0.46 | +0.54 | +1.12 | -4.34 | -0.40 | +7.74 |

A small positive slope with a step of about **-10** on top. The step is a
**Searing Totem boundary**: 55 second totem, re-application gated on the aura
having fallen off, and whether that last application ticks before the fight ends
moves with anything touching the cast rhythm. Totem ticks are **35.0 at haste
ratings 0, 9, 10, 11, 30 and 60 and 37.7 at 19, 20 and 21** -- and **20 is
exactly what the build wears**. Per-tick damage is identical at every value, so
it is a COUNT and not a magnitude.

**SO THE ENCHANT QUESTION ON THIS PROFILE IS UNANSWERED RATHER THAN ANSWERED**,
and both of the procedure's routes measure the artefact. The shipped loadout is
kept, which is also the zero-risk option. It is in the questions table.

### Three tools gained something, and one of them had been blind the whole time

- **`tools/probe_clock.ts`** -- new. One profile at four fight lengths with the
  mana book, the cast counts and the uptimes beside each. Its first draft wrote
  `encounter.durationSeconds`, a field that does not exist, and **all four
  lengths came back identical to the decimal** -- this project's own tell for a
  patch that did not apply rather than for a change worth nothing.
- **`tools/probe_stat_sweep.ts`** -- new. One stat, both directions, paired.
  What distinguishes a weight from a discontinuity.
- **`tools/stat_weights.ts` now takes `DURATION=`**, which it never did, so
  **every weight it has ever printed was a weight at sixty seconds.** On this
  profile `manaPer5` and `spirit` read 0.000, "not measured: the interval
  swallows the value", at the default and are **0.6623 and 0.3613 a point** at
  180s, while Intellect goes 0.1337 to **0.6988** and spell power falls 0.6841
  to 0.4647. The whole table re-sorts.

### And a variant group's labels had expired

Three `elementalPrecombat` rows added actions the opener already carries, so
they were measuring a DUPLICATE: the Elemental Mastery row read IDENTICAL
FIGHTS -- the second cast is refused by its own cooldown -- and the Searing
Totem row read **-5.46**, which is the cost of a wasted pre-combat cast and not
a verdict on the totem. **A variant label is a claim about the build it is
applied to, and it expires when that build ships the thing it adds.** All four
rows are removals now, which is what prices a shipped action.

### The shared checkout, again

Another session was editing this tree live -- seven files modified and three
untracked, appearing mid-session. The baseline reads taken before that was
noticed were re-taken in a worktree at `ea3045e` and **reproduced exactly**, so
nothing here rests on a contaminated run. `git checkout -b` on a shared checkout
also moves **their** HEAD onto your branch; it was put back with
`git symbolic-ref`, which touches no files.

## Round TWENTY-SIX: auto shot is not suspended, and the suspension had two halves

**THE OWNER REVERSED A PREVIOUS RULING:** "a previous ruling where ranged
auto-attack on hunters is paused while casting sniper shot, aimed shot, and
multi-shot. This is no longer the case. The ranged auto-attack is not paused or
suspended. It will trigger DURING the cast of the named skills above."

| | before | after | |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **LW Ranged** | 977.4 | **1021.0** | **+43.7 REAL** |
| BM Hunter | 963.4 | 968.2 | +4.8, noise |
| the other 26 | — | — | **0.0 to the decimal** |

**THE CONTAINMENT CHECK IS THE 26 ROWS AND IT INCLUDES BOTH MELEE HUNTERS.** LW
Melee and Hawk Melee moved 0.0 and correctly so: the ruling names the RANGED
auto-attack, those builds swing two melee hands and never the bow, and their
lists contain no cast at all. So "it is a Hunter profile" does not predict that
a Hunter change moves it -- the same shape as the melee miss change that
measured 0.0 on both feral Druids.

### The suspension had two halves and `hold` only removes one

| | the half | what it did |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | `resetSwingTimers`, in `runCast` | starting the cast threw the bow's progress away and restarted the timer |
| 2 | the mid-cast branch in `scheduleSwing` | a shot that then came due INSIDE the cast was held until the cast ended |

`Ability.swingTimer: 'hold'` already existed -- the Warrior's Improved Slam --
and it removes the first half only. **IT IS THE NEAR MISS, AND IT IS WHAT A
READER REACHES FOR.** On a four-second Sniper Shot against a 2.4-second bow a
shot comes due inside the cast every time, so `hold` would have deferred it by
over a second: most of the loss, kept, with nothing on the results page to show
it. `continue` is a THIRD mode -- the swing lands mid-cast and the weapon keeps
its own rhythm straight through.

**PROVED BY REINSTATING EACH HALF SEPARATELY**, which is the only way to know a
test is not passing on a half fix: putting back the hold fails **7 of the 16**
tests, putting back the reset fails **6**.

**THE SWING HANDLER HAS TO ASK AT THE MOMENT A SWING COMES DUE, and by then the
only thing it has is `castEndsAt` -- a number.** `Combatant.castAllowsSwings`
carries the fact, assigned unconditionally beside `castEndsAt` at the one site
where a cast starts, so the two cannot disagree and an ordinary cast following a
`continue` one closes the window by itself with nothing to clear.
`Combatant.channelling` is the field that LOOKS like the answer and is not: it
holds an ability only for a CHANNEL, so a plain Aimed Shot sets it to null.

**DECLARED ON THE THREE THE OWNER NAMED**, not derived from "it is a shot" --
the rule that made Immolation Trap wait to be named rather than following
Explosive Trap in. Every other cast in the game still interrupts the swing.

### Two tests, because the first one was VACUOUS

**THE FIRST VERSION COMPARED A FIGHT WITH A SNIPER SHOT AGAINST ONE WITHOUT AND
PASSED WITH THE OLD SUSPENSION DELIBERATELY REINSTATED.** LW Ranged's opener
pre-casts Sniper Shot at a four-second lead, so the ability is on its
fifteen-second cooldown for the first ELEVEN seconds of the fight -- the cast at
one second was refused, both arms ran the identical fight, and "the timestamps
match" was true for the wrong reason.

**AND THE GUARD IT CARRIED COULD NOT SEE IT EITHER.** It asserted that a shot
landed inside the cast window, which is true anyway when nothing is cast. **A
test whose fixture cannot express the mistake proves nothing about it** -- the
stock Warlock list's lesson, and Preparation's three tests.

**THE PRESET CANNOT BE COMPARED SHOT FOR SHOT AT ALL, WHICH IS THE SECOND
FINDING.** Deadly Aspects is a 10% haste proc on Auto Shot, so the bow's cadence
is RNG-driven -- and the engine draws from ONE stream, so Sniper Shot's own
attack roll reorders every later draw and the two fights decorrelate. The arms
matched on one harness and differed by a shot on another. So there are two
fixtures now:

| | answers | cannot answer |
| --- | --- | --- |
| a SCRIPTED archer -- a fixed bow, no haste, no rotation, the real abilities | the exact timings, deterministically | whether a real build reaches it |
| the LW RANGED preset | that the real character does it | the exact timings |

**EVERY CASE NOW ASSERTS THE CAST HAPPENED**, by its `cast` event at the chosen
timestamp, which is what makes a refused cast a failure rather than a pass.
**AND EACH CAST IS CENTRED ON A SHOT** rather than started at a fixed moment:
at a flat one second, Multi-Shot's half-second window contained no shot at all,
so its row was passing because there was nothing to suspend. A failing guard is
how that was found.

### What it makes stale is the REASONING, not the entries

**ALL THREE HUNTER LISTS ARE UNCHANGED.** They are the owner's, and their list
outranks a measurement of ours -- which cuts both ways, so a ruling about the
ENGINE is not authority to rewrite them. What is rewritten is every figure
around them that priced a cast against a lost bow cycle, each of which now says
so:

- the **+23** for removing Aimed Shot from the Marksmanship list;
- the **-1** for Multi-Shot, whose first clause has expired and whose second
  ("three targets where there is one") has not;
- the **-12.1** that Sniper Shot was kept IN SPITE OF, on the owner's own call.
  That bill was a cycle and a quarter of bow; what the ability costs now is a
  global cooldown and 365 mana.

**AND THE SHOT-WINDOW GATE NOW COSTS RATHER THAN SAVES**, which is this round's
question for the owner. Removing it from all three gated entries measures
**+27.6 REAL on BM Hunter** and +8.6 (noise) on LW Ranged. It is kept, and the
asymmetry explains BM Hunter's small +4.8 above: its Aimed Shot was already
timed to lose no shot, so the ruling hands it little and the gate is what is
left on the table.
## Round TWENTY-FIVE: what the loop structurally cannot find

**THE OWNER AUDITED THE FIRST PROFILE THE PROCEDURE PRODUCED AND FOUND FOUR
THINGS IT HAD NOT.** This round is the fold-back, and the thing worth keeping is
not the four fixes -- it is that **all four sat in the same blind spot**:

| what the audit found | what the loop did instead |
| --- | --- |
| a dead `mage_armor` entry | `USES=1` reported **1.0 uses** -- the pre-combat cast, charged to the entry |
| two cooldowns wrongly ON the GCD | nothing in this project asks whether an ability is CONFIGURED right |
| the cooldown stack | no measurement proposes a shape nobody wrote |
| the one-minute clock | every tool defaults to it, so it is in the baseline AND in every variant |

**A PAIRED PROBE MEASURES A VARIANT AGAINST A BASELINE, SO ANYTHING TRUE OF BOTH
SIDES CANCELS AND IS INVISIBLE.** The clock cancels. An ability's `triggersGcd`
cancels until something changes it. A list's SHAPE cancels, because a variant
can only reorder, add or remove entries somebody already thought of.

**SO THE PROCEDURE IS SOUND AND INCOMPLETE IN A PREDICTABLE PLACE**, and the
answer is not more variants. `docs/profile-optimisation.md` carries the section
now, with four things to read by hand once a pass -- the clock, the
declarations, the pre-combat/APL overlap, and which entries fire together --
and **"stop when a pass ships nothing" now says explicitly that it means a local
optimum INSIDE THE FRAME THE LOOP MEASURES IN.**

**AND THE RETRACTION HAD LANDED IN THE TWO PLACES NOBODY READS.** The +24.2 for
pre-casting Arcane Power was withdrawn in `tools/arcaneVariants.ts` and in a
commit message, while **three places in this file went on asserting it** and the
procedure cited it approvingly in a third. All four are corrected. **A
correction that reaches one copy is this file's own drift failure**, and the
cheap guard is to grep the figure rather than the file being edited.

**THE GENERAL RULE IS WORTH MORE THAN THE NUMBER: pre-casting is only worth the
GLOBAL COOLDOWN it saves.** Nothing on an ability correctly off the GCD, and a
COST on a cooldown short enough to return inside the fight, because the window
is burned before the build is at full strength.

## Round TWENTY-FOUR: the owner's audit of the Arcane profile

**THREE ITEMS, GIVEN AS AN AUDIT RATHER THAN AS A BRIEF**, and the middle one
was worth all of the figure.

| | the item | result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | *"Mage Armor doesn't need to be captured in the main APL section."* | entry removed. It could never fire -- pre-cast at a 3s lead, thirty-minute buff, one-minute fight |
| 2 | *"Ensure Presence of Mind and Arcane Power don't trigger a global cooldown."* | **they both did.** `triggersGcd: false` on each |
| 3 | *"Set up a cooldown overlap condition with Arcane Wrath / Pyroblast."* | shipped as one `AplEntry.sequence`; **Pyroblast is the payload and Arcane Wrath is not** |

**TOGETHER +33.1, AND FIRE AND FROSTFIRE MOVED 0.0 TO THE DECIMAL** -- neither
takes Presence of Mind or Arcane Power, and their own Mage Armor entries were
left alone deliberately.

### Item 2 was a fact to read, not a measurement

`triggersGcd(ability)` is `ability.triggersGcd ?? ability.onNextSwing === undefined`,
so an ordinary ability is ON the global cooldown unless it opts out -- and
`abilities/mage.ts` was one of three ability files with no `triggersGcd: false`
anywhere. Both were paying a global cooldown they should not have.

**THE MISTAKE IS INVISIBLE IN EVERYTHING BUT THE DPS**, which is why it lasted:
an ability wrongly taking a global cooldown still applies the right aura, for
the right duration, on the right cooldown. Nothing reads wrong on the results
page.

### Item 3: the shape was right, the payload was not, and it was already happening

The potion, Presence of Mind and Pyroblast were three entries that fired
together. Written as a sequence they run **bit-identical fights** -- because
all three are off the global cooldown, and **two of them only became so in item
2**. So the pair-by-emergence was one change away from coming apart silently.
**Emergence is an accident of what is castable; a sequence is a guarantee.**

Measured over 1,000 paired iterations:

| the stack | |
| --- | --- |
| potion > PoM > **pyroblast** | **bit-identical** -- shipped |
| potion > PoM > arcane_wrath | **-72.4** |
| potion > AP > PoM > pyroblast | **-34.9** |
| potion > AP > PoM > arcane_wrath (the screenshot's shape) | **-43.8** |

**PRESENCE OF MIND ERASES A CAST TIME, SO THE PAYLOAD WANTS TO BE THE LONGEST
CAST** -- Pyroblast is six seconds against Arcane Wrath's 3.5, and Arcane Wrath
costs a talent point as well. **And Arcane Power cannot join the stack**: it is
pre-cast at a 0s lead for what was then measured at **+24.2** (**since RETRACTED** --
see below), and a sequence resolves in full before any
of it is cast, so a step on cooldown would refuse the lot and the stack would
silently never fire.

**THIS IS THE FIRST TIME ARCANE WRATH HAS BEEN PRICED AS ANYTHING BUT A
FILLER.** Its -76.7 and -118.2 were measured displacing Arcane Blasts every
cycle; here it is cast once inside a +30% window and displaces nothing, and it
still loses. **The old conclusion survives a reading it was never tested under.**

### Two things the round turned up on its own

**THE EVOCATION TRIPWIRE FIRED, EXACTLY AS IT WAS BUILT TO.**
`mageAbilities.test.ts` pinned ZERO Evocation casts across three profiles and
twenty seeds, with the owner's "This is intended" beside it and a note that it
"fails if a change makes Evocation start firing, which is the right moment to go
back to the owner." Item 2 is more casts a fight, an Arcane Blast at four stacks
costs eight times base, and the build now reaches a tenth of its pool on **seed
10 of 20** and casts it once. Mean headroom is still **2,638** mana, so this is
a TAIL and not a shift. **It is the honest reading of that entry rather than a
regression** -- a conditional entry that is almost never true costs the entries
below it nothing and pays out in a fight that does not go like the measured
ones. Re-pinned PER PROFILE and exactly: Fire 0, Frostfire 0, Arcane 1.

**AND A SEQUENCE STEP WAS INVISIBLE TO `USES=1`.** `printUses` keys on
`abilityId`, so every step after the first vanished from the numbered list AND
fell into the **"not in the list, and still dealing damage"** block -- the one
heading in that report meaning "nobody asked for this damage". Pyroblast
reported exactly that way the moment the stack shipped. Steps now print indented
under their entry with their own cast counts and **no verdict column**, because
a step has no condition of its own and cannot be "never fired" independently.

### And the +24.2 for pre-casting Arcane Power is RETRACTED

**THE OWNER'S QUESTION IS WHAT UNDID IT:** *"is pre-casting arcane power +24.2
over a normal rotational use, or is it +24.2 over the sandwiched in cooldown
overlap? I just find it hard to believe that arcane power would want to be used
prior to reaching 4 stacks of arcane blast buff."*

**IT WAS OVER A NORMAL ROTATIONAL USE, AND IT WAS STALE.** The measurement was
`removePrecombat('arcane_power')` with the LIST ENTRY LEFT IN PLACE -- and that
entry is gated on a **Missile Barrage proc**, which has nothing to do with the
Arcane Blast ramp. So it priced "pre-cast" against "cast on the first barrage",
never against the cap and never against the stack. **And it predates item 2**:
part of what pre-casting bought was a global cooldown the in-fight cast no
longer pays.

Re-measured as a four-way matrix, 600 paired iterations a cell, as a delta from
the shipped pre-cast:

| | 60s | 180s | 300s |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| no pre-cast, barrage entry | +4.19 | +0.52 | **+8.07** |
| no pre-cast, gated at the 4-stack cap | -2.76 | -4.16 | +4.00 |
| no pre-cast, sandwiched into the stack | -2.76 | -4.19 | +3.00 |
| pre-cast AND entry gated at the cap | 0.00 | -0.88 | -0.31 |

**IT DOES NOT REPRODUCE AT ANY LENGTH.** Pre-casting is worth nothing
measurable at 60s and is slightly NEGATIVE by 300s, so the owner's instinct is
right in direction. The reason it is small either way is that the ramp reaches
four stacks in about five seconds against a fifteen second window, so a
pre-cast window already covers most of the four-stack period. **No placement
change is shipped** -- every cell is within a few DPS and the shipped one is
theirs.

**THE GENERAL RULE IS THAT PRE-CASTING IS ONLY WORTH THE GLOBAL COOLDOWN IT
SAVES.** On an ability correctly off the GCD it is worth nothing, and on a
cooldown short enough to return inside the fight it COSTS something, because
the window is burned before the build is at full strength.

**AND THREE EARLIER PLACES IN THIS FILE QUOTED +24.2 AND NOW SAY RETRACTED.** A
correction that lands in one paragraph and leaves the other copies standing is
this file's own drift failure, and this one had landed only in
`tools/arcaneVariants.ts` and a commit message -- neither of which is the
document anybody reads. **Grep for a figure before believing it was updated
with its source.**

### And one I got wrong: the `arcane_power` entry is NOT dead

This round flagged it as dead "for the same reason Mage Armor was" -- pre-cast
at t=0 on a three-minute cooldown, in a one-minute fight. **The owner: "arcane
power being pre-cast doesn't remove it from the main combat APL because the
moment a combat run exceeds 3 minutes it'll want to be cast a second time."**

**THEY ARE RIGHT, AND IT IS MEASURABLE.** Cast counts over twenty seeds:

| fight | arcane_power | evocation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 60s | 1.00 | **0.05** |
| 120s | 1.00 | 0.40 |
| 180s | **1.40** | 0.70 |
| 240s | **2.00** | **1.00** |
| 300s | 2.00 | 1.00 |

So the entry starts firing at 180s and fires every fight by 240s. **The two
cases are not alike at all**: Mage Armor is a thirty-minute buff that no fight
length brings back, and Arcane Power is a three-minute cooldown that a fight
longer than three minutes reaches again. **"It never fires" was a fact about the
CLOCK and was written down as a fact about the LIST.**

### Which makes the Evocation tripwire a clock artefact too

The same table answers the owner's other point -- "things like evocation not
firing might just be the short nature of combat". It is exactly that: **0.05
casts at 60s and 1.00 at 240s.** The ruling "This is intended" was given about
a sixty-second fight, and the test now says so rather than pinning a bare
number as though it described the ruleset.

### And the profile changes REGIME with length, which is the larger finding

**DPS falls 1147.9 -> 954.1 from 60s to 300s, about -17%.** At sixty seconds the
build ends with 2,638 mana spare and is GLOBAL-COOLDOWN bound; by five minutes it
is MANA bound. That is not a smaller number, it is a different optimisation
problem -- and several of this profile's "settled" answers are answers about a
one-minute clock:

- the Major Mana Potion measured **-12.7** against Spellblasting;
- `manaPer5` and `spirit` both weigh **~0.000**;
- Evocation's gate was never reached.

**ALL THREE WOULD BE RE-ASKED AT A LONGER DURATION**, and none of them has been.

### So fight length is an axis now, and the global default is the owner's call

`tools/probe_build.ts` takes `DURATION=` and applies it to the baseline AND
every variant, so a placement question can be asked at the length it actually
matters at. Every profile still carries `durationSeconds: 60` from
`createDefaultProfile`, with a fixed +/-5% `FIGHT_DURATION_VARIANCE`.
**Changing that default moves every published figure on all 28 profiles**, so
it is not a thing to pick unilaterally -- it is put to the owner with the table
above.

## Round TWENTY-THREE: the Arcane Mage, run from a written procedure

**THE FIRST ROUND DRIVEN BY [docs/profile-optimisation.md](docs/profile-optimisation.md)
RATHER THAN BY JUDGEMENT**, which is the thing being tested as much as the
profile. The procedure's three gates are checked before anything is measured,
and Gate B is what made this round mostly a demolition.

### One row per dimension, including the five that did not move

| dimension | what was tried | result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **1 theme** | 35 Arcane / 11 Fire / 5 Frost, Gnome, caster, standing target | unchanged and legal. `encode_talent_build.mjs` decodes to 51 points |
| **2 gear** | E65, untouched | nothing to do. `buildVariants.ts` cannot express an item change |
| **3 enchants** | the whole armour set settled by ARITHMETIC off the stat weights; the weapon measured | **no change.** +65 Spell Power beats Revelation by **24.5** and +25 Agility by **41.8** |
| **4 talents** | the shipped spread, Heating Up, Fire out, Fire hit out, **Arcane Wrath** | **no change.** All Fire out is **-52.4**; Arcane Wrath is **-76.7** and **-118.2** |
| **5 pre-combat** | the whole remaining design space under Gate B -- seven rows | **the four illegal casts REMOVED, -96.5 +/- 9.4.** Arcane Power at t=0 read **+24.2**, **since RETRACTED** |
| **6 combat APL** | the stack gate re-swept, Fire Blast in four places, the PoM/Pyroblast pair, two cooldown re-gates, and **the channel floor** | **+13.2 +/- 3.2 SHIPPED.** Everything else loses |
| **7 consumables** | Spellblasting vs Major Mana, Rune vs Healthstone, Blasted Lands | **no change.** Major Mana is **-12.7** |

### The one finding, and it came off the uptime table

**ARCANE BLAST'S BUFF WAS DOWN FOR 22.3% OF THE FIGHT AND NOTHING HAD ASKED
WHY.** The window is fifteen seconds from FIRST application, the ramp spends six
of them, and Arcane Missiles is a CHANNEL -- so the window kept expiring
part-way through a channel, the remaining missiles were paid unbuffed, and the
re-ramp could not start until the channel ended.

Refusing a channel with under two seconds of window left is **+13.2 +/- 3.2**,
and uptime goes **77.7% -> 83.0%** with Arcane Blast's own casts going 13.1 to
14.0 a fight at a higher average -- 948 to 1,021 -- because each now lands
inside a window rather than after it.

**TWO SECONDS IS MID-PLATEAU RATHER THAN TUNED.** Swept: 0.5s **+4.9**, then 1s
through 3s all **+13.1 to +13.5** and indistinguishable, 4s and 5s back to
noise. The boundary is SAFE, not tight.

**AND THE 30-BATCH HARNESS CALLS IT `noise`** -- 1090.3 against 1080.1 with an
interval of 12.1 -- which is the documented case of a change with a known exact
mechanism being real and labelled noise. The paired figure and the uptime are
what settle it.

### What the gates actually cost, and what they bought

**GATE B DELETED THE PREVIOUS ROUND'S ENTIRE HEADLINE.** The opener finished
FOUR damaging casts before the pull; removing them is **-96.5 +/- 9.4**, measured
by RESTORING them as a variant rather than by subtracting two figures taken at
different iteration counts.

**AND THE REMAINING DESIGN SPACE IS NEARLY EMPTY, WHICH IS WORTH RECORDING SO
NOBODY RE-DERIVES IT.** One damaging cast, so it is Arcane Blast OR Pyroblast;
a channel is refused outright, so Missiles and Evocation cannot go there at all;
everything else must be a non-damaging instant. Of the four this build owns:
**Arcane Power +24.2** (**RETRACTED**, see round 24), the potion **+1.4**, Eureka **-2.0**, Presence of Mind
**-17.7** (its charge is spent on the filler).

**PYROBLAST AS THE PULL INSTEAD OF THE ARCANE BLAST IS +13.8 +/- 9.3 AND IS NOT
TAKEN**, because it would DELETE one of the owner's own two lines rather than add
to it -- which `precombatOpeners.test.ts` refuses and the procedure forbids. **It
is a priced option for the owner, not a decision for us.**

### Two questions answered by measurement rather than by asking

**THE REFRESH BEHAVIOUR IS WORTH ABOUT NOTHING.** HANDOVER called it "the first
thing to settle" and said it blocked the whole profile. Flipped to `reset` the
profile reads **1089.0 against 1090.3**, and a maintenance entry holding four
stacks all fight is **-1.1** on top of that -- because a four-stack refresh costs
EIGHT TIMES base, and the channel floor already captures what maintenance was
buying. **The mechanism was verified before the figure was believed**: under
`reset` plus maintenance, uptime does reach 89.5%, so the rotation the owner
describes is achievable and simply is not worth anything.

**AND ARCANE WRATH IS CLOSED.** Its old justification rested on displacing Arcane
Blasts and breaking the ramp, and CLAUDE.md records that the justification
expires with the ramp reading. Re-measured under BOTH readings it still loses
heavily: **-76.7** and **-118.2** under `ignore`, **-52.2** and **-85.9** under
`reset`. **The reasoning had expired; the conclusion had not.**

### And two reporting gaps found while using the tools

- **`tools/enchant_report.ts` COVERED TEN ARMOUR SLOTS AND NO WEAPON** -- nine
  of the thirteen slots an enchant can target, and a tenth column (`shoulders`)
  that no enchant can target at all. So the staff's +65 Spell Power, the single
  largest enchant on the profile at about 41.8 DPS, was invisible in the report
  that exists to show enchants. **FIXED**: the weapons get a second block, and
  the slot list is checked against one DERIVED from the enchant catalogue, which
  throws naming any enchantable slot that has no column -- verified by removing
  a column and watching it fail. The armour block keeps the owner's
  spreadsheet shape so it still reads against their table by eye.
  **It made the Druids' Crusader visible, and the question raised about it was
  a BAD one that one command closed.** Seeing `Enchant Weapon - Crusader` on a
  held two-hander the Cat and Bear never swing, this was written up as "whether
  it procs at all for a feral Druid is unmeasured". The owner: **"Crusader does
  proc for feral druid forms, and you should already know that and also be able
  to verify that."** Both halves are right -- a paw is BUILT from the held
  weapon and a form swing is a `mainHand` use, which CLAUDE.md already states,
  and the verification is one line of `probe_profile_report`:
  **Holy Strength (Main Hand) reads 32.4% uptime on the Cat and 32.2% on the
  Bear.** So the enchant is live and correctly chosen on both.
  **THE LESSON IS ABOUT THE QUESTION, NOT THE ENCHANT.** A newly visible figure
  invites a question, and the cost of asking one that is already answered in
  this file AND readable in one command is paid by the person who has to answer
  it. **Check the uptime table before writing down a doubt about a proc** --
  which is this file's own rule that a flagged reading on something already
  ruled on is a NOTE and not a question.
- **THE HIT LADDER AND THE HIT WEIGHT CONTRADICT EACH OTHER IN ONE RUN.** The
  ladder offers "+5.00% to cap Spells" and the weight says "nothing: this build
  cannot read it". **The weight is right**: all three Mages carry 11 points of
  school-BLIND hit against a usable cap of 16 and the missing five arrive as
  SCHOOL-scoped hit from a talent, which the ladder does not fold. Measured
  `avoid` is 0.9-2.0% against a 1% floor. Written up as Appendix B of the
  procedure.

## Round TWENTY-TWO: the Arcane Mage, all five dimensions

> **THE PRE-COMBAT HALF OF THIS ROUND IS VOID** -- its opener deals about 1,200
> to 1,400 damage before the pull, which the owner has ruled starts combat, so
> the +95.9 headline is an artefact. **And its APL half answers the wrong
> question**, because Arcane Blast is a maintenance buff held at four stacks
> rather than a ramp that is spent. See the section above. The negative results
> below -- the enchants, the consumables, the Fire investment -- were measured
> against the old opener and are still informative about those dimensions.

**THE OWNER RE-ASKED FOR ONE PROFILE BECAUSE THE FIRST PASS LEFT ITS APL
UNTOUCHED** -- "Spotting check Arcane mage profile. Nothing changed in the APL."
That was correct: the six-profile round moved its talents and its pre-combat and
asked the list nothing at all.

**THE ANSWER IS +95.9 +/- 8.2, ABOUT +8.9%, AND ALL OF IT IS IN THE OPENER.**
Everything else was already at a local optimum, which is worth as much as the
figure: **nineteen variants measured, two shipped.**

| dimension | rows | outcome |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 2. enchants | 5 | **every alternative loses.** Head/legs +1% Haste beats +8 Intellect by 2.07 and +8 Spirit by 5.42; gloves +20 Spell Power beats +1% Haste by 6.54 |
| 3. talents | 5 | **the shipped spread wins.** The Arcane tree has nothing left to buy |
| 4. APL | 10 | **the shipped list wins on every axis**, and its stack gate is the peak |
| 5. consumables | 3 | **the shipped row wins.** Spellblasting beats Major Mana by 13.16 |
| **4. pre-combat** | **9** | **+95.9 +/- 8.2** |

### Why the talents could not be improved

**EVERY ARCANE TALENT THAT TOUCHES DAMAGE IS ALREADY MAXED** -- arcane_focus
5/5, arcane_impact 3/3, arcane_mind 5/5, arcane_instability 3/3 -- and
**missile_barrage has TWO ranks, not three**, so the obvious upgrade does not
exist. What is left in that tree is a wand, a range increase, armour from
Intellect, mana regeneration the build never needs, and `improved_channeling`,
which is pure cast pushback and a declared `OutOfScope` member.

**SO THE ELEVEN FIRE POINTS WERE THE REAL QUESTION, AND THEY PAY.** The damage
table makes them look indefensible -- Pyroblast 4.5% and Ignite 0.3% for eleven
of fifty-one points -- and taking them out measures **-49.9**, because the
points they would move to do *literally nothing*. Taking `incineration` with
them measures **-60.5**, so its "+6% crit to Fire Blast, Ice Lance, **Arcane
Blast** and Scorch" is worth **+10.5** on its own: one Fire talent paying an
Arcane bill.

**AND `elemental_precision` IS DEAD AND UNMOVABLE, WHICH IS A DIFFERENT THING
FROM WORTH KEEPING.** Five points of Frost-and-Fire hit on a build whose only
Fire is 4.8% of the damage: removing it measures **-3.5 +/- 12.0**, noise, and
exactly what the arithmetic predicts -- 6% more miss on 4.8% of the damage is
0.29% of it. It stays because **there is nowhere better for the points to go**,
and `arcane_wrath`, the only unspent Arcane talent that deals damage, is
measured at -77 to -119 wherever it is cast.

**AND HEATING UP COSTS FIVE POINTS WHERE ONLY FOUR CAN LEAVE ARCANE.** The
harness refused the first attempt -- "wanted 51 legal 50 dropped [arcane_power]"
-- which is the `requiresRanks` lesson in its tier-sum form. Funded properly it
measures **-1.6**, and **paired with the Fire Blast entry it would need, -13.5**:
both halves or neither, and here neither.

### Why the APL could not be improved

| the change | |
| --- | --- |
| fire_blast at the bottom | **IDENTICAL FIGHTS** -- Arcane Blast above it is ungated and always castable, so it is a floor |
| fire_blast above arcane_blast | -8.7 +/- 12.0, noise |
| fire_blast above the gated missiles | **-27.2** |
| no pyroblast/presence_of_mind pair | **-13.2**, so the pair is worth +13.2 |
| presence_of_mind spent on arcane_blast | **-50.7** |
| pyroblast at the cap WITHOUT presence_of_mind | **-206.6** -- a real 6s cast is catastrophic |

**AND THE ARCANE BLAST STACK GATE IS SWEPT RATHER THAN COMPARED**, which is this
project's own rule after a two-point Eviscerate comparison hid a +10.8 peak
between its cells. The shipped **4** is the maximum and the fall is monotone on
both sides -- 5 is **-27.9**, 3 is **-39.4**, 2 is **-87.7**, 1 is **-139.8**.

### The opener, which is the whole round

**ARCANE BLAST IS A RAMP AND THE OLD OPENER CAST ONE.** Each stack is 15% of
Intellect as spell power -- **+65 a stack, +260 at four** on a base of 850 -- and
Arcane Missiles is **78% of the damage** with the highest coefficient in the kit.
So the ramp is really pricing every missile in the fight, and a pre-combat cast
