buys it for no global cooldown. Paired over 400 iterations against the shipped
one stack:

| stacks at the pull | |
| --- | --- |
| 0 | **-21.0** |
| 1 | the old baseline |
| 2 | **+31.1** |
| 3 | **+49.9** |
| 4 | **+74.7** |

Monotone to the cap, which is what says the boundary is the cap rather than a
peak inside it. **Pyroblast before the pull adds a further ~25** -- a six-second
cast made free of its cast time -- for **+95.9 +/- 8.2** together, against
+74.7 and +41.5 apart, which do not sum because both buy opening seconds.

**THE SHIPPED OPENER KEEPS THE OWNER'S OWN TWO LINES, AND A TEST IS WHAT
INSISTED.** The best arrangement measured had moved `mage_armor` off its stated
3s lead and replaced the owner's `arcane_blast` at 1.5 with four at other leads;
`precombatOpeners.test.ts` transcribes their openers a second time and failed on
exactly that. The two measure **+104.0 +/- 7.9** against **+95.9 +/- 8.2** --
8.1 apart against a combined interval of **11.4**, so indistinguishable, and
there was no measurement to trade against keeping the transcription.

### And the best figure in the round is discarded

**AN OPENER THAT MEASURED +105.3 IS NOT A POSSIBLE CAST SEQUENCE.** It held a
Pyroblast from t-6 to t0 **and** an Arcane Blast from t-1.5 to t0 --
`runPrecombat` resolves every action at timestamp zero and never asks whether
one cast was still in progress when the next began, so that list banked five
casts into four seconds of real time.

**IT WAS FOUND BY ADDING UP THE LEADS AND THE CAST TIMES BY HAND, BECAUSE
NOTHING ELSE COULD.** The figure sat 1.3 DPS above the best legal row, inside
its interval, in a table where every other row was honest. **A pre-combat list
is a claim about what the character did and the engine does not check it** --
and whether it should REFUSE an overlapping opener is a question for the owner.

**NOR COULD THE REPORTING CONFIRM THE RAMP ARRIVES.** The `USES=1` "before the
pull" block prints each action beside the ability's **whole-fight** cast count,
so four `arcane_blast` rows all read 13.2 and its `<-- NEVER HAPPENED` flag can
never fire for a spell that is also the filler -- and `aura_applied` fires ONCE,
because the rest are refreshes, so counting it says 1 where the answer is 4.
`tools/verify_opener.ts` reads the stack count off the combatant at the pull and
says **4 of 4**.

### And one small thing the uses report found

**EUREKA FIRED ON THE FIRST POLL, UNGATED, and gating it is +1.92 +/- 0.28.**
The Gnome racial is an 8-second +10% window and Arcane Power's entry beside it
is gated on a Missile Barrage proc for exactly that reason. **Its spread is
2.8**, because a gate that only moves WHEN a free off-GCD cooldown fires cannot
flip a roll -- so a figure that small is resolvable rather than noise. The
alternatives: on Arcane Power being up it is **bit-identical** (Arcane Power is
pre-cast, so the gate is never false), on the Arcane Blast cap **-2.92**, and
removed entirely **-16.35**.

**THE SHARED `RACIAL_COOLDOWNS` BLOCK IS WHY IT HAD NO GATE** rather than an
oversight: that constant is spread into lists belonging to no race, so a
condition written there would be a condition on three abilities the character
cannot cast.

### Two stale things fixed on the way

- The preset's own label read **"47 Arcane / 4 Fire"** against an allocation of
  35 / 11 / 5. That string is what the preset rail shows a person.
- `racialCooldowns.ts` described Eureka as **"the next three damaging abilities
  cost 10% less and hit 10% harder"** -- the charges design that
  `racials/auras.ts` says in its own words was deleted for an 8-second window.
  Two files disagreeing about one fact, with the stale one in the list everybody
  reads first.

**THE BASELINE TABLE IS NOT REBUILT**, per the owner's standing instruction that
they keep the baseline. The 30-fight harness reads **1088.0 +/- 36.6 before and
1169.2 +/- 20.7 after**; the paired 800-iteration **+95.9 +/- 8.2** is the
figure to trust.

## Round TWENTY-ONE: three rulings, and the one that was a misread field

**ALL THREE ARRIVED IN ONE MESSAGE AND ONLY ONE OF THEM MOVED A FIGURE**, which
is the usual shape: two confirmed a reading and one overturned an
implementation.

| | the ruling | what it cost |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Arcane Wrath** | "a 3.5 second cast with a 6 second cooldown ... 439 to 529 Arcane Damage, +5% of Maximum Mana, +100% of Spell Power" | the spell was shipped **INSTANT at 406-496 with a borrowed 0.429** -- all three corrected |
| **Winter's Chill** | "at 5 stacks is a 3x multiplier for Cone of Cold" | the compounding default gives `1.4^5 = 5.378`; **ADDITIVE**, and the flag is what got it ruled on |
| **Two Judgements** | "absolutely fine and intended for the raid buff contribution" | nothing. The question comes off the table above |

### The Arcane Wrath failure is the one worth reading

**EVERY RULE THIS PROJECT HAS FOR A SILENT SOURCE WAS APPLIED TO A SOURCE THAT
HAD SPOKEN.** The capture states `"cast": "3.5 sec cast"` **in its own field**.
The probe that read the entry asked for `castTime`, got `undefined`, and that
reading error became the claim "the capture states no cast time" -- written into
a test, with a negative assertion pinning the instant.

**AND FROM THERE EVERY STEP WAS CORRECT.** A tie-break does not apply to a
source that cannot express a field; an omission has not denied anything; a
neighbouring spell's coefficient is borrowable with a `PLACEHOLDER_` name and a
structural argument. So Fire Blast's 0.429 was borrowed on the argument that
"the sheet gives every Mage INSTANT the same `1.5 / 3.5`" -- **sound reasoning
resting on a fact that was not true.** `3.5 / 3.5` is 1.0, which is the owner's
own figure: reading the field would have landed on it without being told.

**THE TWO ERRORS PULLED OPPOSITE WAYS, WHICH IS WHAT MADE IT LOOK ORDINARY.** An
instant cast is generous and a coefficient at 43% of the real one is stingy, so
nothing about the spell's damage per second read wrong.

**AND THIS FILE ALREADY HAD THE RIGHT NUMBER.** The round-twenty scoping table
above says "395 mana, **3.5 sec cast**, 6 sec cooldown" in the row about this
very spell. **A fact that gets documented still needs somebody to read it** --
which is the mirror of this project's own rule that a difference that gets
documented still needs somebody to call it wrong, and is cheaper to avoid:
the source was right, the handover was right, and one field name was not.

### And the justification the owner asked for: it is not worth taking

"This seems like a spell worth taking, or at least providing very solid
justification for not taking." Five placements, paired over 400 iterations
against a 1077.6 baseline:

| the placement | |
| --- | --- |
| entry above the gated Arcane Missiles | **-118.53 +/- 11.59** |
| entry above Arcane Blast (the filler slot) | **-77.15 +/- 12.00** |
| entry at the BOTTOM of the list | 0.00, **IDENTICAL FIGHTS** -- never cast |
| talent taken, no entry -- the control | 0.00, **IDENTICAL FIGHTS** |
| gated on Arcane Blast at max stacks | 0.00, **IDENTICAL FIGHTS** -- never cast |

**AND THE SPELL IS NOT THE PROBLEM. IT CARRIES ITS WEIGHT.** In the filler slot
it is **25.5% of the profile's damage on 6.6 casts**, which is more than Arcane
Blast's own 15.4% in the same run.

**WHAT IT DISPLACES IS NOT DAMAGE, IT IS SPELL POWER.** Arcane Blast falls
**13.8 casts a fight to 9.8**, and Arcane Blast is this build's RAMP: each stack
grants 15% of Intellect as spell power, so at 342 Intellect five stacks are
**+257 on a base of 785**. The proof is in the column a DPS figure cannot show
-- Arcane Missiles' cast count falls by 0.2 and its SHARE falls **67.0% to
56.6%**, so every missile is hitting for about 15% less. It carries the highest
coefficient in the kit at 1.4729 and it is two thirds of the profile.
**Arcane Blast is not competing with Arcane Wrath on damage per second; it is
buying spell power for the 67% of the build that is Arcane Missiles.**

**SO THE PER-SECOND ARITHMETIC THAT MADE THIS LOOK CLOSE WAS COMPUTED AT BASE
SPELL POWER**, and that is the whole error. 434/sec for the Wrath against
381/sec for the Blast -- true at 785, and at five stacks Arcane Blast is
`236 + (785 + 257) x 0.4286 = 683` over 1.5 seconds, **455/sec and already
ahead** before a point of what the ramp does for anything else. **A per-second
comparison on a build with a ramp has to be taken at the RAMPED figure**, or it
prices the spell that breaks the ramp as though the ramp were free. That
arithmetic was sitting in `tools/mageVariants.ts` predicting the opposite of
what the measurement found, which is this project's own rule about a prediction
in a comment -- specific, mechanical, and reading exactly like a finding.

**AND PROTECTING THE RAMP MAKES THE SPELL UNREACHABLE**, which is why the gated
row is a 0.00 rather than a smaller loss: at max stacks the two Arcane Missiles
entries above it win, and below max stacks the gate refuses. There is no state
in which Arcane Wrath is the top castable entry -- the "entry above never
yields" cause of a never-fired entry, arrived at deliberately.

**WHICH BUILD WOULD WANT IT**: one whose core spell does not depend on a ramp a
3.5 second cast interrupts. Nothing here is that build, and the day a Mage
profile exists without Arcane Blast it is one line to re-measure --
`PRESET=mage_arcane VARIANTS=arcaneWrath npx vite-node tools/probe_build.ts`.

### Winter's Chill: the flag is what got it answered

`AuraDefinition.additiveDamageStacks` is new and this aura is its only caller.
The engine's default raises a damage multiplier to the POWER of the stack count
-- which is right for Improved Scorch's Fire Vulnerability, where 3% a stack
makes the two readings 1.159 against 1.150 and nobody would notice. At **40% a
stack they are a factor of 1.8 apart**, so the convention's existing callers
were no evidence about this one, and the assertion carried both figures with the
alternative pinned as a negative. The answer came back in one line.

**IMPROVED SCORCH KEEPS ITS 1.159**, because a ruling covers what it says and is
not extended by analogy -- `additiveDamageStacks` defaults to false for exactly
that reason. Both are worth nothing today: Cone of Cold is not declared and no
Mage profile takes the talent.

## Round EIGHTEEN: two owner rulings, and the questions that earned them

**BOTH OF THESE WERE ROWS IN THE TABLE ABOVE**, raised by round seventeen and
deliberately not decided. That is the whole method: a reading flagged as a
reading is what gets ruled on, and both answers arrived in one message.

| | the ruling | what it moved |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | "All Tauren race profiles need to have their templates gear sets tweaked to gain **+10 hit rating**, which should be subtracted from elsewhere on the template set without violating the item budget information which this project knows" | five template rows, and the five builds go back on their hit caps |
| 2 | "**Moonkin and Leader of the Pack are no longer exclusive.** And all profiles should have both enabled by default in the raid buffs" | every profile that casts anything gains 3% spell crit |

### 1. The Tauren hit cap, and why the second half of the instruction was free

Tauren Endurance was "Total Health increased by 5% and **chance to hit increased
by 1%**" and the patch made the second clause crit damage. The owner's benchmark
templates had been CREDITING that 1%:

| | asked for | its siblings ask | now |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Prot Warr | 70 | 80 | **80** |
| Enh Shaman | 70 | 80 | **80** |
| Cat | 30 | -- (4 points come from Nature's Reach) | **40** |
| Bear | 30 | -- (the same 4) | **40** |
| Moonkin | 110 | -- (the same 4, against a 16-point spell cap) | **120** |

**NOTHING ELSE ABOUT THE GENERATOR CHANGED, WHICH IS THE PART WORTH KNOWING.**
A rating already costs budget and is already paid for out of CRIT first, so
"subtracted from elsewhere without violating the budget" is what `spreadRating`
and `RATING_DONORS` already do -- the owner's own donor rule answering their own
second clause. `benchmarkGear.test.ts`, which asserts every generated item fits
its budget and leaves nothing in `unmodelled`, passed untouched.

**WHERE THE POINT CAME FROM SPLITS ON WHETHER THE LIST BUYS CRIT**, read off
`tools/benchmark_stat_report.ts`:

| | crit | and | the other 23 rows |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Cat | 108 -> **106** | attack power 2207 -> 2196 | |
| Enh Shaman | 176 -> **174** | attack power 1974 -> 1965 | |
| Moonkin | 154 -> **152** | spell power 774 -> 769 | |
| Prot Warr | 47 -> 47 | stamina 294 -> **292** | |
| Bear | 42 -> 42 | stamina 288 -> **285** | **0.0, every column** |

The two TANKS have no Crit in `preferred` at all, so there is no donor to drain
and the overflow rule pays out of the base attributes instead. That is what
`toLines` already documents as the behaviour on a slot carrying no crit, and it
is why two of the five look like they cost nothing.

**THE MOONKIN IS THE SHARPEST CASE AND THE SPELL FLOOR IS WHY.** Spells miss on
a flat 17% floored at 1%, so its last point was unusually valuable: 120 rating
plus Nature's Reach is 16 points, back ON the floor at 1% miss where 15 points
was 2% -- a DOUBLING rather than a point. That figure has now moved three times
without the rule changing (10/7%, 16/1%, 15/2%, 16/1%), and
`druidHitAndDotApplication.test.ts` records all four readings above the
assertion.

### 2. The two Druid auras stop excluding each other

The exclusivity was declared when the two were the same 3% global crit. A patch
split them by attack TYPE -- Moonkin Aura spell crit only, Leader of the Pack
melee and ranged only -- and **three sites were left pinning the declaration
with the question written on them**, because removing it is a raid COMPOSITION
decision that moves every profile's figure.

**SO `moonkin_form` JOINS `PRESET_RAID_BUFFS` AND THE MOONKIN'S SWAP IS GONE.**
It ran the one raid-buff list in the project that substituted an entry for this
pair; `MOONKIN_RAID_BUFFS` is deleted and it runs the shared raid like everybody
else. Two swaps remain, both the ranged Hunters' Grace of Air, and
`presets.test.ts` asserts that exception list is EXHAUSTIVE -- which is what made
this a one-line change that could not be half-applied.

**THE RULING COST NOTHING TO APPLY BECAUSE THE CHOOSER WAS NEVER WHAT WORKED.**
The older ruling was "they don't stack, but that can be handled on the GUI", and
`withRaidBuff` did switch one off -- which was only ever half the surface: it
knows nothing about a TALENT, and nothing about a profile loaded from JSON with
both ids. That is what let a Moonkin read **+6% crit**, measured at 24.243%
against 21.243%. The no-double-dip rule has lived in the shared aura ID ever
since, so there was no exclusivity in a chooser left to remove -- and **the
Moonkin's own spell crit does not move** when the box is ticked, because its
talent and the raid entry are one id.

**`RaidBuff.exclusiveWith` NOW HAS NO DECLARATION ANYWHERE.** The field,
`areExclusive` and the panel's "replaces X" line are kept -- the next exclusive
pair should be one line rather than a re-implementation, and `withRaidBuff` is
still load-bearing for the catalogue ordering that makes two identical
selections one file. `raidBuffs.test.ts` asserts `areExclusive` answers false
for **every pair in the catalogue**, which is the third instance of that guard
after `isWeaponUseOf(attack, 'ranged')` and `RacialRequirement.weaponTypes`, and
the first where the thing with no caller is a RULE rather than a predicate.

### What was NOT measured, and one tool that could not answer it

**NO PROFILE WAS RE-BASELINED**, on the owner's standing instruction. Every
figure above is a stat off the built character or a row of
`benchmark_stat_report.ts`; the DPS consequences of both rulings are the owner's
to measure. Ten tests changed and six asserted the OPPOSITE of what now holds,
each re-derived rather than adjusted; the `statHeadroom` inversion was probed by
reverting the Cat row, which fails on exactly that build.

**AND `rotation_fingerprint.ts` WAS THE WRONG TOOL, WHICH IS WORTH RECORDING.**
It hashes the combat LOG, so selecting one more raid buff on all 25 presets
moved **all 84 hashes** -- an aura applied at the pull writes a line and
consumes no random number, so a pure melee build whose new buff grants
`spellCritChance` runs an identical fight and still reports a different hash.
`eventsProcessed` is no substitute either: it was unchanged on 20 of 28 profiles
and a fight can diverge completely while processing the same number of events.
**A hash answers "is this log byte-identical", which is the same question as
"did any decision change" only for a change that cannot write a line.** Where a
change legitimately touches a stat, the containment check is the MECHANISM --
which stat the aura moves, asserted on the stat, which is what
`raidBuffs.test.ts` and `druidTalents.test.ts` now do in both directions.

## Round EIGHTEEN b: Ice Lance, where the ruling covered the PRODUCT

> Ice Lance was changed to use 1.5/3.5/3.

**THE THIRD RULING ON ONE CONSTANT, AND THE SECOND THAT A FLAGGED READING
PRODUCED.** It arrived unprompted, minutes after round 18 merged, against a row
in the table above.

### What the three rulings were, in order

| | | the frozen product |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | "Ice lance now has a **1.5/3.5/4** spell power coefficient instead of 1.5/3.5" -- and the frozen multiplier was 4 | `1.5/3.5/4 * 4` = **0.4286** |
| 2 | asked whether the `/4` had already done the frozen division: **"This is intended stop asking."** | unchanged, and now ratified |
| 3 | a patch: "Ice Lance damage against Frozen targets changed to a 200% increase (Was 300%)", so the multiplier is 3 and the note says nothing about the coefficient | `1.5/3.5/4 * 3` = **0.3214** |
| 4 | **"Ice Lance was changed to use 1.5/3.5/3"** | `1.5/3.5/3 * 3` = **0.4286** again |

So the coefficient is `1.5 / 3.5 / 3` = 0.1429 unfrozen, the frozen case is
exactly `1.5 / 3.5`, and the figure the spell has had frozen since the owner
first touched it never actually moved. **The divisor tracks the multiplier**, and
"intended" in step 2 covered the PRODUCT rather than the two factors.

### The implemented reading was wrong for one release, and the rule that chose it is still right

Step 3 left two readings a THIRD apart. The one implemented was that the `/4` is
a separate settled fact, on this project's own rule that **a statement of a
VALUE does not overturn a separate ruling about which terms a multiplier
reaches** -- which is the rule that settled these same two numbers correctly in
step 1, and is still the right default.

**IT WAS WRONG ON THIS INSTANCE AND THE LABEL IS WHAT COST ONE MESSAGE INSTEAD
OF A RELEASE.** The reading was written down as a reading, in three places, with
both figures and what each would cost -- and `iceLance.test.ts` pinned the
alternative as a **negative assertion**, `not.toBeCloseTo(1.5 / 3.5)`, with a
note saying that whoever restored the symmetry would have to change that line
and what they should read first. The person who could settle it read the note.

**SO: IMPLEMENT THE DEFAULT AND FLAG THE INSTANCE.** A good general rule having
answered a question is not the same as the question being closed, and treating
it that way is what would have buried this one.

### What changed, and the two constants stay two

`ICE_LANCE_SP_COEFFICIENT` is `1.5 / 3.5 / 3`. Six of the eight tests in
`iceLance.test.ts` moved, including every one that measures damage that landed;
probed by setting the divisor back to 4, which fails all six.

**THE TWO THREES ARE NOT DERIVED FROM EACH OTHER**, which is worth saying while
they agree, because the last time they diverged it was a patch doing it.
Writing `1.5 / 3.5 / ICE_LANCE_FROZEN_MULTIPLIER` would move the coefficient on
nobody's authority next time -- and `game/combat` cannot import `game/abilities`
anyway. Each is asserted against its own stated value and neither against the
other, which is `critRageMultiplierFor`'s arrangement: **pin by IDENTITY rather
than by equality**, or the next divergence fails in the constant that did not
move.

**THE GUARD IS STILL THE END-TO-END PRODUCT.** The two wrong readings of the
pair -- the multiplier applied twice, or folded into the divisor and not applied
at all -- are NINE apart on a frozen cast and both produce a plausible number,
so every assertion here measures damage and divides rather than checking a
constant.

### What it is worth, and the share figure the question row got wrong

**THE FROZEN COEFFICIENT GOES 0.3214 TO 0.4286**, a third more spell power
scaling. The flat half is untouched.

**AND IT REACHES ONE PROFILE, BECAUSE ICE LANCE IS IN ONE LIST.** `USES=1
SEEDS=3` on the three Mages:

| | Ice Lance | |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Frostfire** | **3.3 casts a fight, 8.6%** of its damage | the only entry in the project |
| Arcane | not in the list | |
| Fire | not in the list | |

**THE QUESTION ROW SAID "35.7% of Frostfire" AND THAT FIGURE WAS THREE CHANGES
STALE.** 35.7% is what Ice Lance was worth BEFORE the owner quartered its
coefficient -- the share that made the spell worth looking at in the first
place. It fell to 20.6% on that change and is 8.6% now, after the Heating Up
ruling and two rounds of list work moved everything around it. **A share quoted
to justify asking a question is a dated claim like any other**, and it was
carried forward through two rewrites of that row without being re-read.

**EVERY CAST IS A FROZEN ONE**, which is the sharpest consequence and is a fact
about the LIST rather than the spell: the entry is gated on
`selfHasStack('fingers_of_frost')`, so the unfrozen coefficient is worth
literally nothing to any profile and the frozen product is the whole of it.

**SO WHAT THIS RULING ACTUALLY DOES IS SPLIT THE FROZEN NERF IN HALF.** Before
the frozen patch, a frozen Ice Lance was `0.4286` of spell power and `145 x 4`
of base. The patch took the multiplier to 3, which cut BOTH -- and the owner's
answer puts the coefficient back where it was while leaving the base at `145 x
3`. **The frozen bonus fell for the flat damage and not for the scaling**,
which is what "the divisor tracks the multiplier" means in damage terms and is
not something either reading of the note said out loud.

**NOT RE-BASELINED**, on the owner's standing instruction. The DPS is yours:
Frostfire read 906.6 +/- 61.9 over 30 fights after the change, which is a
diagnostic and not a baseline -- the published table is 300 fights a profile and
three rounds behind.

## Round NINETEEN: Before the Pull, for all 28 profiles

**THE FEATURE SHIPPED WITH NO CALLER AND THIS IS WHERE IT WAS ACTUALLY TESTED.**
`precombat` arrived with the mechanism, the panel, the migration and a test
whose own comment read "no preset carries a pre-combat list, so the hook returns
immediately". The owner has now specified an opener for every profile in one
message, and implementing it found **one engine incoherence, two impossible
lines, one silently-refused ordering and one reporting hole**.

### The one engine change: `secondsBefore` is when the action STARTED

The owner drew the distinction in their own wording -- an instant is "3s before
Battle Stance", a cast is "**2.5s before start casting** Frostfire Bolt" -- and
**every one of the ten "start casting" leads is exactly that spell's cast time
on that build**:

| | | | |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Wrath | 1.5 | Aimed Shot | 2 |
| Arcane Blast | 1.5 | Incinerate | 2 |
| Mind Blast | 1.5 | Frostfire Bolt | 2.5 |
| Lightning Bolt | 2 | Fireball | 3 |
| **Sniper Shot** | **4** | **Shadow Bolt** | **2.5, which is 3.0 MINUS BANE** |

The last two are what make it a reading rather than a coincidence: neither is a
figure anybody would pick out of the air, and the second is not the number on
the ability at all -- it is the talent-reduced cast time of that one build.

**SO `runPrecombat` AGES BY THE LEAD MINUS THE CAST TIME**, clamped at zero.
Without it a pre-cast spell contradicted itself -- the damage landed at t=0
while everything the same cast applied was already aged:

| | what the old reading gave it |
| --- | --- |
| Fireball at 3s | its DoT 3 seconds into an 8 second burn at the pull |
| Frostfire Bolt at 2.5s | the same, on 62% of that profile's damage |
| Arcane Blast at 1.5s | its 15 second stacking window already a tenth gone |
| Aimed Shot at 2s | a 6 second cooldown back at t=4 rather than t=6 |
| Sniper Shot at 4s | a 15 second cooldown back at t=11 |

**THE CLAMP IS A CASE AND NOT A GUARD.** The owner's ORIGINAL example, from when
the feature was designed, was "Cast Fireball <= 2.5 sec before combat initiates"
-- a 2.5 second lead on a three second cast, which cannot have finished and
therefore finishes at the pull. `precombat.test.ts` has asserted that since the
feature shipped and it still passes untouched.

### The owner corrected four readings, and every line is implemented now

**THE FIRST PASS LEFT THREE THINGS OUT AND GOT THE PHRASING CONFIRMED.** Their
reply is the clearest statement of the method this round produced: a flagged
reading gets answered, an omission does not.

| | the first reading | the owner |
| --- | --- | --- |
| the LEAD TIME | "N seconds before" is when the action STARTED, so a cast finishing at the pull ages by nothing | **confirmed**: "'3s before Battle Stance' means t-minus 3 seconds from combat starting ... everything listed is meant to be a set sequence to properly initiate combat at 0s with all non-combat skills already pre-fired" |
| the Cat's **RAVAGE** | unimplementable: "must be prowling AND behind the target" is `stealth` plus `positioning`, two `OutOfScope` members | **"You do NOT need to scope in behind the target positioning. Once again this should be assumed in all cases EXCEPT when 'Target attacks back' is checked."** Plus the whole ability, stated |
| the melee Hunters' **MONGOOSE BITE** | impossible before the pull -- its dodge window needs a landed attack | **"good catch. Replace Mongoose Bite with Strider kick instead for those pre-combat instances."** |
| the SEVEN state lines | omitted, because `runPrecombat` skips them in silence | **"Even if lines were already true, like Moonkin Form, just leave it in the pre-combat so users know that."** |

### The positioning ruling overturned four caveats at once

Shred, Backstab and Ambush each carried an `unmodelled` entry saying the
position "is dropped ... and is not pretended to be met", and the Cat's own list
called the drop "generous to the build".

**THE BEHAVIOUR NEVER CHANGED AND THE NOTES WERE WRONG.** All three always dealt
their damage; what the caveats did was tell a reader the build was getting
something it had not earned, while three `unmodelled` entries inflated the gap
census with a decision. **A caveat that apologises for the engine can be wrong
about the RULESET**, and nothing in the code contradicts it.

**THE EXCEPTION IS NOT BUILT, BECAUSE NOTHING REACHES IT.** A character whose
target swings back stands in FRONT of it -- that is why the encounter grants the
target 14% parry -- so a behind-the-target ability should be refused on such a
build. No engine field carries `targetAttacks`, and every profile that can cast
one of these sets it false, so a gate would be unreachable.
`positioning.test.ts` asserts the invariant instead, and **the day a tank build
takes Shred that test fails and the gate becomes worth building.**

### Prowl and Ravage, the two new Druid abilities

The owner supplied Ravage outright -- "60 energy, Instant cast ... 350% damage
plus 343 ... Must be prowling and behind the target. Awards 1 combo points" --
and **every figure agrees with `forever-druid-spellbook.json` at rank 4, word
for word**. So the numbers were never what could be wrong; the SHAPE was:

| | |
| --- | --- |
| the 350% | a WEAPON FRACTION, with the 343 OUTSIDE it -- Shred's shape one row up, and the owner's own flat-damage ruling |
| the gate | the `PROWL` aura, which is Ambush's arrangement: a permanent aura whose only reader is the opener it unlocks |
| the SPEND | **the one clause neither source states.** Ambush is the precedent and removes the aura gating it. Without it a Cat holding Prowl could Ravage whenever the energy allowed -- a repeatable 350% instant, which is a very large invented number. Labelled as a reading, and one line to flip |

**PROWL IS A REAL CAST AND THE FORM ABOVE IT IS NOT**, which is the distinction
the opener makes visible: it is declared, free, on a ten second cooldown, and at
the pull that cooldown reads **nine seconds** -- the lead rewound, which is the
half of `runPrecombat` a reader is least likely to expect from the panel.

### A periodic that accrues before the pull

**BLOODRAGE AT A TEN SECOND LEAD WAS TEN RAGE AND SHOULD BE TWENTY.** The owner:
"you get 10 rage instantly, and then another 1 rage per second for 10 seconds
... **You end up with exactly 20 rage generated from Bloodrage after 10
seconds**." All three Warrior openers press it at exactly its duration, so the
whole over-time half elapsed before the pull -- and the aura was aged past its
own end and removed with that half undelivered.

`PeriodicEffect.accruesBeforeCombat` is the fix, and it is **declared rather
than derived because the engine cannot tell the two kinds of periodic apart**:
both are an `onTick` closure and what it does is decided inside. A resource
granted to the CARRIER is accrual; damage dealt to a TARGET would be damage in a
fight that had not started. The default loses the elapsed ticks, which is what
every DoT wants, and Bloodrage is the one caller.

**IT DOES NOT DOUBLE-COUNT AT A SHORTER LEAD**, which is the half to check
rather than reason about: the ticks already scheduled keep firing after the pull
and stop when the AGED window closes, so a four-second lead is four before and
six after. Probed by switching the flag off -- **10 against 20**, exactly the
two halves.

### And the seven state lines are listed rather than omitted

"2s before Stealth" on three Rogues, and "Cat Form", "Moonkin Form" and "Dire
Bear Form". None is an ability: a Druid's form is a COMBAT STYLE and a Rogue's
Stealth is an `openingAura`, and `onPrecombat` runs AFTER that loop, so each is
already true when the opener runs.

**THE ARGUMENT FOR OMITTING THEM WAS TRUE OF THE ENGINE AND BESIDE THE POINT.**
A pre-combat list is also the thing a person reads to see how the fight opens,
and a missing step reads as a step not taken. So `PRECOMBAT_STATES` carries the
seven with what makes each one true, and the two readers that would otherwise
misreport them use it: the APL panel, which labelled them "(not in this build)"
-- exactly backwards on the row somebody is checking -- and the opener guard,
which asserts every action FIRES.

**IT IS NOT A WAY TO DECLARE SOMETHING INERT.** Each entry reads the PROFILE, so
a Moonkin naming `cat_form` is still wrong, and the guard drives that both ways.

### The ordering inside one lead time is load-bearing

**BATTLE SHOUT COSTS 10 RAGE AND A WARRIOR OPENS ON AN EMPTY BAR.** Bloodrage's
instant grant is 10 -- 15 with Improved Bloodrage -- so a shout written before
its Bloodrage is refused and skipped. The owner's own openers put the two in the
right order at one lead time; **three hand-built test fixtures did not**, and
each produced a missing Battle Shout row rather than an error.
`Array.prototype.sort` being stable is the whole mechanism that keeps two
actions at the same lead in declaration order, and that is now asserted.

### What the three Warrior lists lost, and who else was using them

The owner's instruction was per PROFILE -- "Charge, Battle Stance, and Battle
Shout should be removed from the normal APL" -- and **none of the three fired on
those presets anyway**: Charge is castable for one instant, the preset already
opens in its stance, and `battle_shout` is in `PRESET_RAID_BUFFS` so the shout's
own condition refused it all fight. So the lists are shorter and the characters
do the same things.

**BUT A PROFILE-SPECIFIC LIST IS ALSO WHAT ANY HAND-BUILT CHARACTER OF THAT
STYLE AND STANCE RUNS**, and such a character has no raid buff and no opener --
so it now gets none of the three unless it declares them. **The two GENERIC
Warrior lists keep theirs**, which is the right scope and is why they were left
alone. Four test fixtures had to be given openers before their own assertions
about Battle Shout meant anything again.

**AND `chargeAtThePull` IS GONE WHILE ITS BUILTIN STAYS.** The entry carried a
`charge_stance_allowed` condition, because `PriorityRotation` would otherwise
have cast a stance change to unblock it and dragged the tank into Battle Stance
at the pull. A pre-combat cast has no rotation attached, so `castAbility`'s
ordinary stance check is the whole of it -- and VANGUARD still gates it with no
talent named, by adding Defensive to that character's own copy of Charge.
**The builtin may not be deleted**: a stored list is written out IN FULL on
every saved profile and `compileCondition` THROWS on an id it does not know, so
removing it would break every profile saved before this change, at the moment
its fight starts. `aplFormat.test.ts` carries that as a one-id exception with
the list asserted exhaustive.

### And the reporting half, which this change created and then closed

**A PRECOMBAT ACTION WAS INVISIBLE TO `USES=1`.** It is not a list entry, so the
uses loop never reaches it, and the "not in the list, and still dealing damage"
block filters on `damage > 0` -- which a shout and a stance never have. So three
actions a fight vanished from the report the day they moved, which is this
project's own "an ability in the book, in no list, dealing no damage was
invisible to everything" with a new way in.

`measure_profiles.ts` prints a **"before the pull"** block now, oldest action
first, with a `<-- NEVER HAPPENED` flag -- because a zero there is the finding
for exactly the reason a zero in the list is. The Protection warrior reads:

    -- before the pull --
        bloodrage_cast            10.0s before    1.8 uses
        battle_shout_cast         10.0s before    1.0 uses
        defensive_stance_cast      3.0s before    1.0 uses
        charge                     0.0s before    1.0 uses
        mighty_rage_potion         0.0s before    1.0 uses
        heroic_strike              0.0s before   19.5 uses

The uses figure is POOLED with the fight's own casts, which is the caveat the
list rows already carry and reads oddly here: `heroic_strike` shows 19.5 beside
"0.0s before" because the damage table is keyed by ABILITY and not by where the
cast came from. What the column answers is "did this happen at all".

### What was not measured

**NO PROFILE WAS RE-BASELINED**, on the owner's standing instruction, and this
round would have needed a full one: 26 of the 28 profiles now take actions they
did not take before. The assurance here is the guard rather than a figure --
every declared action is asserted to fire at the pull, on all 28, in a real
fight.

**WHAT IS KNOWN DIRECTIONALLY** is that an opener is not free: it spends mana and
rage, it puts its own cooldowns on the clock with the lead time rewound, and the
0-second actions are off the global cooldown by design. A Bear's "Enrage +
Mighty Rage Potion + queue Maul + Primal Bite" is four actions in the instant a
priority list could spend one.

**AND THE ONE THING FLAGGED FOR THE OWNER CAME BACK ANSWERED**, which is this
round's own method working: the note read "Bloodrage grants 10 rage instantly
AND ten more over ten seconds, and at a 10-second lead the over-time half is
LOST ... `runPrecombat` cannot deliver a periodic effect's accrued resource, and
shortening the lead is the owner's call rather than a fix to make here."

They neither shortened the lead nor left it: they stated the total. "You end up
with exactly 20 rage generated from Bloodrage after 10 seconds." So the fix was
the engine's after all -- see **A periodic that accrues before the pull** above.
**Flagging it was what produced the figure that settled it**, and the figure
ruled out the option the note had offered.

## Round TWENTY: the raid is full

**THE OWNER'S INSTRUCTION, IN ONE SENTENCE:** "For all profiles ensure that all
raid buffs are checked on except for Curse of Recklessness." With a second:
"ensure that Cat Druid and Moonkin are not double-dipping from Leader of the
Pack and Moonkin Aura."

**NOT THE ROUND 20 THAT WAS SCOPED.** The client refresh is still blocked on the
six respecified spreads; this is the raid selection, which needed none of them.

### What was missing

Nineteen of the catalogue's twenty-three were selected. The three that joined are
**Judgement of the Crusader**, **Gift of Arthas** and **Grace of Air Totem**;
Curse of Recklessness stays off, which is the one the owner named.

### Both per-profile exceptions are gone, for two different reasons

| | why it can go |
| --- | --- |
| the two ranged Hunters' **swap** of Grace of Air for Windfury | both are selected now, so there is nothing to substitute. Windfury reaches no bow, and **a ticked box is allowed to be worth nothing** |
| the Enhancement Shaman's **drop** of Windfury Totem | its own imbue "disables any benefit you personally receive from Windfury Totem" -- which is a rule about a CHARACTER, and is enforced in the ENGINE now |

**THE SECOND WAS IN THE WRONG PLACE ALL ALONG, AND IT IS THE MOONKIN MISTAKE
AGAIN.** A selection cannot enforce something a user can re-tick -- which is
exactly how a Moonkin came to read +6% crit when its exclusivity lived in a
chooser and a talent reached the same aura without passing through it.
`windfuryTotemReaction`'s `canTrigger` refuses while the imbue is up.

**ASKED AT PROC TIME AND NOT AT BUILD TIME**, because the imbue arrives as a
pre-combat CAST and `onPrecombat` runs after `openingAuras` -- a build-time check
would have found no aura. It also covers a mid-fight re-imbue, which the
Enhancement list does when the hour expires.

### The double-dip check: not happening, and measured

| | removing both party buffs |
| --- | --- |
| **Cat Druid** | melee crit **0.000** change -- its own Leader of the Pack talent already supplies it |
| **Moonkin** | spell crit **0.000** change -- its own Moonkin Form talent already supplies it |
| Bear | melee **0.000**, same as the Cat |

Each Druid loses only the half it has no talent for -- the Cat gives up 3% SPELL
crit and the Moonkin 3% MELEE -- which is the aura split working exactly as the
patch that separated them intended. Every profile holds each aura at **one
stack**. `tools/probe_party_crit.ts` is the check, kept as a command.

### What it moved: everything, and the split is the finding

| | n | mean | what reaches them |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **the three Paladins** | 3 | **+114.1** | Judgement of the Crusader's 161 Holy spell power |
| physical, non-tank | 14 | +50.5 | Grace of Air's agility, Gift of Arthas' +8 a hit |
| tanks | 3 | +43.6 | the Prot Paladin is +82.3 of that, for the Holy reason above |
| casters | 8 | **+9.1** | Gift of Arthas alone: no agility read, no Holy dealt |

**Mean 798.8 -> 843.5, twenty-eight rows up and none down.**

**JUDGEMENT OF THE CRUSADER IS ISOLATED RATHER THAN BLAMED**, which is this
project's own rule about the obvious suspect: +79.5, +74.9, +63.0 and +49.4 on
the four Paladins, and **exactly 0.0 on DW Fury and the Fire Mage**. The two
zeros are the control -- it is Holy spell power and cannot reach a build that
deals none.

### The one question it raised, now answered

**TWO JUDGEMENTS ARE SELECTED AT ONCE, AND IT IS INTENDED.** Wisdom and the
Crusader, which one target cannot carry in the game being modelled;
`exclusiveWith` has no declaration anywhere, so nothing refuses it. Implemented
as instructed and recorded as a question, because it is worth 63 to 80 DPS on
three profiles and is a raid COMPOSITION ruling rather than a defect.

**THE OWNER'S ANSWER: "Two Judgements is absolutely fine and intended for the
raid buff contribution."** So the box is a raid CONTRIBUTION rather than a claim
about one debuff slot, and `raidBuffs.test.ts` asserts both ids on all 28 presets
-- the question is closed and pinned, because a ruling left as prose gets
re-raised.

## Round NINETEEN e: four rulings, and two were already done

**ASKED TO CONFIRM THE SHAMAN, MAGE AND PRIEST TREE CHANGES WERE IMPLEMENTED.**
They are not -- see the tree-diff round -- but four of the semantic changes
inside them could be settled without the blocked refresh, because they are
ABILITIES and talent EFFECTS rather than tree shape.

### Two needed nothing, and I should have checked before asking

| | |
| --- | --- |
| **Arcane Blast** | fully implemented already: `ARCANE_BLAST_INTELLECT_TO_SPELL_POWER` 0.15, a 15-second window with `refreshBehaviour: 'ignore'`, four stacks, the spender deleted, and the damage already cut 40% -- which is exactly the client's rank-1 57-65 becoming 34-38 |
| **the Improved Mind Flay ramp** | `MIND_FLAY_ESCALATION_BONUS` compounds 20% a tick, and its own comment records the 1.2133 over three ticks that the new wording describes |

**CLAUDE.md SAID SO AND I RAISED THEM AS QUESTIONS ANYWAY.** Its Arcane Blast
entry ends "the question was answered by being removed" and names the deleted
spender. **A file that records an answer is only useful if it is read before the
question is re-asked**, which is this project's own rule about a reason that
describes a working half -- pointed at the notes rather than at a declaration.

### Shadow Weaving crosses to the target

The owner: "Shadow Weaving is now a debuff, where the target takes more shadow
damage from me." It was a whole-character `damageDoneMultiplier` on the CASTER
with a caveat admitting the reach -- "a Shadow priest deals almost nothing but
Shadow, so the two readings agree for this build."

**THAT CAVEAT IS THE SHAPE THIS PROJECT HAS BEEN CAUGHT BY TWICE** on Demonic
Sacrifice and Shadow and Flame: a claim about a PROFILE rather than about the
effect. It is `damageTakenBySchool` now, which is Fire Vulnerability's
arrangement one file over.

**AND IT REPORTED ON NEITHER TABLE UNTIL IT DECLARED `isDebuff`.**
`AuraInstance.isDebuff` reads the DEFINITION rather than asking who holds the
aura -- so the moved aura applied, stacked and multiplied the damage correctly
and showed up in no uptime table at all: `buffUptime` reads friendly actors and
`debuffUptime` filters on the flag. **The reporting split is the only reason it
was caught**, because the old test looked in `buffUptime` and failed where one
combined table would have passed on the wrong side.

**AND THE TEST ASSERTED THE OPPOSITE IN ITS OWN NAME** -- "stacks Shadow Weaving
on the CASTER, which is a Forever change" -- which is `rogueAbilities.test.ts`'
lesson again: a test named for what the project believes enforces that belief
until the belief is wrong. CLAUDE.md's "Shadow Weaving is on the CASTER in
Forever and on the target in Classic" is corrected with it, and **it expired by
Forever moving TOWARD Classic**, which is not the direction this file usually
has to worry about.

### Mind Flay keeps two of its three ticks

The owner: "preserves a minimum of the first 2 ticks ... and thus only cuts off
the last tick when interrupted. **This may no longer be optimal play, but I
still want the functionality so the user can choose.**"

`Ability.protectedChannelTicks`, read on ticks DELIVERED rather than on elapsed
time -- a channel is hasted, so only the count is stable.

**DECLARED RATHER THAN DERIVED, WHICH INVERTS THE USUAL PREFERENCE ON PURPOSE.**
`channelTicks - 1` is the tempting general rule and ARCANE MISSILES is the only
other interruptible channel here, at five ticks: deriving it would make its first
FOUR un-interruptible on nobody's authority.

**THE TWO PRIEST RULINGS PULL AGAINST EACH OTHER.** The ramp compounds 20% a
tick, so the three ticks are 1, 1.2 and 1.44 of the base -- and the tick an
interrupt may now take is the BIGGEST of them, **39.6% of the channel rather
than a third**. The owner called the cost in the same sentence they asked for it.

### What it moved: nothing at all

**28 of 28 rows BIT-IDENTICAL.** Shadow Weaving is worth the same to a build that
deals only Shadow damage whichever side holds it, and `applyAura` draws no random
number -- so the fights are the same fights. The interrupt floor is unreachable
on every stock list, because only the Warlock's three entries interrupt anything.

**THAT IS THE CONTAINMENT CHECK AND IT IS ALSO THE POINT**: the rulings are now
right, and the published table does not move.

## Round NINETEEN d: the lead time buys regeneration

**ASKED, THEN RULED.** The question was whether natural regeneration happens
during the pre-combat phase -- it did not, because `Simulation.begin()` calls
`onPrecombat` and only then `startResourceRegeneration`, and every pre-combat
action resolves at timestamp zero. The owner: **"yes, credit the regen and
back-date the five-second rule."**

**IT MAKES THE LEAD'S OBSERVABLE CONSEQUENCES FOUR.** Their original ruling
enumerated exactly TWO -- "an aura it applied has N seconds less left at the
pull, and its own cooldown comes back N seconds sooner" -- and
`accruesBeforeCombat` made it three for Bloodrage's rage. **So a list of
consequences in this project is open rather than closed**, which is the thing to
carry forward from this round more than the rule itself.

### What it is

`runPrecombat` keeps a NEGATIVE local offset and walks the window: credit each
pool for the gap before every action, cast it, stamp its spend at the moment it
happened, and credit the final gap to the pull.

**THE CLOCK IS NOT MOVED AND CANNOT BE.** `SimulationClock` only goes forward
and every scheduled event, swing timer and aura window is expressed against it
-- the reason this whole feature resolves at timestamp zero. So
`ResourceRegen.amountPerTick` takes an optional `at`, and **each tick is
evaluated at its own moment**.

**THAT IS THE RULE, AND `rate x seconds` WOULD HAVE GOT IT WRONG.** A single
multiplication hands a caster back every point its own lockout should have
suppressed. Asking per tick is what makes the two halves of the ruling compose
instead of cancel.

**AND THE STAMP IS A PLAIN NUMBER, SO A NEGATIVE ONE IS ARITHMETIC RATHER THAN A
SPECIAL CASE.** `spentWithin` asks `now - last < windowMs`: a spend at -4500
reads as 4.5 seconds ago at the pull, and the lockout expires 500ms into the
fight by itself with nothing to clear. Before this it sat at ZERO, so a Mage
that drank Mage Armor 4.5 seconds early arrived with the full five seconds still
to run.

**THE BOUNDARY TICK IS FREE**, which is worth recording because the test got it
wrong first: the comparison is STRICT, so the tick at exactly five seconds after
a spend is already unlocked. A six-second lead credits **21** ticks of its
one-second tail, not 20 -- the draft expected 9,100 and the engine said 9,105.

### The two halves fight each other, on purpose

| | |
| --- | --- |
| **energy** | no lockout, so a Rogue banks the lot. Blade Flurry's one-second lead is **ten energy**, and the two Combat Rogues open on **85 rather than 75** |
| **mana** | the back-dated lockout suppresses almost exactly what the lead would have bought. Fire spends 900 at leads of 4.5s and 3s and gets **91** back |

**SO A CASTER'S CREDIT BEING NEARLY ZERO IS THE RULING WORKING RATHER THAN
FAILING**, and it is why both halves had to land in one commit: crediting alone
would have been a quiet buff to every caster in the project.

**TEN A SECOND AND NOT TWENTY.** `ENERGY_PER_SECOND` is `0.5 x 20` -- half an
energy twenty times a second -- because the literal reading of the owner's "1
energy 20 times a second" was built, measured at +35% to +53% on every energy
build, and withdrawn. **The figure was quoted as twenty when this question was
first answered and corrected off the constant**: the CADENCE is twenty and the
RATE is ten.

### What it moved: almost nothing, and the reason is the useful part

| | |
| --- | --- |
| **bit-identical** | **23 of 28** |
| moved | Combat Mut **-5.09**, Combat **+0.71**, Arcane +0.24, Fire -0.17, Frostfire +0.10 |
| mean | 798.91 -> **798.76** |

Every one of the five is inside its own interval, and the two Combat Rogues
move in OPPOSITE directions on the same +10 energy -- one changed decision
reorders the whole random stream, so that is noise rather than a finding.

**A POOL GAIN ONLY MOVES A FIGURE IF IT CROSSES A CAST'S COST AT A DECISION
POINT.** That is what explains 23 identical rows on a change that moves what 22
profiles arrive with: BM Hunter gains **63 mana** and runs a bit-identical
fight, because 63 does not buy another Aimed Shot, so no decision anywhere
differs. Twelve more profiles gain mana the lockout then makes irrelevant.

**AND NO TEST BROKE**, which is the first round in several where that is true --
3,728 passing, against four repairs last round.

`tools/probe_precombat_pools.ts` prints the pool and the lockout remainder per
profile, because neither quantity has a zero anywhere to notice: **a pool
arriving short reads exactly like a pool that was meant to be short.**

## Round NINETEEN c: a free cast acts again at once

**THE OWNER, ANSWERING 19b'S ONE QUESTION IN ELEVEN WORDS**: "Vanish into Ambush
should not be interrupted by a swing."

### What was wrong, and why no list could fix it

Vanish is off the global cooldown, so it leaves the actor FREE -- and
`nextDecisionTime` gave a free actor `now + ROTATION_POLL_MS`. A tenth of a
second in which an auto-attack could land and take the stealth window the Vanish
had just opened.

**IT IS THE CHARGE PROBLEM AND THIS IS THE SECOND THING IT HAS COST.** CLAUDE.md
already recorded the first: Charge's `canCast` is true for one instant, the
off-GCD cast left the actor free, and "the window is gone" -- one cast a fight to
ZERO. **That one was worked around by MOVING THE ENTRY to second**, which works
when the thing stealing the gap is another entry. Here it is a SWING, and no
ordering outranks a swing.

### Two designs that could not express it, both tried first

| | why not |
| --- | --- |
| `AplEntry.sequence` | it steps ONE cast per decision, so the poll still sits between the steps -- and `resumeSequence` then ABANDONS the pointer, because the refusal is the stealth gate rather than `on_gcd` |
| the same, as Vanish then Ambush | `sequenceIsAffordable` checks Ambush BEFORE the Vanish is cast, so the window it needs is not up and the whole sequence is refused. **The feature cannot hold "step one ENABLES step two"** |

**AND A FLAG ON VANISH ALONE WOULD HAVE BEEN A HOLE.** Premeditation is also off
the global cooldown and sits ABOVE Ambush in both lists -- so Vanish would chain
into Premeditation and then wait out a poll anyway, which is the owner's own
three-step cycle broken at the second step. The opt-out list would have had to
grow to cover every off-GCD entry above an Ambush, and would reopen the moment
anybody added one.

### What it is

One line in `nextDecisionTime`: a cast that SUCCEEDED and left the actor free
returns `now` rather than `now + ROTATION_POLL_MS`.

**TERMINATION IS THE WHOLE OF THE SAFETY ARGUMENT**, because a decision
scheduled at its own timestamp can recur forever:

| | |
| --- | --- |
| only after a cast that SUCCEEDED | an IDLE actor is the unbounded case, and it still polls |
| every off-GCD ability declares a `cooldownMs` | each wins at most once per instant, so the chain is bounded by the list's length |

**THE SECOND WAS NOBODY'S RULE AND IS NOW A TEST.** `freeCastChain.test.ts`
asserts it across all nine classes, the four racials and the consumables,
because an ability added later with no cooldown and no global cooldown would
**hang** a fight rather than fail one -- the worst failure available here.

**AN ON-NEXT-SWING ABILITY IS EXCLUDED OUTRIGHT**, and that is not merely
defensive. It is off the global cooldown by DERIVATION and is the one family
with no cooldown: Heroic Strike is refused while already armed, but **Heroic
Strike and Cleave share a slot** and would overwrite each other's queue forever,
draining a warrior's whole rage bar in one instant.

### What it moved

| | before | after | |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **LW Melee** | 806.9 | **821.8** | **+14.9 REAL** -- the only one |
| **Hemo Dagger** | 775.9 | **790.1** | +14.2 |
| **Frostfire** | 944.0 | **954.0** | +9.9 |
| **Rupture** | 700.8 | **709.5** | +8.7 |
| Shockadin 2H | 817.6 | 814.4 | -3.2, the largest loss |

**ALL 28 ROWS MOVED, mean 795.6 -> 798.9, TWENTY UP AND EIGHT DOWN**, every loss
inside its own interval. That shape is what says it is right rather than merely
different: removing a wasted idle can only help a build that had something to do
in it, and the one `REAL` verdict is **LW Melee**, which CLAUDE.md already
describes as global-cooldown-bound rather than resource-bound. A recovered 100ms
belongs to exactly that build.

**AND THE OWNER'S TWO PROFILES MORE THAN RECOVER WHAT 19b COST THEM**: across
both rounds Rupture is 707.4 -> 709.5 and Hemo Dagger 784.9 -> 790.1, so the
stealth rule is now free on the builds it was taken from.

**EVERY VANISH WINDOW IS SPENT ON AN AMBUSH**, asserted over three seeds on both
lists -- which is the ruling itself rather than a figure, and the thing to check
if either profile moves again.

### Four tests broke, and not one of them was about this

**THE LESSON IS WORTH MORE THAN THE RULE**, because all four were fixtures
pinning a timing they never meant to:

| | what it was really asserting |
| --- | --- |
| `costRefund` x2 | a built character CARRIES ITS ROTATION, so one `advanceTo(1)` tick now cast a chain ending on the global cooldown. The hand-driven Hamstring was refused for `on_gcd`, cost nothing, and **a refund test read a cost of zero as "the full cost"** -- the one reading that fixture is least able to notice, because zero is what a perfect refund looks like |
| `mageAbilities` | it `find`-ed "any row whose attempts equal its casts" as its comparison spell, so WHICH spell decided the result was whatever the fight produced first. It names **Scorch** now, which the sibling test directly beside it already did |
| `paladinTalents` | "nothing is left banked at the end of a fight that swung" is a claim about WHERE THE FIGHT STOPPED. It asks the event stream for applied-against-removed pairs now, allowing the one window still in flight |

**AND THE FIRST REPAIR OF THE MAGE TEST WAS WRONG IN AN INSTRUCTIVE WAY.** It
anchored on the owner's 1% spell miss FLOOR -- no spell can report a rate below
it -- which is true of the CHANCE and false of a measured frequency: Frostfire
Bolt reads **0.8%** across sixty fights. **A floor bounds the roll, not the
sample.** Reaching for a ruleset constant felt like the stable choice and was
still a wrong one.

## Round NINETEEN b: stealth breaks, and the answer was wider than the question

**ONE SENTENCE FROM THE OWNER, ARRIVING STRAIGHT AFTER ROUND 19 MERGED**, which
is the 18b shape again: "Almost every completed cast or attack breaks
stealth/prowl, and for the purposes of this simulation the only thing that
doesn't is a rogue's premeditation."

**IT ANSWERS A QUESTION THAT WAS ASKED ABOUT ONE ABILITY.** Round 19 declared
Ravage and flagged one clause as a reading rather than a fact -- "Ravage spends
Prowl, following Ambush. Neither your message nor the capture says so, and
without it the Cat could Ravage repeatedly for 60 energy. One line to flip if
that's wrong." The answer is not about Ravage at all, and not a flip: **it is
everything's rule**, so the line comes out and the engine owns it.

**THE FLAG IS WHAT GOT IT RULED ON**, which is this project's recurring finding
about a labelled interpretation -- and the caveat came off the same day, because
a caveat outlives the doubt it describes.

### What it is

| | |
| --- | --- |
| `AuraDefinition.brokenByAction` | the two STATES declare it: `stealth` and `prowl` |
| `Ability.breaksActionAuras` | the opt-out. **Exactly one `false` in the project** |
| `AuraCollection.breakOnAction` | one implementation, called from the two places an action happens |

**DERIVED, NOT DECLARED**, which is `refundsCostOnMiss`' shape and is what the
word "almost" asks for: a new ability gets the rule for free, and the one that
forgot would be the exception rather than silently wrong.

**THE TWO CALL SITES ARE `runCast` AND `swing`.** In `runCast` rather than
`castAbility`, so a cast that is started and interrupted does not count --
"completed" is the owner's own word -- and so a channel's first tick does. In
`swing` before the on-next-swing early return, so a queued Heroic Strike counts
as the attack it is, and **before the hit roll, so a MISS still breaks it**: the
attack happened, and whether it connected is the combat table's business.

### Premeditation, and why the exception is what makes the opener possible

**NOT DERIVABLE FROM ANYTHING ELSE ABOUT IT.** Premeditation is free, off the
global cooldown and deals no damage, and none of those is the reason: Vanish is
free and off the GCD too and APPLIES the window, while a Thistle Tea is free and
breaks it. The ruleset simply says this one does not.

**AND EVERY STEALTH OPENER THE OWNER SPECIFIED IN ROUND 19 IS STEALTH, THEN
PREMEDITATION, THEN AMBUSH.** So if this broke the window, the two combo points
it banks would cost the Ambush they were banked FOR -- the sequence would be
impossible rather than merely worse. The exception is load-bearing on three
profiles.

### Cutthroat deliberately does NOT carry it

It gates the same Ambush, and tagging it would have been the obvious tidy-up.
The owner ruled that its proc IS Ambush's stealth requirement, so it is a
ten-second **proc window spent by the Ambush** rather than a state an action
ends -- and a Rogue reaches 60 energy in only **30 of 207 windows** as it is, so
any swing landing inside one destroying it would quietly delete most of three
talents' worth of wiring.

**AMBUSH'S `if`/`else` IS GONE WITH IT.** It read "Cutthroat first; see the note
above on why the order is a decision" -- one window or the other, never both.
Now one is spent by the ability and the other ended by the engine, so with both
up both go, which is what the rule says and what the `else` was quietly
preventing.

### TWO WAYS TO HALF-WIRE IT WERE WRITTEN, AND THE FULL SUITE PASSED ON BOTH

**3,709 tests green on a rule that was inert for most characters and
self-defeating for the rest.** This is the round's real finding.

| | what it was | what it would have cost |
| --- | --- | --- |
| below `runCast`'s early return | a character with no cast reaction leaves that function by another path | the rule applying to Rogues and Druids **with a talent** and to nobody else -- the `resourceRegenMultiplier` failure exactly |
| after `onCast` instead of before | **Vanish and Prowl do nothing but apply one** | it removed the window each had just opened: the whole of both abilities |

**THE SECOND IS NOT FIXED WITH MORE OPT-OUTS**, which is the tempting repair and
would have grown the exception list from one to three to cover a mistake in an
ordering. **The rule is about what was up BEFORE the action**: breaking first
says that exactly, and whatever the action establishes is newer than the action.
Neither Ambush nor Ravage reads the aura in `onCast` -- the gate is `canCast`,
which `checkCast` has already passed by then.

**AND THE TESTS THAT FOUND NEITHER WERE THE WHOLE SUITE.**
`tests/engine/stealthBreaking.test.ts` is what asks whether the rule FIRES: a
cast that is not the one spending the window, a SWING, a character with no cast
reactions, and an aura the action itself opened. Its own first draft failed
twice on the fixture rather than the rule -- a bare combatant defaults to
`autoAttack: 'none'`, so nothing swung at all, and `WeaponProfile` has no
`speed` field, so the swing that did land scheduled its successor at NaN. **A
fixture that cannot take the action cannot test what the action does.**

### What it cost: the Vanish-Ambush pair, by about one Ambush a fight

| profile | before | after | Ambush uses |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Rupture** | 707.4 +/- 6.3 | **700.8 +/- 5.4** (-6.6) | **4.2 -> 3.4** |
| **Hemo Dagger** | 784.9 +/- 7.7 | **775.9 +/- 9.1** (-9.0) | **2.7 -> 2.2** |
| Pure Hemo | 712.5 | **712.5** | no Ambush; **bit-identical** |
| Cat | 894.1 | **894.1** | Ravage 1.0 -> 1.0; **bit-identical** |

**THE HARNESS CALLS BOTH `noise` AND THE USES COLUMN IS WHAT SAYS THEY ARE
NOT.** The mechanism is exact and named: Vanish is off the global cooldown, so
the actor is left FREE and `nextDecisionTime` returns `now + ROTATION_POLL_MS`
-- **the documented Charge problem on a different ability** -- and in that 100ms
a swing can take the window the Vanish just opened.

**THE LIST RECOVERS MOST OF IT**, which is why the DPS moves so much less than
the uses do: 0.8 of 4.2 Ambushes is 3.3% of Rupture's damage, about 23 DPS, and
the energy goes to Hemorrhage instead. **A DPS figure alone could not have said
which half moved.**

**THE TWO BIT-IDENTICAL ROWS ARE THE CONTAINMENT CHECK**, 894.1 and 712.5 to the
decimal: Ravage ends Prowl by a new route to the same outcome, and a Pure Hemo
Rogue's opening window was never spent by anything. Breaking an aura emits a
telemetry line and consumes no random number, so an unchanged decision sequence
is an unchanged fight.

**AND WHETHER THE 100ms SHOULD BITE IS THE OWNER'S**, now in the questions table
above: the poll is a property of this simulation rather than of the ruleset, and
a player pressing Vanish then Ambush does not take a swing in between.

### What it closed in the scope table: nothing, and that is the answer

CLAUDE.md's own rule is that **when a ruling widens what is modelled, the
`scope` tags are the first place to look** -- it cost three Rogue talents a
whole release once. All six `stealth` entries were re-read and **all six
survive**, for three different reasons: **detection** is three of them,
**a stealth opener this project does not declare** is two (Garrote and Cheap
Shot, named beside clauses that DO apply), and **Camouflage** is the odd one --
it reduces "your speed penalty from your Stealth ability and its cooldown", and
there is no Stealth ABILITY here at all, so there is no cooldown to reduce and
the penalty is positioning.

**SIX NOs FOR ONE READ**, which is the cheap half of that rule.

### Three notes that the ruling made false, and were corrected with it

| where | what it said |
| --- | --- |
| `auras/rogue.ts` | "there is no detection, no **break-on-damage**, no movement" -- true on the day, and the ruling is precisely a break |
| the same note | plain Stealth "lasts until something breaks it, and **nothing here models anything that would**" |
| `abilities/rogue.ts` | the stealth bullet's "there is STILL no stealth system" needed its third widening recorded |

**THE DURATION ARGUMENT GOT STRONGER RATHER THAN WEAKER**, which is worth
saying because it reads the other way: `STEALTH_DURATION_MS` was borrowed from
Vanish's stated ten seconds for a window nothing spent, and the owner's rule
means the FIRST action takes it -- a cast or a swing, well inside ten seconds on
every build.

## Round TWENTY is scoped and not started: the client caught up

**THE PATCH HAS SHIPPED.** Re-running the importers a day after round seventeen
put the client at **1.60.1.70425**, where round seventeen captured 70291 -- and
**every tree change the notes described is in it**, plus eleven that are in no
note at all.

**THE REFRESH IS ON THE BRANCH NOW.** This paragraph read "it was run, read, and
reverted, because its blocker is six profile build URLs that only the owner can
respecify" -- and the blocker turned out not to need them. Seven preset
allocations had stopped being legal, and five of the seven were a point going
where the client put its replacement: a rename, a lost rank paying for a gained
one, two tier rows swapping places. **Only the Warlock's needed a judgement**, and
its own opener decided it -- the owner's "2.5s before start casting Shadow Bolt"
is a 3.0 second cast with Bane 5/5 on it, so Malediction's five deleted points
had a stated destination.

**SO "ONLY THE OWNER CAN RESPECIFY THIS" WAS TRUE OF THE 1.60.1.70170 PATCH AND
NOT OF THIS ONE**, and the difference is what the client did rather than how many
points moved: that patch DELETED talents every Paladin build spent points on with
nothing in their place, and this one mostly renamed and reshuffled. **A blocker
inherited from the last patch of the same kind is a dated claim like any other**
-- `tools/probe_allocations.ts` prints the `dropped` list per preset, which is
the cheap way to tell a rename from a deletion before concluding either.

### The three note-derived values are CONFIRMED, which is the headline

`PATCH_NOTE_TALENT_VALUES` carried three figures taken from notes the client had
not shipped, with the client's own row recorded beside each so a refresh would
expose a wrong one. 70425 ships all three **verbatim**:

| | |
| --- | --- |
| `warlock/shadow_mastery` | 2, 4, 6, 8, 10 |
| `paladin/champion_of_the_light` | 10, 20, 30 |
| `priest/mental_strength` | 2, 4, 6, 8, 10 |

**THE DETECTOR SAID SO BY FAILING**, which is what it is for: the importer run
failed `patchNoteValues.test.ts`'s "the client still says it" on
`shadow_mastery`, naming the talent and printing both rows.

**AND THE INSTRUCTION THIS PARAGRAPH GAVE ITSELF WAS FOLLOWED.** It read "delete
that whole file in the commit that lands the refresh -- the failing test is the
reminder", and the refresh commit deletes `patchNoteValues.ts`, its test and its
call site in `talentValue`. **A layer built to expire, which announced its own
redundancy and was removed on that announcement**, which is the first time
anything in this project has gone round that whole loop.

The shape is kept in CLAUDE.md with the two things to rebuild it with, because
the next note that runs ahead of the client will want it -- and the note there
says what matters more: **it must not be left standing empty**, since a table
with no rows reads exactly like a mechanism that is doing something.

So the risk the layer was written against -- the Dual Wield Specialization case,
where a note and a client tooltip disagreed and the owner ruled for the CLIENT --
did not materialise on any of the three.

### EVERY FIGURE ROUND SEVENTEEN DERIVED IS CONFIRMED BY 70425

**THIS IS THE PART WORTH THE MOST**, because round seventeen implemented
forty-odd notes against a client that carried none of them. 70425 carries them,
and it agrees with every figure that was derived rather than transcribed:

| | derived in 17 | the client now says |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Arcane Blast damage | `midpoint(364, 424) x 0.6` = **236.4** | 218-254, midpoint **236** |
| Arcane Blast cast | 1.5 sec | **1.5 sec cast** |
| Arcane Blast ramp | 15% of Intellect, 175% cost, 4 stacks, 15 sec | all four, verbatim |
| Chain Lightning mana | `round(485 x 0.65)` = **315** | **315 Mana** |
| Shadow Word: Death | 18 sec, over a note whose "(was 12)" matched nothing | **18 sec cooldown** |
| Devouring Plague | 24 sec | **24 sec cooldown** |
| Vampiric Embrace | 30 sec | **30 sec cooldown** |
| Stormstrike | 8% of base mana | **8% of base mana** |
| Shield Specialization | 4% mana, 3 sec | **4%, 3 sec** |
| Ice Lance frozen | multiplier 3 | "**200% increased damage** to Frozen targets" |
| Improved Mind Flay | compounding per instance | "each instance ... more damage than its **previous** instance" |
| Resourcefulness | melee and ranged ABILITY crits | "your melee and ranged **ability** Critical Strikes" |
| Leader of the Pack | melee and ranged crit only | "**melee and ranged** Critical Strike chance" |
| Moonkin Aura | SPELL crit only | "**spell** Critical Strike chance" |
| Reckoning | stores up to 4 charges | "**Stores up to 4** extra attacks" |

**THE TWO ARITHMETIC DERIVATIONS ARE THE ONES TO NOTICE.** Arcane Blast's
`-40%` was read as being the same change as its cast time, because
`2.5 / 3.5 x 0.6 = 1.5 / 3.5` exactly -- and the client's own midpoint is 236
against the 236.4 that produced. Chain Lightning's "approximately 35%" was
rounded to 315 on the grounds that a stated OPERATION on a known operand is
sourced even when the result is not quoted; the client says 315.

**AND THE ONE FLAGGED MISMATCH IS SETTLED IN FAVOUR OF THE PRESCRIPTION.** The
Shadow Word: Death note read "(was 12 seconds)" where both the capture and this
repository said 15, and 18 was applied anyway because "increased to 18" is a
prescription and the parenthetical is history. 70425 says 18.

The 5%-and-7%-spell-damage version did not ship. (The client tooltip does not carry the note's "consumed by next Cone of Cold" clause.) |
| **Fire Nova's two missing figures** | "significantly reduced" is now **375 mana** (was 520) and **232-258 damage** (was 413-459, so a midpoint of 245 against 436). Round seventeen correctly applied only the coefficient and recorded that this left the ability pointing the wrong way; both numbers now exist |
| **Arcane Wrath's real damage** | the TALENT tooltip says 122-146 and the SPELL at max rank says **406-496**, which is the rank-1 trap this project has been caught by twice. 395 mana, 3.5 sec cast, 6 sec cooldown, and "+5% of maximum Mana as Spell Damage" |

### And two CORRECTIONS 70425 forces on round seventeen

**RECKONING'S INTERNAL COOLDOWN IS BLOCKS ONLY, AND THIS ONE IS DONE.** The
client reads "Cannot occur from **Blocks** more than once every 1.5 sec", where

second internal cooldown on how often it can be triggered" -- and applied it to
the crit-taken path as well. The crit path is 100% at 5/5 on the one build that
takes it, so it was a real difference in how fast charges bank. **It landed in
round seventeen's last commit**, one `canTrigger` clause, and it needed no tree
data: four tests in `paladinTalents.test.ts` moved with it and one was

across both halves of the talent.

**MOONKIN FORM'S TALENT AND SPELL TOOLTIPS DISAGREE WITH EACH OTHER AT THE SAME

**THE EXCLUSIVITY ROUND SEVENTEEN LEFT ALONE IS CORROBORATED BY THAT SAME LINE.**
It was left as a QUESTION on the reasoning that removing it is a raid
composition decision; the client still calls the two exclusive, so the

### And two hand-filled values moved, neither of them lost

The importer reported mage 4 -> 3 and shaman 3 -> 2 hand-filled kept, which is
the shape of CLAUDE.md's rename warning -- "a renamed talent is the one case
where the importer's MERGE does not protect a hand-filled value". **Both are
benign and were checked rather than assumed**: `call_of_thunder` was REMOVED, so
its hand-fill correctly went with it, and `missile_barrage` is **2 ranks now**,
so the importer derives its values from the per-rank text and no hand-fill is
needed. Nothing has to be restored.

### A false positive worth recording, because it nearly went in the report

The first pass of the broken-allocation check reported `deflection` as over its
cap -- 5 points against a cap of 3. It is **not**: the Rogue's Deflection has
always been 3 ranks and the Warrior's 5, and the check keyed its rank table by
talent id alone. **Talent ids are unique only WITHIN a class**, which is the
collision `talentsForClass` exists for and which this file already records about
`TALENT_AURAS`. Caught by checking the one surprising row rather than by
re-reading the script.

## Round seventeen: a patch, and a client two builds behind it

**THE FIRST THING THIS ROUND DID WAS NOT READ THE NOTES.** CLAUDE.md's rule is
that a patch starts with the three importers and the decoder, and running them
first is the only reason the rest of this is honest:

| | |
| --- | --- |
| client was | 1.60.1.70170 |
| client is | **1.60.1.70291** |
| of the forty-odd notes, in either data source | **none** |

So the notes describe a build that has not shipped. Every figure in this round
comes from a NOTE, which is this project's existing reading -- `SWIPE_AP_COEFFICIENT`
is 3% because the notes said so while `WoWSimWorksheet.xlsx` says 10%. The risk
that comes with it is the Dual Wield Specialization case pointing the other way,
where a note and a client tooltip disagreed and the owner ruled for the client.
**Every note-derived talent value is therefore in ONE table that records what
the client says beside it**, so a refresh makes each entry either redundant or
demonstrably wrong rather than quietly authoritative.

### What the importers found, which the notes do not mention

| | |
| --- | --- |
| **Druid** | Predatory Instincts **renamed to Natural Instinct**, with a second clause and a second number. Its row went from `[10, 20]` to `[[10, 12], [20, 25]]` |
| **Warrior** | Impale now **requires Deep Wounds 3/3** |
| **Paladin** | Reckoning's row gained the 1.5s internal cooldown this project already had |
| **Priest** | Penance 81 -> 44 damage; **Hunter** Mongoose Bite window 5s -> 10s |

**THE RENAME IS THE DANGEROUS ONE AND IT IS CORRECT BY LUCK.** A talent id is
slugified from the client's name, so a renamed talent IS a new key -- and the
values row went from one number to two. `critDamageBonus` takes no `valueIndex`
and `talentNumber` defaults to index 0, so it still reads the crit damage figure
**because the client happened to put the surviving clause first**. That is
Aftermath's shape exactly, and the next clause reorder is what makes it wrong.

All 19 hand-filled single-rank values survived the merge, which is the check a
rename makes necessary: Heating Up lost its value that way last patch.

### The four commits

| | what it was |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | the client refresh, and **nine racials** |
| 2 | twelve class notes that carry their own numbers, and a new **value-override layer** |
| 3 | the Hunter's four, and **Reckoning's charges** |
| 4 | **Heating Up's two clauses**, two Judgements, and **Arcane Blast's redesign** |

### The finding the notes do not mention: five profiles fell off their hit cap

**TAUREN ENDURANCE'S 1% HIT BECAME 2% CRIT DAMAGE, AND THE OWNER'S GEAR
TEMPLATES WERE CREDITING THAT 1%.** The two Tauren melee rows ask for 70 hit
rating where every other melee row asks for 80; the two feral Druids ask for 30
against a 4-point Nature's Reach; the Moonkin asks for 110 against the same 4.
Every one landed exactly on its cap WITH the racial:

| | | |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Prot Warr, Enh Shaman | 7 of 8 melee | one point short |
| Cat, Bear | 7 of 8 melee | one point short |
| **Moonkin** | **15 of 16 spell** | its spells now miss **2%** where they sat on the owner's **1% FLOOR** |

Melee and ranged have NO miss floor, so the eighth point was the last one that
bought anything. The Moonkin is the sharpest case because the floor made its
last point unusually valuable -- this DOUBLES its spell miss rather than adding
a point to it.

**NOT FIXED, BECAUSE THE TEMPLATES ARE THE OWNER'S SPREADSHEET.** Raising five
rows by 10 rating -- which under their own donor rule comes out of CRIT -- is a
change to their numbers on nobody's authority. It is pinned as a test in
`statHeadroom.test.ts` instead, and fails the moment a template moves.

**AND IT IS THE ONE PLACE A RACIAL CHANGE REACHES A FIGURE THROUGH THE GEAR**,
which is why no audit here could have found it: there is no zero anywhere, no
caveat, and nothing for the census to count.

### Two interpretations retired, in opposite ways

**ARCANE BLAST'S REDESIGN ANSWERED A FLAGGED QUESTION BY DELETING IT.** "Lasts
8 sec or until any other damage spell is cast" read literally said the bonus was
destroyed by the only thing that could collect it, so the clause would have paid
nothing -- and the reading taken (the other spell BENEFITS, then the stacks go)
was this project's most load-bearing interpretation. HANDOVER carried it as a
question. The redesign makes the window a stated fifteen seconds, so
`arcaneBlastSpender` is deleted and nothing has to guess. **A flagged
interpretation usually earns a ruling; this one earned a rewrite.**

**AND ICE LANCE'S WENT THE OTHER WAY: A SETTLED FACT BECAME A QUESTION AGAIN.**
While the frozen multiplier and the divisor in the owner's `1.5 / 3.5 / 4` were
both four they multiplied out to exactly `1.5 / 3.5`, and asked whether the `/4`
had already done the frozen division the owner said **"This is intended stop
asking."** The note takes the multiplier to three; the product is 0.3214 and the
symmetry is gone. The separate-facts reading is implemented and the alternative
is pinned as a NEGATIVE assertion, so the day somebody restores the symmetry
they have to argue with a line rather than with a coincidence.

### The arithmetic that settled two numbers nobody stated

**ARCANE BLAST'S "-40% COEFFICIENT" *IS* ITS CAST TIME CHANGE.** Coefficients
here follow `castTime / 3.5`, the old 0.714 is `2.5 / 3.5` rounded, and

    2.5 / 3.5 x 0.6  =  1.5 / 3.5     exactly

so the note's two clauses are one change written twice. Two independent
sentences landing on the same figure is the strongest evidence available.

**AND "THE CORRECT COEFFICIENT FOR INSTANT CAST SPELLS" IS THE SAME NUMBER.**
The note names a convention without stating one, and this ruleset states it nine
times -- Holy Shock, Hammer of Wrath, Mind Blast, Shadow Word: Death, Fire
Blast, Scorch, Conflagrate, Shadowburn, Searing Pain all carry `1.5 / 3.5`. The
two rows the note names, Judgement of Righteousness at 0.5 and Fury at 0.45,
were **the two outliers**. A convention read off nine abilities plus a note that
independently names the two breaking it is a derivation rather than a guess.

### Two engine fields, both because the obvious implementation was near-zero

**`DamageRequest.attackPowerCoefficient`**, for the Hunter's traps. "Scale with
5% of your Attack Power" cannot be `powerCoefficient`: that field picks its pool
BY SCHOOL and a trap is FIRE, so 0.05 there reads the Hunter's SPELL power --
close to nothing. The traps would have gained almost exactly zero, kept an
ordinary damage row, and looked implemented.

**`CastModifier.exceptWhileAura`**, for Heating Up. `requiresCastTime` reads the
ability's DECLARED cast time and Pyroblast always declares one, so the aura
matched a cast Presence of Mind had already made instant and all three stacks
were thrown away -- with nothing on the results page to show it.

### What is BLOCKED, and not approximated

**THIRTEEN NOTES NEED A TREE SHAPE THE CLIENT HAS NOT SHIPPED.** A rank count, a
row and a prerequisite are read by the decoder, the legality rules and the Talent
panel, so inventing one is inventing game data with a build URL that will not
decode:

| | |
| --- | --- |
| **Mage** | Missile Barrage to row 1 as a 2-point talent; **Arcane Wrath** (new); Critical Mass to 5 points; Fire Power to 3 and redesigned; Winter's Chill redesigned -- **the notes give TWO different versions of it**; **Frost Blast** (new spell, "low frost damage" and no figure) |
| **Priest** | **Dark Inspiration** (new); Mental Strength's row move -- its VALUE is applied |
| **Shaman** | **Elemental Mastery** (new 31-point); Lava Burst's move and new rank 4 |

**AND THREE STATE NO FIGURE AT ALL**: Fire Nova's base damage and mana cost
"significantly reduced", and Chain Lightning's "approximately" 35%.

**CHAIN LIGHTNING IS THE LINE BETWEEN THE TWO CATEGORIES.** "Reduced by
approximately 35%" states an OPERATION and an operand, and this project has the
cost from the client -- so 315 is the rounding of a stated percentage rather than
an invented number. "Significantly reduced" states no operation, so Fire Nova's
other two halves are left alone.

### Four notes needed nothing, and that is written down

**"A note we did nothing about" and "a note nobody read" look identical from a
diff**, so each is recorded where somebody would look for it:

| | |
| --- | --- |
